Principaloid bundles
Thomas Strobl, Rafał R. Suszek

Abstract
. We present a novel generalisation of principal bundles—principaloid bundles: These are fibre bundles π : 𝒫 → B \pi\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\to B where the typical fibre is the arrow manifold 𝒢 \mathscr{G} of a Lie groupoid 𝒢 M \hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt and the structure group is reduced to the latter’s group of bisections. Each such bundle canonically comes with a bundle map 𝒟 : 𝒫 → ℱ \mathscr{D}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{F} to another fibre bundle ℱ \mathscr{F} over the base B B , with typical fibre M M . Examples of principaloid bundles include ordinary principal G {\rm G} -bundles, obtained for 𝒢 := G ∙ \mathscr{G}:=\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt , bundles associated to them, obtained for action groupoids 𝒢 := G ⋉ M \mathscr{G}:={\rm G}\ltimes M , and general fibre bundles if 𝒢 \mathscr{G} is a pair groupoid. While π \pi is far from being a principal 𝒢 \mathscr{G} -bundle, we prove that 𝒟 \mathscr{D} is one. Connections on the principaloid bundle π \pi are thus required to be 𝒢 \mathscr{G} -invariant Ehresmann connections. In the three examples mentioned above, this reproduces the usual types of connection for each of them. In a local description over a trivialising cover { O i } \{O_{i}\} of B B , the connection gives rise to Lie algebroid-valued objects living over bundle trivialisations { O i × M } \{O_{i}\times M\} of ℱ \mathscr{F} . Their behaviour under bundle automorphisms, including gauge transformations, is studied in detail. Finally, we construct the Atiyah–Ehresmann groupoid At ⁡ ( 𝒫 ) ℱ \,\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.84724pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.84724pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{At}(\mathscr{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt which governs symmetries of 𝒫 \mathscr{P} , this time mapping distinct 𝒟 \mathscr{D} -fibres to one another in general. It is a fibre-bundle object in the category of Lie groupoids, with typical fibre 𝒢 M \hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt and base B × B B \hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.19788pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.19788pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B\times B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.42424pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.42424pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.42424pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt . We show that those of its bisections which project to bisections of its base are in a one-to-one correspondence with automorphisms of π \pi .



1.
Introduction

Principal bundles
{\mathsf{P}}
and their associated bundles
as well as connections on them are of great importance in differential geometry and are also omnipresent in large parts of theoretical physics. In this paper, we propose and study a new generalisation of this framework where the structure Lie group of a principal bundle is replaced by a Lie groupoid in a particular way.

There are several equivalent ways of defining principal bundles. One is by saying that
{\mathsf{P}}
is a fibre bundle over
\Sigma
with typical fibre
{\rm G}
, where
{\rm G}
is a (finite-dimensional) Lie group, for which the structure group
{\rm Diff}({\rm G})
is reduced to
L({\rm G})
, the group of left translations of
{\rm G}
. In other words, the fibre bundle
\pi_{\mathsf{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathsf{P}}\to\Sigma
is equipped with an equivalence class of atlases with the property that all transition maps within one such atlas, when restricted to any point in the overlap of trivialisation charts in the base, take values in
L({\rm G})\subset{\rm Diff}({\rm G})
.

In this description, it is evident that
{\mathsf{P}}
carries a fibrewise right
{\rm G}
-action. Since the Lie subgroup of
{\rm Diff}({\rm G})
consisting of diffeomorphisms of
{\rm G}
which commute with
L({\rm G})
is given by
R({\rm G})
, the group of right translations of
{\rm G}
, there is the following elegant alternative: A principal
{\rm G}
-bundle
{\mathsf{P}}
is given simply by a free and proper right
{\rm G}
-action on a manifold
{\mathsf{P}}
. All the data above can be recovered from here.

This opens different ways for a generalisation to Lie groupoids, from which we here choose the first one as our starting point.
1
1

1

It deserves to be mentioned that the concept of a principaloid bundle, developed here in full detail, first arose in discussions of the first author with Oleksii Kotov in 2005.

To set the notation, throughout this paper, a Lie groupoid is denoted by
\mathscr{G}
or
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
, where
\mathscr{G}
is the arrow manifold and
M
the object manifold. Furthermore,
{\mathbb{B}}\equiv{\rm Bisec}(\mathscr{G})
denotes the (generally infinite-dimensional) group of bisections of
\mathscr{G}
. Its elements
\beta\in{\mathbb{B}}
are sections of the source map
s
of
\mathscr{G}
,
i.e.
, maps
\beta\hskip-2.0pt:M\to\mathscr{G}
such that
s\circ\beta={\rm id}_{M}
, with the additional property:
t\circ\beta\in{\rm Diff}(M)
, written in terms of the target map
t
of
\mathscr{G}
.
{\mathbb{B}}
can act on
\mathscr{G}
from the left or from the right, essentially by left and right translations.
2
2

2

Whenever it cannot be avoided, maps with an infinite-dimensional manifold as domain or codomain—as for the
{\mathbb{B}}
-actions above in the case where
M
is not a finite set—we assume the mapping to be smooth in the sense of Kriegl and Michor
[
KM97
]
.
We denote the corresponding two subgroups of
{\rm Diff}(\mathscr{G})
by
L({\mathbb{B}})
and
R({\mathbb{B}})
. For example, given arbitrary
\beta\in{\mathbb{B}}
and
g\in\mathscr{G}
, the diffeomorphism
L_{\beta}\in L({\mathbb{B}})
maps
g
as follows:
L_{\beta}(g)=\beta(t(g)).g
, where the dot denotes the multiplication in
\mathscr{G}
. (For further details on this and other statements regarding Lie groupoids, their bisections, and related notions, see Appendices.)

Definition.
A
principaloid bundle
, or principaloid
\mathscr{G}
-bundle, is a fibre bundle
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\to\Sigma
with typical fibre
\mathscr{G}
, whose structure group is reduced to
L({\mathbb{B}})
(or a subgroup thereof).

The commonly adopted generalisation of principal bundles, on the other hand,
leads to what is called
principal groupoid bundles
, see,
e.g.
,
[
MacK87
,
Mo91
]
, or principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundles if the groupoid is specified. This notion is significantly different from the one considered here: In general, principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundles are not even fibre bundles. However, they also play an important role in the setting of principaloid bundles, as secondary or induced structures, for which reason we first recall this notion.

The definition of a principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle is based on a particular choice of a
\mathscr{G}
-action on the total space of the bundle. This is already subtler than in the case of Lie groups. Indeed, for a general Lie groupoid
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
, multiplication is defined only for composable arrows,
i.e.
,
g_{1}.g_{2}
is defined only for elements
g_{1},g_{2}\in\mathscr{G}
such that
t(g_{2})=s(g_{1})
. Consequently, for an action of a groupoid on a manifold
{\rm\breve{P}}
, one needs an additional map
\mu\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm\breve{P}}\to M
, called the moment map. An element
g\in\mathscr{G}
then maps
\mu^{-1}(\{t(g)\})
to
\mu^{-1}(\{s(g)\})
. Requiring this (right) action along
M
to be—in an appropriate sense—proper and free turns
{\rm\breve{P}}
into a principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle, with a smooth base
\Sigma
which is canonically identifiable with
{\rm\breve{P}}/\mathscr{G}
. The setting is summarised in the following diagram:

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 98.43326pt\raise-50.40134pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

By the principality of the
action, one finds that the fibre of
\pi_{\rm\breve{P}}
at a given point
\sigma\in\Sigma
is diffeomorphic to the
t
-fibre
t^{-1}(\{\mu(\pi_{\rm\breve{P}}^{-1}(\{\sigma\}))\})
in
\mathscr{G}
. Since
t\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{G}\to M
is only required to be a surjective submersion,
{\rm\breve{P}}
does not have a typical fibre in general.

The groupoid
\mathscr{G}
comes with two surjective submersions:
s
(the source map) and
t
(the target map) to its object manifold
M
, and so it should not come as a surprise that every principaloid bundle is canonically endowed with two important maps: One modelled on
s
as the moment map
\mu\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\to M
, and one modelled on
t
as the base projection of a surjective submersion
\mathscr{D}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{F}
, a bundle map to another fibre bundle
\mathscr{F}
over
\Sigma
, with typical fibre
M
, which is canonically induced by
\mathscr{P}
. We shall show in the present paper that these data combine in the following manner:

Theorem A.

Every principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}\to\Sigma
is a principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle over
\mathscr{F}
in a canonical way,

(2)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.02084pt\raise-25.14511pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-43.01247pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower-3.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 50.42499pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 44.80692pt\raise-25.14511pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{D}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.82777pt\raise-42.34578pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower-3.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 84.6158pt\raise-19.95761pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 105.43964pt\raise-43.01247pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 110.65555pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 150.50412pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.7544pt\raise-0.65642pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.7544pt\raise 0.65642pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 119.98805pt\raise-43.01247pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.7544pt\raise-0.65642pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.7544pt\raise 0.65642pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}{\hbox{\kern-8.39583pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.39583pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.84857pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 36.84857pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}\equiv\mathscr{P}/\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.325pt\raise-75.43535pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathscr{F}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.82777pt\raise-93.3027pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower-3.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 105.25972pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 154.42773pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-100.58047pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 50.21666pt\raise-100.58047pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 110.65555pt\raise-100.58047pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

The
\mathscr{G}
-action preserves the
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}
-fibres, where
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}=\pi_{\mathscr{F}}\circ\mathscr{D}
.

The present setting is not only natural, but also subsumes other known constructions as special cases: On the one hand, for a Lie groupoid over a point,
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
, we recover a principal
{\rm G}
-bundle
{\mathsf{P}}\to\Sigma
. Note that here the group of bisections
{\mathbb{B}}\cong{\rm G}
so that the structure group becomes the usual one,
{\rm G}
, and
\mathscr{P}\cong{\rm P}
. Furthermore, the
\mathscr{G}
-action in (
2
) reduces to the standard right
{\rm G}
-action on
{\rm P}
.

On the other hand, if we choose
\mathscr{G}=M\times M
, the pair groupoid of a connected manifold
M
, then
{\mathbb{B}}\cong\mathrm{Diff}(M)
and
\mathscr{P}\cong\mathscr{F}\times M
, where
\mathscr{F}
is a general fibre bundle over
\Sigma
with typical fibre
M
, and an unrestricted structure group
{\rm Diff}(M)
. Note that here the right
\mathscr{G}
-action of (
2
) corresponds to diffeomorphisms of the second factor in
\mathscr{F}\times M
, leaving
\mathscr{F}
untouched.

Finally, let the structure Lie groupoid be an action Lie groupoid,
\mathscr{G}:={\rm G}\ltimes M
, defined for an arbitrary
{\rm G}
-action
\lambda\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm G}\times M\to M
on a manifold
M
. Consider a principaloid
\mathscr{G}
-bundle in which the structure group
{\mathbb{B}}
is reduced to
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}=\{\ (g,\cdot)\;|\;g\in{\rm G}\ \}\cong{\rm G}
. Then,
\mathscr{P}\cong{\mathsf{P}}\times M
, where
{\mathsf{P}}
is an ordinary principal
{\rm G}
-bundle over
\Sigma
. Moreover, the bundle
\mathscr{F}
becomes none other than the bundle associated to
{\mathsf{P}}
by the action
\lambda
. In fact, the map
\mathscr{D}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm P}\times M\cong\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{F}
turns out to coincide with the corresponding quotient map in the usual construction of the associated bundle.

Thus, principaloid bundles unify principal
{\rm G}
-bundles, associated bundles, as well as general fibre bundles under a common roof—while simultaneously offering a rich class of additional examples that invite further exploration.

There are also other ways of characterising a principaloid bundle worth mentioning here. We first observe that a right-
\mathscr{G}
-equivariant diffeomorphism of
\mathscr{G}
, for the right action given by right translations, always comes—as shall be shown—in the form of a left-multiplication by a bisection,
{\rm Diff}_{r(\mathscr{G})}(\mathscr{G})=L({\mathbb{B}})
. Furthermore, every
\mathscr{G}
-action on a manifold
N
gives rise to a
{\mathbb{B}}
-action on
N
. While the former action is, for a given element, defined only on submanifolds of
N
, mapping
\mu
-fibres to
\mu
-fibres, the latter one is an honest group action, defined over all of
N
. We then have the following results:

Theorem B.
There are the following
alternative
ways of characterising a principaloid bundle (in the second case with the additional assumption that every
g\in\mathscr{G}
admits a
\beta\in{\mathbb{B}}
such that
\beta(s(g))=g
):

•

A fibre bundle in the category of right
\mathscr{G}
-modules with typical fibre given by the canonical right
\mathscr{G}
-module
\mathscr{G}
and with the trivial
\mathscr{G}
-action on the base.

•

A fibre bundle in the category of
{\mathbb{B}}
-spaces with typical fibre given by the canonical right
{\mathbb{B}}
-space
\mathscr{G}
, with the trivial
{\mathbb{B}}
-action on the base, together with an equivalence class of
{\mathbb{B}}
-equivariant atlases.

Such definitions are closer to the definition of an ordinary principal
{\rm G}
-bundle which uses a principal action of the structure group
{\rm G}
. Note that due to the identity
{\mathbb{B}}\cong{\rm G}
, which holds true for the groupoid
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
, there arise two generalisations of such a
{\rm G}
-action for general groupoids: by a (partially defined)
\mathscr{G}
-action, or by a (globally defined)
{\mathbb{B}}
-action.

Connections are central objects in the study of fibre bundles. We define a connection
\Theta
on a principaloid
\mathscr{G}
-bundle as a
\mathscr{G}
-invariant Ehresmann connection
\Theta
on
\mathscr{P}\to\Sigma
. Since
\mathscr{G}
acts on
\mathscr{P}
by (diffeomorphically) mapping
\mu
-fibres to
\mu
-fibres, see Diagram (
2
), the above condition on
\Theta
is meaningful only if the horizontal subbundle
{\rm H}\mathscr{P}\subset{\rm T}\mathscr{P}
lies inside
{\rm ker}{\rm T}\mu
, which we therefore implicitly assume when demanding that
\Theta
be
\mathscr{G}
-invariant. The
\mathscr{G}
-invariance further implies that
\Theta
induces an Ehresmann connection
\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}
on
\mathscr{F}\cong\mathscr{P}/\mathscr{G}
.

Since every principaloid
\mathscr{G}
-bundle
\mathscr{P}
is also a
{\mathbb{B}}
-space, a (compatible) connection on
\mathscr{P}
is also
{\mathbb{B}}
-invariant with respect to the globally defined right
{\mathbb{B}}
-action
\mathscr{R}
on
\mathscr{P}
. Under the assumption about
\mathscr{G}
mentioned in Theorem B—that every arrow
g
has a global bisection
\beta
passing through it—the
\mathscr{R}({\mathbb{B}})
-invariance of
\Theta
implies its
\varrho(\mathscr{G})
-invariance, where
\varrho
denotes the right
\mathscr{G}
-action of Diagram (
2
). (The fact that the horizontal distribution must lie in the kernel of
{\rm T}\mu
is a consequence of large stabiliser groups of the
{\mathbb{B}}
-action here.)

In the three special examples of principaloid bundles discussed above, such a connection is in a one-to-one correspondence with (i) for
\mathscr{G}={\rm G}
: an ordinary principal connection, (ii) for
\mathscr{G}=M\times M
: an unconstrained Ehresmann connection on the general fibre bundle
\mathscr{F}
, and (iii) for
\mathscr{G}={\rm G}\ltimes M
and structure group
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}
: an ordinary principal connection together with the connection on the associated bundle
\mathscr{F}
canonically induced by it.

For ordinary principal
{\rm G}
-bundles
{\mathsf{P}}
,
{\rm G}
-invariant Ehresmann connections can be described by Lie algebra-valued 1-forms on the total space of the bundle. This does not generalise to principaloid bundles as Lie algebroid-valued 1-forms. The reason is simple: An Ehresmann connection on a bundle
\mathscr{P}\to\Sigma
can be understood as a projector
\Theta\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm T}\mathscr{P}\to{\rm V}\mathscr{P}
onto the vertical subbundle
{\rm V}\mathscr{P}\subset{\rm T}\mathscr{P}
, modelled on
{\rm T}\mathscr{G}
. Now, for a group,
\mathscr{G}\cong{\rm G}
, the tangent bundle trivialises as
{\rm T}{\rm G}\cong{\rm G}\times\mathfrak{g}
, where
\mathfrak{g}=\mathrm{Lie}({\rm G})
is the Lie algebra of
{\rm G}
, which, as a vector space, we can identify with the tangent space of
G
at the identity. The Lie algebroid
E\to M
of a Lie groupoid
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
, on the other hand, contains only those tangent vectors at the identity bisection of
\mathscr{G}
which are tangent to the
s
-fibres,
E\cong{\rm Id}^{*}{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s
.

Nevertheless, we shall show that a connection 1-form
\Theta
locally gives rise to 1-forms valued in the tangent Lie algebroid of the structure groupoid
\mathscr{G}
, generalising the local Lie algebra-valued 1-forms
A\in\Omega^{1}(O,\mathfrak{g})
in a trivialisation of
{\mathsf{P}}
over
O\subset\Sigma
. The situation is, however, more intricate than in the case of ordinary principal bundles, essentially due to the appearance of the bundle
\mathscr{F}\to\Sigma
with typical fibre
M
of non-zero dimension in Diagram (
2
), whereas for principal
{\rm G}
-bundles
\mathscr{F}
reduces to
\Sigma
.

In a local trivialisation of
\mathscr{P}
over
O\subset\Sigma
, the connection 1-form
\Theta
on
\mathscr{P}
is in a one-to-one correspondence with a section
{\rm A}\in\Gamma({\rm pr}_{1}^{*}T^{*}O\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}E)
over the corresponding trivialisation
O\times M
of
\mathscr{F}
. Hence, at a point
(\sigma,m)\in O\times M
, one has, after natural idendtifications,

(3)

\displaystyle{\rm A}(\sigma,m)\;\in\;T_{\sigma}^{*}O\otimes E_{m}\,,

where, as above,
E\to M
denotes the Lie algebroid of
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
and
E_{m}
its fibre over
m\in M
.

The Lie algebroid-valued
{\rm T}O
-foliated 1-forms
A
are glued together according to an affine transformation law on overlaps of trivialisation domains, which generalises the familiar transformation law for local connection 1-forms on ordinary principal bundles in a natural way. Specific formulas are technically involved, so we refer the reader to Definition
3.12
and Theorem
3.15
in the main text below for further details.

Automorphisms of a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
are bundle automorphisms which commute with the right
\mathscr{G}
-action on
\mathscr{P}
. There is a groupoid behind these automorphisms, the Atiyah groupoid
\mathrm{At}(\mathscr{P})
. The object manifold of this groupoid is composed of the
\mathscr{D}
-fibres in
\mathscr{P}
and is thus parametrised by points in the bundle
\mathscr{F}
. In total, we get two commuting principal groupoid actions on
\mathscr{P}
, as summarised in the following diagram:

(4)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.84724pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 40.4507pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 71.05415pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.97777pt\raise-5.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.97777pt\raise-28.78609pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 111.63676pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 148.29576pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-15.84724pt\raise-36.1194pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm 0.76909pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.63914pt\raise-0.76909pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.1868pt\raise-67.03928pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.63914pt\raise 0.76909pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.63914pt\raise-0.76909pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.63914pt\raise 0.76909pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.63914pt\raise-0.76909pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}{\hbox{\kern 40.4507pt\raise-36.1194pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 71.57498pt\raise-36.1194pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.69337pt\raise-49.01909pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{D}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.7146pt\raise-65.68741pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 40.4507pt\raise-108.02486pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 71.36665pt\raise-108.02486pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 111.63676pt\raise-108.02486pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 148.29576pt\raise-108.02486pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

where we dropped maps to
\Sigma
or
\Sigma\times\Sigma
on the left for aesthetic reasons, so as to arrive at a diagram resembling a trident. Note that while
\mathscr{P}/\mathscr{G}=\mathscr{F}
, the action of
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
projects nontrivially
to the base
\Sigma
of
\mathscr{P}
and one obtains
\mathscr{P}/\mathrm{At(\mathscr{P})}=M
.

As a bundle over
\Sigma
,
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
has typical fibre
\mathscr{P}
, where the Čech 1-cocycle of
\mathscr{P}
is used for the gluing on overlaps by employing the
right

{\mathbb{B}}
-action on
\mathscr{P}
. It can also be viewed as a fibre bundle over
\Sigma\times\Sigma
, in which case the typical fibre is
\mathscr{G}
—and gluings by means of the Čech 1-cocycle are performed using the left-multiplication over the first copy of
\Sigma
and the right-multiplication over the second copy of
\Sigma
. In fact, one can view
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
as a fibre-bundle object in the category of Lie groupoids as follows:

(5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.0347pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.0347pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\>\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.0347pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.02496pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 26.02496pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.87221pt\raise-18.22636pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.87221pt\raise-28.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-36.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 22.26106pt\raise-36.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

Adding the kernel of the groupoid morphism
\pi
into the picture, one obtains an exact sequence of Lie groupoids
{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})\hookrightarrow{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\twoheadrightarrow{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)
. Here, by definition,
{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})
is the preimage of the identity bisection
{\rm Id}(\Sigma)\subset\Sigma\times\Sigma
along the epimorphism
\pi
of Lie groupoids; it is a fibre bundle over
\Sigma
with typical fibre
\mathscr{G}
, where the gluing is performed by conjugation or the adjoint action of the transition 1-cocyle. Differentiation of this leads to the corresponding exact sequence of Lie algebroids, studied as particular
Q
- or
\mathrm{dg}
-bundles in
[
KS14
]
. Objects of
\mathrm{At}(\mathscr{P})
are
\mathscr{D}
-fibres of
\mathscr{P}
sitting over points in
\mathscr{F}
, and, as such, also over points in
\Sigma
. A groupoid element of
\mathrm{At}(\mathscr{P})
, characterised by a triple

(\sigma_{2},g_{1},\sigma_{1})
,
g_{1}\in\mathscr{G}
,
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\Sigma
in a local chart, then maps a point
p\in\mathscr{P}
, given by
(\sigma_{1},g_{2})
for some
g_{2}\in t^{-1}(\{s(g_{1})\})\subset\mathscr{G}
in a local chart, to the point
(\sigma_{2},g_{1}.g_{2})
in this description. In this manner, the action of
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
on
\mathscr{P}
covers the canonical left action of
{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)
on its base
\Sigma
.

Every groupoid action gives rise to an action of the group of bisections, where bisections can be also viewed as submanifolds in the groupoid mapping to
M
diffeomorphically by means of the source and target maps. Applying this logic to the right wing of Diagram (
4
),
i.e.
, to the
\mathscr{G}
-action on
\mathscr{P}
, one recovers the (right)
{\mathbb{B}}
-action on
\mathscr{P}
. For the left wing,
i.e.
, the
\mathrm{At(\mathscr{P})}
-action on
\mathscr{P}
along
\mathscr{F}
, we obtain a characterisation or description of the automorphisms. Find below an expanded version of the trident diagram

119.09633pt\raise-100.69156pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.79813pt\raise-100.69156pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 205.51968pt\raise-100.69156pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 230.7799pt\raise-131.11159pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm id}_{\Sigma}}$}}}\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 351.15215pt\raise-100.69156pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-151.03734pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 68.46483pt\raise-151.03734pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 141.85335pt\raise-151.03734pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 201.90857pt\raise-151.03734pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 173.47447pt\raise-119.36446pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(f,{\rm id}_{\Sigma})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 154.96272pt\raise-108.69156pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 250.04012pt\raise-151.03734pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 296.34526pt\raise-151.03734pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 351.15215pt\raise-151.03734pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 404.48682pt\raise-151.03734pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

(7)

where the exact sequence of Lie groupoids mentioned before is highlighted by the wiggly arrows. Now recall that objects of
\mathrm{At(\mathscr{P})}
are fibres of
\mathscr{D}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{F}
. A
\pi_{\mathscr{F}}
-fibre
\pi_{\mathscr{F}}^{-1}(\{\sigma_{1}\})
assembles all
\mathscr{D}
-fibres sitting over the point
\sigma_{1}\in\Sigma
into the
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}
-fibre over that point, which is isomorphic to the groupoid
\mathscr{G}
. If one takes an arbitrary bisection of the groupoid
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.84724pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.84724pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{At(\mathscr{P})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
, it will map two
\mathscr{D}
-fibres over
\sigma_{1}
to
\mathscr{D}
-fibres sitting over, in general, two different points in
\Sigma
. This is, however, in contradiction with the fact that an automorphism of the bundle
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\to\Sigma
must map
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}
-fibres to
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}
-fibres. This can be cured if one considers only bisections
\beta_{\Phi}
of the groupoid
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.84724pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.84724pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{At(\mathscr{P})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
which are
\pi
-projectable,
i.e.
, those bisections which induce diffeomorphisms
f
on the base
\Sigma
of
\mathscr{P}
.

Note that such complications disappear in the case of an ordinary principal bundle
{\mathsf{P}}
, resulting from ours for the groupoid
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
, in which case the trident degenerates to a W-shaped diagram, where on the left
\mathscr{F}
is replaced by the base
\Sigma
. In such a case, there is a one-to-one correspondence between arbitrary bisections of the Atiyah groupoid
\mathrm{At({\mathsf{P}})}
and automorphisms of
{\mathsf{P}}
.

Theorem C.

Every principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}\to\Sigma
gives rise to a fibre-bundle object

\displaystyle\left({\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\Rightarrow\mathscr{F}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left({\rm Pair}(\Sigma)\Rightarrow\Sigma\right)

in the category of Lie groupoids. The
\pi
-projectable bisections of its total space
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\Rightarrow\mathscr{F}
are in a one-to-one correspondence with automorphisms of
\mathscr{P}
, and those projecting to
{\rm Id}(\Sigma)
with its vertical autormorphisms, i.e., with gauge transformations of
\mathscr{P}
. These gauge transformations act on local connection data
A_{i}
over
O_{i}\times M
—see (
3
)—subordinate to a cover
\{O_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
\Sigma
as in (
177
) in the main text.

Every principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}\to\Sigma
is a principal
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
-bundle over
M
in a canonical way. Together with the structure of a principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle of Theorem B, this combines into a principal
({\rm At}(\mathscr{P}),\mathscr{G})
-bibundle object in
{\rm{\bf Bun}}(\Sigma)
, described by the Trident Diagram (
4
).

We foresee numerous applications of the present formalism. One of these concerns gauge theories lying outside the traditional framework of principal bundles, such as the Poisson sigma model
[
I94
,
ScStS94
]
or the generalisations of Yang–Mills theories provided in
[
S04
,
KS15
]
. Another class would be the study of conditions under which higher-geometric and -cohomological structures defined on the manifold
M
descend to the bundle
\mathscr{F}
upon pullback to
\mathscr{P}
along the moment map
\mu
—
i.e.
, the modelling of descent of objects such as
n
-gerbes and field theories determined by the associated differential characters to characteristic foliations of Lie groupoids through construction of
\mathscr{G}
-equivariant structures on these objects, along the lines of
[
GSW11
,
GSW13
]
.
