2.
Principaloid bundles

Let
\mathscr{G}\sim\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt\sim{\rm{\bf Gr}}
denote a Lie groupoid and
{\mathbb{B}}:={\rm Bisec({\bf Gr})}
its group of bisections. There is a canonical right
\mathscr{G}
-action on
\mathscr{G}
by right translations
r
, and three canonical
{\mathbb{B}}
-actions: the left-multiplication
L
, the right-multiplication
R
, and the conjugation
C
. By means of the target map
t\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{G}\to M
,
L
induces also an action of
{\mathbb{B}}
on
M
, which we denote by
t_{*}
and call the shadow action. We refer to Appendix
B
for further details, notation, and conventions.

2.1.
Three definitions, and relations between them

Let us consider three generalisations of the definition of a principal
{\rm G}
-bundle, with
{\rm G}
a Lie group, and so simultaneously a Lie groupoid over a point
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bullet}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
wih
{\rm Bisec}({\rm G})\cong{\rm G}
. We start with one which emphasises the existence of a right action of the structure groupoid.

Definition 2.1
.

A
principaloid
\mathscr{G}
-bundle
is a fibre-bundle object in the category of right
\mathscr{G}
-modules with a fibrewise right action of
\mathscr{G}
and typical fibre given by the canonical right
\mathscr{G}
-module
\mathscr{G}
.

Remark 2.2
.

Let us unwrap this first definition: It describes a fibre bundle
(\mathscr{P},\Sigma,\mathscr{G},\pi_{\mathscr{P}})
with a base given by a manifold
\Sigma
, to which the total space
\mathscr{P}
maps by a surjective submersion
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\to\Sigma
(the base projection). The total space
\mathscr{P}
carries the structure of a right
\mathscr{G}
-module
(\mathscr{P},\mu,\varrho)
, see Def.
C.1
, with an action

\displaystyle\varrho\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow\mathscr{P},\ (p,g)\longmapsto\varrho(p,g)\equiv\varrho_{g}(p)\equiv p\blacktriangleleft g

which preserves
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}
-fibres. The base admits an open cover
\{O_{i}\}_{i\in I}
with local trivialisations

(8)

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\mathscr{P}}^{-1}(O_{i})\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}\,,\qquad i\in I\,,

which are
\mathscr{G}
-equivariant diffeomorphisms in the sense of Def.
C.1
. In other words, for all
\sigma\in O_{i}
and
g,h\in\mathscr{G}
, we have the equivalence

\displaystyle(g,h)\in\mathscr{G}{}_{s}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad(\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g)),h)\in\mathscr{P}{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\,,

and the identity

\displaystyle\varrho_{h}\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau^{-1}(\sigma,g)\bigr)=\mathscr{P}\tau^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,r_{h}(g)\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{P}\tau^{-1}(\sigma,g.h)

holds true whenever either condition is satisfied.

There is another definition, which takes as its base the choice of the structure group and its realisation on the typical fibre. This definition is, then, more directly related to the abstract notion of principal bundle as a fibre
{\rm G}
-bundle with the specific choice of the realisation of the structure group on the typical fibre, namely:
L({\rm G})\subset{\rm Diff}(\mathscr{G})
, see
[
KMS93
]
.

Definition 2.3
.

A
preprincipaloid bundle
is a fibre bundle with typical fibre
\mathscr{G}
which admits a reduction of the structure group to the realisation
L({\mathbb{B}})\subset{\rm Diff}(\mathscr{G})
of the group
{\mathbb{B}}
of bisections of
\mathscr{G}
by left-multiplication. A
principaloid bundle
is a preprincipaloid bundle together with a choice of such a reduction.

Remark 2.4
.

A preprincipaloid bundle is a fibre bundle
(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},\Sigma,\mathscr{G},\pi_{\mathscr{P}})
which admits a choice of local trivialisations

(9)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{-1}(O_{i})\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}\,,\qquad i\in I\,,

associated with an open cover
\{O_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of its base
\Sigma
, for which the corresponding transition maps take values in the group of bisections
{\mathbb{B}}
of
\mathscr{G}
, i.e., for every
(\sigma,g)\in O_{ij}\times\mathscr{G}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}\circ\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{j}^{-1}(\sigma,g)=\bigl(\sigma,\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g\bigr)\,,

where

\displaystyle\beta_{ij}\in C^{\infty}(O_{ij},{\mathbb{B}})\,.

Accordingly, there exists a bundle isomorphism

(10)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\cong\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,(O_{i}\times\mathscr{G})/\sim_{L{}_{\beta_{\cdot\cdot}}}\,,

which puts the bundle on the right-hand side in the role of a model of
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
.

Fixing such a trivialisation, up to equivalence, constitutes a choice—of which there could be many—turning
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
into a principaloid bundle. Here, two trivialisations are considered equivalent if their joint refinement has transition functions in
L({\mathbb{B}})
.

Given that left-multiplication commutes with right-multiplication, it is clear that once a reduction of the structure group to
L({\mathbb{B}})
has been fixed,
{\mathbb{B}}
acts on
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
from the
right
by bundle automorphisms which cover the identity on
\Sigma
(termed vertical): Such an action is induced from right-mutiplications
R
in local trivialisations as they glue smoothly over intersections of the trivialisation charts. Note that different reductions,
i.e.
, different equivalence classes of trivialisations, in general give rise to different
{\mathbb{B}}
-actions.

The
{\mathbb{B}}
-action induced on the principaloid bundle
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
can be presented as a group homomorphism

(11)

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathbb{B}}\longrightarrow{\rm Aut}_{{\rm{\bf Bun}}(\Sigma)}(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}})_{\rm vert}\,.

We have, for all
(\sigma,g)\in O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}
and
i\in I
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}_{\beta}\circ\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g):=\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,R_{\beta}(g)\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,g\vartriangleleft\beta\bigr)\,,

and so trivialisations within an equivalence class are (tautologically)
{\mathbb{B}}
-equivariant.

Remark 2.5
.

Principaloid bundles can be viewed as bundles (non-canonically) associated to principal
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundles by the action
L\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathbb{B}}\to{\rm Diff}(\mathscr{G})
. Such principal
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundles are represented—in virtue of the classic Clutching Theorem—by (classes of) the corresponding transition 1-cocycles
\{\beta_{ij}\}_{(i,j)\in I^{\times 2}_{\mathcal{O}}}
. It is not hard to see that principaloid bundles, while offering us the practical advantage of finite dimensionality, actually carry the same Čech-cohomological information as the underlying infinite-dimensional objects. Indeed, the 1-cocycle
\{\beta_{ij}\}_{(i,j)\in I^{\times 2}_{\mathcal{O}}}
can readily be recovered from its realisation
\,L_{\beta_{ij}}\,
on the typical fibre
\mathscr{G}
through evaluation on the identity bisection
{\rm Id}(M)\subset\mathscr{G}
,

\displaystyle L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)}({\rm Id}_{m})\equiv\bigl(\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\bigr)\bigl(t\bigl({\rm Id}_{m}\bigr)\bigr).{\rm Id}_{m}=\bigl(\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\bigr)(m)\,.

Finally, one may ask to what extent the existence of the
{\mathbb{B}}
-action is a defining property of a principaloid bundle. In order to study this question, let us introduce another notion, adapted to the right action of
{\mathbb{B}}
.

Definition 2.6
.

A
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle

\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
over
\Sigma
is a fibre-bundle object in the category of right
{\mathbb{B}}
-spaces, with a fibrewise right action of
{\mathbb{B}}
and typical fibre given by the canonical right
{\mathbb{B}}
-space
\mathscr{G}
, on which
{\mathbb{B}}
acts by right-multiplication.

We shall now examine relations between the three definitions given above. In the first step, we establish an equivalence between the first two definitions of principaloid bundles.

Theorem 2.7
.

Every principaloid
\mathscr{G}
-bundle carries a canonical structure of a principaloid bundle, and vice versa.

Proof.

Consider a principaloid
\mathscr{G}
-bundle
\mathscr{P}
with
\mathscr{G}
-equivariant trivialisations
\mathscr{P}\tau_{i},\ i\in I
as in Remark
2.2
. As their smooth inverses are also (automatically)
\mathscr{G}
-equivariant, we obtain, for arbitrary
\sigma\in O_{i}\cap O_{j}\equiv O_{ij}
and
g\in\mathscr{G}
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}\circ\mathscr{P}\tau_{j}^{-1}(\sigma,g)=\bigl(\sigma,t_{ij}(\sigma,g)\bigr)\,,

for some smooth maps
t_{ij}\hskip-2.0pt:O_{ij}\to{\rm Diff}_{r(\mathscr{G})}(\mathscr{G}),\ \sigma\mapsto t_{ij}(\sigma,\cdot)
with values in

\displaystyle{\rm Diff}_{r(\mathscr{G})}(\mathscr{G}):=C^{\infty}_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathscr{G},\mathscr{G})\subset{\rm Diff}(\mathscr{G})\,,

the
group of
r(\mathscr{G})
-equivariant diffeomorphisms of

\mathscr{G}
, see Def.
C.1
. This subgroup of
{\rm Diff}(\mathscr{G})
is identified in the following proposition.

Proposition 2.8
.

For every Lie groupoid
\mathscr{G}
,

\displaystyle{\rm Diff}_{r(\mathscr{G})}(\mathscr{G})=L({\mathbb{B}})\,.

Proof of Proposition:

The relation
L({\mathbb{B}})\subset{\rm Diff}_{r(\mathscr{G})}(\mathscr{G})
follows straightforwardly from the fact that
L({\mathbb{B}})
is implemented by left translations on
\mathscr{G}
, which commute with right translations.

For the reverse relation, note that for every
\Phi\in{\rm Diff}_{r(\mathscr{G})}(\mathscr{G})
and
g\in\mathscr{G}
, we have the identity

\displaystyle\Phi(g)\equiv\Phi\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}.g\bigr)=\Phi\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr).g\,.

Define a smooth map

\displaystyle\varphi\hskip-2.0pt:M\longrightarrow\mathscr{G},\ m\longmapsto\Phi({\rm Id}_{m})

to rewrite the above as

(12)

\displaystyle\Phi(g)=\varphi\bigl(t(g)\bigr).g\,.

This further implies

\displaystyle(s\circ\varphi)\bigl(t(g)\bigr)=s\bigl(\Phi(g).g^{-1}\bigr)=s\bigl(g^{-1}\bigr)=t(g)\equiv{\rm id}_{M}\bigl(t(g)\bigr)\,,

which yields the identity

\displaystyle s\circ\varphi={\rm id}_{M}

in virtue of the surjectivity of
t
.

Up to now, we have not fully taken into account the assumption of invertibility of
\Phi
, which further constrains
\varphi
. By a standard argument, the
\mathscr{G}
-equivariance of
\Phi
implies the same property of its inverse, and so—in the light of the reasoning presented above—there exists a smooth map
\widetilde{\varphi}\hskip-2.0pt:M\to\mathscr{G}
such that, for every
g\in\mathscr{G}
,

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}(g)=\widetilde{\varphi}\bigl(t(g)\bigr).g\,.

We then obtain equalities

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(\Phi^{-1}\circ\Phi\bigr)(g)=\widetilde{\varphi}\bigl(t\bigl(\varphi\bigl(t(g)\bigr).g\bigr)\bigr).\varphi\bigl(t(g)\bigr).g=\widetilde{\varphi}\bigl((t\circ\varphi)\bigl(t(g)\bigr)\bigr).\varphi\bigl(t(g)\bigr).g\,,

(14)

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(\Phi\circ\Phi^{-1}\bigr)(g)=\varphi\bigl((t\circ\widetilde{\varphi})\bigl(t(g)\bigr)\bigr).\widetilde{\varphi}\bigl(t(g)\bigr).g\,,

valid for every
g\in\mathscr{G}
, or, equivalently,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varphi}\bigl((t\circ\varphi)(m)\bigr).\varphi(m)={\rm Id}_{m}\,,

(15)

(16)

\displaystyle\varphi\bigl((t\circ\widetilde{\varphi})(m)\bigr).\widetilde{\varphi}(m)={\rm Id}_{m}\,,

valid for every
m\in M
. Upon evaluating the target map on both sides of the two identities, we arrive at the conclusion that
t\circ\widetilde{\varphi}
is a smooth inverse of
t\circ\varphi
, and so, in particular,

\displaystyle t\circ\varphi\in{\rm Diff}(M)\,.

From our hitherto analysis, the mapping
\varphi
emerges as a bisection of
\mathscr{G}
, and Eq. (
12
) yields

\displaystyle\Phi\equiv L_{\varphi}\,,

which is exactly the postulated result.

\blacksquare

In virtue of the above proposition, the transition maps
t_{ij}
of the principaloid
\mathscr{G}
-bundle
\mathscr{P}
take the special form

\displaystyle t_{ij}(\sigma)=L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)}\,,

and so right-
\mathscr{G}
-equivariant trivialisations determine a reduction of the structure group of the bundle as in Def.
2.3
.

For the converse claim, consider a principaloid bundle
(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},\Sigma,\mathscr{G},\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}})
with a choice of reduction as in Remark
2.4
. For the structure of a right
\mathscr{G}
-module on
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
, define smooth maps

\displaystyle\mu_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{-1}(O_{i})\longrightarrow M,\,\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g)\longmapsto s(g)\,.

On overlaps
O_{ij}\times\mathscr{G}\ni(\sigma,g)
of trivialisation charts, we find

\displaystyle\mu_{j}\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g)\bigr)=\mu\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g\bigr)\bigr)\equiv s\bigl(\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g\bigr)=s(g)\equiv\mu_{i}\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g)\bigr)\,.

Hence, the
\mu_{i}
are restrictions
\mu_{i}=\mu\big|_{\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{-1}(O_{i})}
of a globally smooth map

\displaystyle\mu\hskip-2.0pt:\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\longrightarrow M\,.

Next, consider smooth maps

\displaystyle\varrho_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{-1}(O_{i}){}_{\mu_{i}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow\pi_{\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}}^{-1}(O_{i}),\ \bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g),h\bigr)\longmapsto\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g.h)\,.

Again, the above glue to a globally smooth map

\displaystyle\varrho\hskip-2.0pt:\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}

since, for arbitrary
(\sigma,g)\in O_{ij}\times\mathscr{G}
and
h\in t^{-1}(\{s(g)\})
,

(18)

\displaystyle\varrho_{j}\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g),h\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varrho_{j}\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g\bigr),h\bigr)\equiv\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,\bigl(\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g\bigr).h\bigr)=\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright(g.h)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,g.h\bigr)\equiv\varrho_{i}\bigl(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g),h\bigr)\,,

where in the third equality we used Eq. (
209
). The triple
(\widetilde{\mathscr{P}},\mu,\varrho)
satisfies axioms (GrM1)–(GrM3) of a right
\mathscr{G}
-module as it is locally modelled on the canonical right-
\mathscr{G}
-module structure of Eq. (
197
), in conformity with Def.
2.1
.
∎

Remark 2.9
.

Given the equivalence between the notions of principaloid bundle and principaloid
\mathscr{G}
-bundle, we shall use them interchangeably, choosing the latter, in particular, whenever we want to emphasise the underlying structure groupoid.

Having established the equivalence of the first two generalisations of the notion of principal
{\rm G}
-bundle to Lie-groupoidal symmetries, we may, next, investigate a relation between principaloid bundles and
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundles. To this end, observe that a
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle is a fibre bundle
(\widehat{\mathscr{P}},\Sigma,\mathscr{G},\pi_{\widehat{\mathscr{P}}})
on whose total space
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
there is
given
an action

(19)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{R}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathbb{B}}\longrightarrow{\rm Aut}_{{\rm{\bf Bun}}(\Sigma)}(\widehat{\mathscr{P}})_{\rm vert}

such that for
every
choice of local trivialisations

(20)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\widehat{\mathscr{P}}}^{-1}(O_{i})\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}\,,\qquad i\in I

we have
{\mathbb{B}}
-equivariance

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{R}}{}_{\beta}\circ\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g)=\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,R_{\beta}(g)\bigr)\,,\qquad(\sigma,g)\in O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}\,.

We then obtain, for arbitrary
\sigma^{\prime}\in O_{i}\cap O_{j}\equiv O_{ij}
and
g\in\mathscr{G}
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}\circ\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{j}^{-1}(\sigma^{\prime},g)=\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},t_{ij}(\sigma^{\prime},g)\bigr)\,,

where the
t_{ij}\hskip-2.0pt:O_{ij}\to{\rm Diff}_{{\mathbb{B}}}(\mathscr{G}),\ \sigma\mapsto t_{ij}(\sigma,\cdot)
are smooth maps with values in

(21)

\displaystyle{\rm Diff}_{{\mathbb{B}}}(\mathscr{G})=\bigl\{\ \Psi\in{\rm Diff}(\mathscr{G})\>|\;\forall\beta\in{\mathbb{B}}\hskip-2.0pt:\>\Psi\circ R_{\beta}=R_{\beta}\circ\Psi\ \bigr\}\,,

the
group of
R({\mathbb{B}})
-equivariant diffeomorphisms of
\mathscr{G}
. Thus, whenever, for some choice of local trivialisations, the transition maps
t_{ij}
take the special form:

(22)

\displaystyle t_{ij}(\sigma)=L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)}\,,

expressed in terms of a Čech 1-cocycle
\beta_{ij}\hskip-2.0pt:O_{ij}\to{\mathbb{B}},\ (i,j)\in I^{\times 2}_{\mathcal{O}}
, the
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
is a principaloid bundle. This, however, is not true in general, and so
not
every
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle is a principaloid bundle. This is readily seen by means of the following example
3
3

3

The authors thank Damian Kayzer and Jakub Filipek for suggesting and working out (a special case of) this example.
.

Example 2.10
.

Consider, after Ref.
[
SWo16
, Remark 2.18 b)]
, the pair groupoid of a manifold
M=M_{1}\sqcup M_{2}
defined by two non-diffeomorphic connected manifolds
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
of the same dimension. Since
{\mathbb{B}}\cong{\rm Diff}(M)
, there is clearly no global bisection through a point
(m_{2},m_{1})\in M_{2}\times M_{1}\subset M\times M
as there is no (global) diffeomorphism which could map
m_{1}
to
m_{2}
. We have the following decomposition of the arrow manifold of
{\rm Pair}(M)
into disjoint connected components

\displaystyle M\times M=M_{1}\times M_{1}\sqcup M_{1}\times M_{2}\sqcup M_{2}\times M_{1}\sqcup M_{2}\times M_{2}\,.

Take an arbitrary diffeomorphism of
M\times M
with the corresponding decomposition

(23)

\displaystyle\Phi={\rm id}_{M_{1}\times M_{1}}\sqcup{\rm id}_{M_{1}\times M_{2}}\sqcup(f_{2}\times{\rm id}_{M_{1}})\sqcup{\rm id}_{M_{2}\times M_{2}}\,,

written for some
f_{2}\in{\rm Diff}(M_{2})\setminus\{{\rm id}_{M_{2}}\}
. As the diffeomorphism has a trivial restriction to the second argument in its domain, it manifestly commutes with

\displaystyle R({\mathbb{B}})=\bigl\{\ {\rm id}_{M}\times(f_{1}\sqcup f_{2})\ |\ (f_{1},f_{2})\in{\rm Diff}(M_{1})\times{\rm Diff}(M_{2})\ \bigr\}\subset{\rm Diff}(M)\,.

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle L({\mathbb{B}})=\bigl\{\ (f_{1}\sqcup f_{2})\times{\rm id}_{M}\ |\ (f_{1},f_{2})\in{\rm Diff}(M_{1})\times{\rm Diff}(M_{2})\ \bigr\}\subset{\rm Diff}(M)\,,

and so, clearly,
\Phi\notin L({\mathbb{B}})
.

Restricting the class of structure groupoids as below leads to an equivalence of the two notions, the pricipaloid (
\mathscr{G}
-)bundle and the
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle.

Definition 2.11
.

A Lie groupoid
\mathscr{G}
shall be called
{\rm Id}
-reducibile
if through every point
g\in\mathscr{G}
, there is a global bisection
\beta_{g}\in{\mathbb{B}}
such that

(24)

\displaystyle g=\beta_{g}(s(g))\equiv R_{\beta_{g}}\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr)\,.

Example 2.12
.

There is a large class of
{\rm Id}
-reducibile Lie groupoids including all source-connected ones, see
[
ZCL09
, Thm. 3.1]
, but evidently also every action groupoid.

Theorem 2.13
.

For an
{\rm Id}
-reducibile Lie groupoid
\mathscr{G}
, there is a one-to-one correspondence between principaloid (
\mathscr{G}
-)bundles and
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundles.

Proof.

That every principaloid bundle is a
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle is implied directly by the existence of the right action
\mathscr{R}
of
{\mathbb{B}}
, see (
11
).

Conversely, transition maps
t_{ij}
of a
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle associated with arbitrary local trivialisations take values in
{\rm Diff}_{\mathbb{B}}(\mathscr{G})
, see (
21
), and so they take the desired form (
22
) in direct consequence of a proposition which we prove next.

Proposition 2.14
.

For every
{\rm Id}
-reducibile Lie groupoid
\mathscr{G}
, the commutant or centraliser
C_{{\rm Diff}(\mathscr{G})}(R({\mathbb{B}}))\equiv R({\mathbb{B}})^{\prime}
within
\,{\rm Diff}(\mathscr{G})
of the subgroup
R({\mathbb{B}})
, the group of right-multiplications by bisections, is
L({\mathbb{B}})
, the group of left-multiplications by bisections:

(25)

\displaystyle{\rm Diff}_{{\mathbb{B}}}(\mathscr{G})\equiv R({\mathbb{B}})^{\prime}=L({\mathbb{B}})\,.

Proof of Proposition:

Consider a diffeomorphism
\Phi\in{\rm Diff}(\mathscr{G})
with the property that, for all
(g,\beta)\in\mathscr{G}\times{\mathbb{B}}
, one has

(26)

\displaystyle\Phi(g\vartriangleleft\beta)=\Phi(g)\vartriangleleft\beta\,,

for every point
g\in\mathscr{G}
. We shall demonstrate that this implies the identity

(27)

\displaystyle s\circ\Phi=s\,.

Intuitively, this identity is clear: If it were
not
satisfied at, say,
g\in\mathscr{G}
, we could deform a given
\beta
, for which (
26
) holds true at
g
, by replacing
\beta
with its product
\beta\cdot\delta
with a bisection
\delta\in{\mathbb{B}}
differing from
{\rm Id}
only in a neighbourhood of
s(\Phi(g))
disjoint
4
4

4

The existence of such a neighbourhood is guaranteed by the Hausdorff topology of
M
.
from that of
s(g)
. When plugged back into Eq. (
26
), the deformed bisection would give us the same left-hand side, but a changed right-hand side, and so a contradiction. Below, we make this reasoning precise by passing to the tangent of
{\mathbb{B}}
.

Making use of Prop.
E.1
(v), we may rewrite this identity as

\displaystyle\Phi\bigl(l_{g}\circ R_{\beta}({\rm Id}_{s(g)})\bigr)=\Phi\bigl(R_{\beta}\circ l_{g}({\rm Id}_{s(g)})\bigr)\equiv\Phi\bigl(g\vartriangleleft\beta\bigr)=\Phi\bigl(g\bigr)\vartriangleleft\beta\equiv R_{\beta}\circ l_{\Phi(g)}\bigl({\rm Id}_{s\circ\Phi(g)}\bigr)=l_{\Phi(g)}\circ R_{\beta}\bigl({\rm Id}_{s\circ\Phi(g)}\bigr)\,.

This yields

(28)

\displaystyle{\rm Id}_{s\circ\Phi(g)}\vartriangleleft\beta=l_{\Phi(g)^{-1}}\circ\Phi\circ l_{g}\bigl({\rm Id}_{s(g)}\vartriangleleft\beta\bigr)\,.

Invoking Thm.
E.5
and taking
\beta\equiv F^{t}_{\mathscr{X}}\circ{\rm Id}
to be the value of the flow
F^{\cdot}_{\mathscr{X}}
of a vector field
\mathscr{X}\in\Gamma_{\rm c}({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}t\big|_{{\rm Id}(M)})
, attained at a ‘time’
t\approx 0
away from the initial condition
{\rm Id}
, we take the derivative of relation (
28
) in the parameter
t
at
t=0
, whereby we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}\bigl({\rm Id}_{s\circ\Phi(g)}\bigr)={\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{s(g)}}\bigl(l{}_{\Phi(g)^{-1}}\circ\Phi\circ l{}_{g}\bigr)\bigl(\mathscr{X}\bigl({\rm Id}_{s(g)}\bigr)\bigr)\,.

Next, we apply the following argument to eliminate the possibility of
s\circ\Phi(g)\neq s(g)
: Assume
\mathscr{X}
to satisfy
\mathscr{X}({\rm Id}_{s\circ\Phi(g)})\neq 0
, and so also
\mathscr{X}({\rm Id}_{s(g)})\neq 0
. If the inequality were true, we could take separating neighbourhoods
O_{1}\ni{\rm Id}_{s(g)}
and
O_{2}\ni{\rm Id}_{s\circ\Phi(g)}
(with
O_{1}\cap O_{2}=\emptyset
) and replace the vector field
\mathscr{X}
with
\mathscr{X}^{\chi}\equiv\chi\vartriangleright\mathscr{X}
for some
\chi\in C^{\infty}({\rm Id}(M),{\mathbb{R}})
with
{\rm supp}\,\chi\subset O_{2}
and
\chi({\rm Id}_{s\circ\Phi(g)})=1
, whereupon we would arrive at the contradiction

\displaystyle 0\neq\mathscr{X}\bigl({\rm Id}_{s\circ\Phi(g)}\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{X}^{\chi}\bigl({\rm Id}_{s\circ\Phi(g)}\bigr)={\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{s(g)}}\bigl(l{}_{\Phi(g)^{-1}}\circ\Phi\circ l{}_{g}\bigr)\bigl(\mathscr{X}^{\chi}\bigl({\rm Id}_{s(g)}\bigr)\bigr)=0\,.

With this result in hand, we may use the assumption of
{\rm Id}
-reducibity to find, for every
g\in\mathscr{G}
,

(30)

\displaystyle\Phi(g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\vartriangleleft\beta_{g}\bigr)=\Phi\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr)\vartriangleleft\beta_{g}\equiv\Phi\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr).\beta_{g}\bigl((t_{*}\beta_{g})^{-1}\bigl((s\circ\Phi)\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr).\beta_{g}\bigl((t_{*}\beta_{g})^{-1}\bigl(s\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)=\Phi\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr).\beta_{g}\bigl((t_{*}\beta_{g})^{-1}\bigl(t(g)\bigr)\bigr)\,,

and so, by Prop.
E.4
(i),

\displaystyle\Phi(g)=\Phi\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr).g\,,

see Eq. (
215
). From this point onwards, the proof continues exactly as that of Prop.
2.8
.

\blacksquare

The above reasoning implies that
every
trivialisation determines a reduction of the structure group of
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
as in Def.
2.3
.
∎

For
non
-bisection-complete structure groupoids
\mathscr{G}
, instead, principaloid bundles form a subset of the set of
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundles. In general, this subset is proper, which can be established directly through examination of
{\mathbb{B}}
-actions on the total space of a (simple) preprincipaloid bundle induced by reductions from different
{\mathbb{B}}
-equivariance classes, see Example
2.27
below.

We close the present section with a corollary of the reasoning employed in the last proof, which shall prove useful when we come to discuss connections on pricipaloid bundles.

Proposition 2.15
.

For every Lie groupoid
\mathscr{G}
, vector fields from the subspace

\displaystyle\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})^{R({\mathbb{B}})}:=\{\ \mathscr{X}\in\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})\quad|\quad\forall_{\beta\in{\mathbb{B}}}\hskip-2.0pt:R_{\beta\,*}(\mathscr{X})=\mathscr{X}\ \}\,,

i.e., invariant under right-multiplication by bisections, are tangent to
s
-fibres,

\displaystyle\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})^{R({\mathbb{B}})}\subset\Gamma({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s)\,.

Proof.

By a standard argument, the flow
\Phi_{\mathscr{X}}
of an
R({\mathbb{B}})
-invariant vector field
\mathscr{X}\in\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})^{R({\mathbb{B}})}
is
R({\mathbb{B}})
-equivariant. Now, recall identity (
27
), which uses merely the
R({\mathbb{B}})
-equivariance of
\Phi
, and does
not
require that the latter be a global diffeomorphism. It applies to
\Phi_{\mathscr{X}}
verbatim, yielding

\displaystyle s\circ\Phi_{\mathscr{X}}=s\,.

Thus, integral lines of
\mathscr{X}
are contained in
s
-fibres, and so their tangents lie in
{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s
. However, the tangents are, by definition, just values attained by
\mathscr{X}
along the corresponding integral lines, and the latter go through every point in
\mathscr{G}
, which implies the statement of the proposition.
∎

2.2.
The induced principal groupoid bundle

In the literature, there is yet another generalisation of principal
{\rm G}
-bundles with a structure group
{\rm G}
replaced by a structure groupoid
\mathscr{G}
, which goes under the name of principal groupoid bundles
[
MacK87
,
Mo91
]
. In this case, the main requirement is that there be an action of the groupoid
\mathscr{G}
on the total space
{\rm\breve{P}}
along a moment map
\mu\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm\breve{P}}\to M
. The action is required to be transitive and free along the fibres in
{\rm\breve{P}}
, which, in general, is not a fibre bundle, but a surjective submersion over the base
\Sigma
. (See Appendix
C
for details).

The notion of a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
, introduced in the present paper, is different from this generalisation. However, every principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}\to\Sigma
is a principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle in a canonical way, but over a different base, which itself is a fibre bundle over
\Sigma
. More specifically:

Theorem 2.16
.

Every principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
canonically induces a fibre bundle
\mathscr{F}
with model

(31)

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}\cong\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,\bigl(O_{i}\times M\bigr)/\sim_{t_{*}\beta_{\cdot\cdot}}\,,

written in terms of the transition 1-cocycle
\{\beta_{ij}\}_{(i,j)\in I^{\times 2}_{\mathcal{O}}}
of
\mathscr{P}
, which we realise by the shadow action of
{\mathbb{B}}
on
M
. It comes with a bundle map

(32)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.07774pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.05624pt\raise-21.67914pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 40.79095pt\raise-21.65958pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathscr{F}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.76254pt\raise-28.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-35.73051pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 20.62915pt\raise-35.73051pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 51.34164pt\raise-35.73051pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\quad,

locally modelled on
t\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{G}\to M
.
The quintuple
(\mathscr{P},\mathscr{F},\mathscr{D},\mu,\varrho)
, in which
\mu\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\to M
is the moment map for the defining
\mathscr{G}
-action
\varrho\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\to\mathscr{P}
on
\mathscr{P}
, carries a canonical structure of a principal-
\mathscr{G}
-bundle object in the category of fibre bundles over
\Sigma
, in the sense captured by the commutative diagram

(33)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 17.97232pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.97232pt\raise-24.97844pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm id}_{\Sigma}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-42.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.95067pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 55.29022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 23.00833pt\raise 5.00694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathscr{P}}}$}}}\kern 49.67215pt\raise-24.97844pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{D}}$}}}\kern 79.76208pt\raise-21.21953pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.57185pt\raise-42.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 94.83118pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 127.56656pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.80638pt\raise-0.5914pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.80638pt\raise 0.5914pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 103.16867pt\raise-42.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.80638pt\raise-0.5914pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.80638pt\raise 0.5914pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.80638pt\raise-0.5914pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.80638pt\raise 0.5914pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 27.95067pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 55.42911pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.09512pt\raise-54.96384pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathscr{F}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 89.43535pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 131.49017pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

Proof.

Formula (
31
) implies that the induced bundle admits local trivialisations

(34)

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\mathscr{F}}^{-1}(O_{i})\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }O_{i}\times M

such that the corresponding transition mappings are

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}\circ\mathscr{F}\tau_{j}^{-1}\hskip-2.0pt:O_{ij}\times M\longrightarrow O_{ij}\times M,\ (\sigma,m)\longmapsto\bigl(\sigma,t_{*}\bigl(\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\bigr)(m)\bigr)\,.

Now, the local mappings

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\mathscr{P}}^{-1}(O_{i})\longrightarrow\pi_{\mathscr{F}}^{-1}(O_{i}),\ \mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,g\bigr)\longmapsto\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,t(g)\bigr)

glue up smoothly at
\sigma^{\prime}\in O_{ij}
as

(36)

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}_{j}\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},g\bigr)\bigr)=\mathscr{D}_{j}\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},L_{\beta_{ji}(\sigma^{\prime})}\bigl(g\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{F}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},t\bigl(\beta_{ji}(\sigma^{\prime})\bigl(t(g)\bigr).g\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},t_{*}\beta_{ji}(\sigma^{\prime})\bigl(t(g)\bigr)\bigr)=\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},t_{*}\beta_{ij}(\sigma^{\prime})\circ t_{*}\beta_{ji}(\sigma^{\prime})\bigl(t(g)\bigr)\bigr)

(38)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},t_{*}\bigl(\beta_{ij}(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot\beta_{ji}(\sigma^{\prime})\bigr)\bigl(t(g)\bigr)\bigr)=\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},t(g)\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{D}_{i}\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},g\bigr)\bigr)\,.

Taking the above into account, alongside the structure of a right-
\mathscr{G}
-module space
(\mathscr{P},\mu,\varrho)
on
\mathscr{P}
detailed in the second part of the proof of Thm.
2.7
, we demonstrate the existence, on the fibred square
\mathscr{P}\times_{\mathscr{F}}\mathscr{P}\equiv\mathscr{P}\,{}_{\mathscr{D}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\mathscr{D}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{P}
, of a unique map

(39)

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathscr{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\times_{\mathscr{F}}\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G}

with the property
({\rm pr}_{1},\phi_{\mathscr{P}})=({\rm pr}_{1},\varrho)^{-1}
. The latter property can be expressed more explicitly as

(40)

\displaystyle t\circ\phi_{\mathscr{P}}(p_{1},p_{2})=\mu(p_{1})\,,\qquad\qquad p_{2}=\varrho\bigl(p_{1},\phi_{\mathscr{P}}(p_{1},p_{2})\bigr)\,,

with arbitrary
(p_{1},p_{2})\in\mathscr{P}\times_{\mathscr{F}}\mathscr{P}
. Consider smooth maps

\displaystyle\phi_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\mathscr{P}}^{-1}(O_{i})\times_{\mathscr{F}}\pi_{O_{i}}^{-1}(O_{i})\longrightarrow\mathscr{G},\ \bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g_{1}),\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g_{2})\bigr)\longmapsto g_{1}^{-1}.g_{2}\,,

whose well-definedness hinges on the identity
s(g_{1}^{-1})=t(g_{1})=t(g_{2})
, derived from

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,t(g_{1})\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{D}\circ\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g_{1})=\mathscr{D}\circ\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g_{2})\equiv\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,t(g_{2})\bigr)\,.

These maps satisfy the equality, written for an arbitrary
\sigma\in O_{ij}
,

(42)

\displaystyle\phi{}_{j}\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g_{1}),\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g_{2})\bigr)=\phi{}_{j}\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g_{1}\bigr),\mathscr{P}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g_{2}\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\bigl(\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g_{1}\bigr)^{-1}.\bigl(\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g_{2}\bigr)=\bigl(g_{1}^{-1}\vartriangleleft\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\bigr).\bigl(\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g_{2}\bigr)=g_{1}^{-1}.\bigl(\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\vartriangleright\bigl(\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g_{2}\bigr)\bigr)

(44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g_{1}^{-1}.\bigl(\bigl(\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\cdot\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\bigr)\vartriangleright g_{2}\bigr)=g_{1}^{-1}.g_{2}\equiv\phi{}_{i}\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g_{1}),\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g_{2})\bigr)\,,

in whose derivation we have invoked Eqs. (
208
) and (
210
). Hence, they glue up to a globally smooth map

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathscr{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\times_{\mathscr{F}}\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G}\,,\qquad\phi_{\mathscr{P}}\big|_{\pi_{\mathscr{P}}^{-1}(O_{i})\times_{\mathscr{F}}\pi_{\mathscr{P}}^{-1}(O_{i})}\equiv\phi_{i}\,.

The desired properties (
40
) are readily verified in the local presentation.
∎

Definition 2.17
.

We shall call
\mathscr{F}
the
shadow
(
bundle
) of the principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
and
\mathscr{D}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{F}
the
sitting-duck map
.
5
5

5

Recall that a sitting duck is an easy target.
We call the map (
39
) the
division map
of
\mathscr{P}
.

Remark 2.18
.

In the light of Thm.
C.10
, the above theorem implies that the shadow bundle
\mathscr{F}
is diffeomorphic to
\mathscr{P}/\mathscr{G}
and the sitting-duck map is the quotient map
\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{P}/\mathscr{G}
. Note, however, that in contrast to ordinary Lie-group actions, freeness and properness of a
\mathscr{G}
-action does not, in general, give a principaloid bundle.

Remark 2.19
.

The division map
\phi_{\mathscr{P}}
of
\mathscr{P}
defined in the proof above is a straightforward generalisation of the standard division map
\phi_{\rm P}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm P}\times_{\Sigma}{\rm P}\to{\rm G}
of a principal
{\rm G}
-bundle. Indeed, if we take into account Example
2.23
, we obtain
\mathscr{P}\times_{\mathscr{F}}\mathscr{P}\equiv{\rm P}\times_{\Sigma}{\rm P}
for
\mathscr{G}={\rm G}
, and the mapping is seen to assign to any pair of points in the
t
-fibre over a given point in the base (in a local trivialisation) the unique arrow that connects one of them to the other.

We close the present section, dedicated to a more detailed study of the defining action of the structure groupoid on a principaloid bundle, with a reinterpretation of the
{\mathbb{B}}
-action (
11
) as an induced one. The induction is a consequence of the following general result, which shall become of particular relevance in our discussion of automorphisms of principaloid bundles.

Proposition 2.20
.

Let
(X,\mu,\varrho)
be a right
\mathscr{G}
-module. The action
\varrho\hskip-2.0pt:X{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\to X
canonically induces a right action
\varrho_{\mathbb{B}}\hskip-2.0pt:X\times{\mathbb{B}}\longrightarrow X
of
{\mathbb{B}}
on
X
, as determined by the following commutative diagram:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 50.04659pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-34.4763pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{{\rm id}_{M}}\hskip-1.0ptM\times{\mathbb{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.0077pt\raise 5.93333pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.92778pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt{\rm id}_{X}\times{\rm Inv}\circ{\rm ev}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 119.83456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 119.83456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 138.73445pt\raise-24.97844pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varrho}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 138.73445pt\raise-42.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-16.07637pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\times{\mathbb{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-50.04659pt\raise-24.97845pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{({\rm id}_{X},\mu)\times{\rm Inv}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-8.41664pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.70589pt\raise-55.14438pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varrho_{\mathbb{B}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 131.19974pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 131.19974pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

where
{\rm ev}\hskip-2.0pt:M\times{\mathbb{B}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G},\ (m,\beta)\longmapsto\beta(m)
is the
evaluation map
. An analogous statement holds true for left
\mathscr{G}
-modules.

Proof.

The diagram yields the map

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathbb{B}}\hskip-2.0pt:X\times{\mathbb{B}}\longrightarrow X\hskip-2.0pt:(x,\beta)\longmapsto x\blacktriangleleft\beta^{-1}\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)^{-1}\,.

Here,
\beta^{-1}
is the inverse of
\beta
in the group
{\mathbb{B}}
, whereas the inverse applied after evaluation of this
\beta^{-1}
on
\mu(x)
is the one in
\mathscr{G}
. The well-definedness of the map follows from the identity

\displaystyle t\bigl(\beta^{-1}\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)^{-1}\bigr)\equiv t\bigl(\beta\bigl((t_{*}\beta)^{-1}\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)=\mu(x)\,.

It now suffices to check the axioms of a group action, invoking those of the groupoid module along the way. We have, by (GrM2) from Def.
C.1
in Appendix
C
, for every
x\in X
,

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathbb{B}}(x,{\rm Id})\equiv x\blacktriangleleft\bigl({\rm Id}_{\mu(x)}^{-1}\bigr)^{-1}=x\blacktriangleleft{\rm Id}_{\mu(x)}=x\,.

Similarly, by (GrM1), (
190
), and (GrM3), for every
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in{\mathbb{B}}
,

(46)

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathbb{B}}\bigl(\varrho_{\mathbb{B}}(x,\beta_{1}),\beta_{2}\bigr)\equiv\bigl(x\blacktriangleleft\beta_{1}^{-1}\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)^{-1}\bigr)\blacktriangleleft\beta_{2}^{-1}\bigl(\mu\bigl(x\blacktriangleleft\beta_{1}^{-1}\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)^{-1}\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigl(x\blacktriangleleft\beta_{1}^{-1}\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)^{-1}\bigr)\blacktriangleleft\beta_{2}^{-1}\bigl(s\bigl(\beta_{1}^{-1}\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)^{-1}\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}=\bigl(x\blacktriangleleft\beta_{1}^{-1}\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)^{-1}\bigr)\blacktriangleleft\beta_{2}^{-1}\bigl(\bigl(t_{*}\beta_{1}^{-1}\bigr)\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}

(48)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x\blacktriangleleft\bigl(\beta_{2}^{-1}\bigl(\bigl(t_{*}\beta_{1}^{-1}\bigr)\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)\bigr).\beta_{1}^{-1}\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}\equiv x\blacktriangleleft\bigl(\beta_{2}^{-1}\cdot\beta_{1}^{-1}\bigr)\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)^{-1}=x\blacktriangleleft(\beta_{1}\cdot\beta_{2})^{-1}\bigl(\mu(x)\bigr)^{-1}

(50)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\varrho_{\mathbb{B}}\bigl(x,\beta_{1}\cdot\beta_{2}\bigr)\,.

∎

Remark 2.21
.

One may also view the statement of Prop.
2.20
in a more geometric manner: The induced action
\varrho_{\mathbb{B}}
can be realised through restriction of
\varrho
to submanifolds in
\mathscr{G}
obtained by evaluation of bisections on
M
. Specialising this to the principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
, we obtain the following diagrammatic representation of the induced action:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 27.95067pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 55.29022pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.00833pt\raise 5.00694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathscr{P}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.67215pt\raise-25.14511pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{D}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.69301pt\raise-43.01247pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower-3.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 83.1206pt\raise-20.7175pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 98.35074pt\raise-43.01247pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 89.43535pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\beta(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 119.66106pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 141.00061pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 141.00061pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.75732pt\raise-0.65305pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.75732pt\raise 0.65305pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 110.82385pt\raise-43.01247pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.75732pt\raise-0.65305pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.75732pt\raise 0.65305pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.75732pt\raise-0.65305pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.75732pt\raise 0.65305pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 27.95067pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 55.42911pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.09512pt\raise-55.29718pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathscr{F}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 96.15237pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.0138pt\raise-25.14513pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\beta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 104.5482pt\raise-8.05554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 144.92422pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

For later purposes, it will be useful to,
vice versa
, implement the
\mathscr{G}
-action by the
{\mathbb{B}}
-action on
\mathscr{P}
. This is not always possible, since, in general, not every
g\in\mathscr{G}
admits a global bisection passing through it. Thus, we stumble upon the condition of
{\rm Id}
-reducibility once again.

Corollary 2.22
.

For every principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
with a bisection-complete structure groupoid, the following identity holds true for arbitrary
(p,g)\in\mathscr{P}{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}

\displaystyle\varrho(p,g)=p\vartriangleleft\beta^{-1}_{g^{-1}}\,,

where
\beta_{g}\in{\mathbb{B}}
is any bisection through
g
, as specified in Def.
2.11
.

2.3.
Examples

Example 2.23
.

If
\mathscr{G}={\rm G}
, a Lie group, then
{\mathbb{B}}\cong{\rm G}
and a principaloid bundle reduces to a standard principal
{\rm G}
-bundle. Similarly, as
\mathscr{F}\cong\Sigma
, so does the induced principal groupoid bundle (
33
). Hence, in this special case, the two notions coincide.

Example 2.24
.

If
\mathscr{G}={\rm Pair}(M)
, then
{\mathbb{B}}\cong{\rm Diff}(M)
and a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}\cong\mathscr{F}\times M
, where
\mathscr{F}
is a generic fibre bundle with typical fibre
M
and no restriction on the structure group. Here, the sitting-duck map
\mathscr{D}={\rm pr}_{1}
and the moment map
\mu={\rm pr}_{2}
. The groupoid
{\rm Pair}(M)
acts just on the second factor
M
and does not affect
\mathscr{F}
. So, in this case, we recover general fibre bundles
\mathscr{F}
.

Principaloid bundles are particular fibre bundles, where the fibre is
\mathscr{G}
and the structure group is reduced to
{\mathbb{B}}={\rm Bisec}(\mathscr{G})
. At the same time, and as the previous example shows, general fibre bundles
\mathscr{F}
, with fibre
M
and no restriction on the structure group
{\rm Diff}(M)
, can be described as particular principaloid bundles. This may be compared to the relation between Poisson manifolds
(M,\Pi
) and Lie algebroids
E\to M
: While the former induce particular Lie algebroids on
E={\rm T}^{*}M
, the latter are particular Poisson manifolds, where
E^{*}
carries a canonical fibre-linear Poisson structure.

Example 2.25
.

Let
\mathscr{G}={\rm G}\times M
, the action groupoid associated with an action
\lambda
of a Lie group
{\rm G}
on a manifold
M
. In this case, there exists an embedding of the group
G
into the group
{\mathbb{B}}
of bisections of
\mathscr{G}
, given by
\iota\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm G}\ \to{\mathbb{B}},\,g\mapsto\beta_{g}
, where
\beta_{g}(m)=(g,m)
. Let
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}:=\iota(G)
and
\mathscr{P}_{0}
be a principaloid bundle in which the structure group is restricted/reduced to
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}\subset{\mathbb{B}}
.

Then,
\mathscr{P}_{0}\cong{\mathsf{P}}\times M
, where
{\mathsf{P}}
is a principal
{\rm G}
-bundle with the transition mappings
\beta_{ij}\hskip-2.0pt:O_{ij}\to{\rm G}
induced from the transition 1-cocycle with values in
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}\cong{\rm G}
. The induced shadow bundle
\mathscr{F}_{0}\cong{\mathsf{P}}\times_{\lambda}M
is just the associated bundle obtained from
P
by the action
\lambda
, with quotient map
\mathscr{D}\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathsf{P}}\times M\to{\mathsf{P}}\times_{\lambda}M
. Note that here still
all
of
{\mathbb{B}}
acts on
\mathscr{P}_{0}={\mathsf{P}}\times M
and this action does not factorise into separate actions on
{\mathsf{P}}
and
M
, except for the subgroup
{\mathbb{B}}_{0}
. The moment map
\mu_{0}={\rm pr}_{2}
intertwines the
{\mathbb{B}}
-action on
\mathscr{P}_{0}
and
M
.

So, in the above fashion, we recover standard principal
G
-bundles
P\to\Sigma
together with associated ones in the form of couples
(\mathscr{P}_{0},\mathscr{F}_{0})
.

Example 2.26
.

For a symplectic groupoid of Example
B.6
, with a symplectic arrow manifold
(\mathscr{G},\omega)
and a Poisson object manifold
(M,\Pi)
, it is customary to
restrict
the choice of bisections to those
\beta\in{\rm Bisec}(\mathscr{G})
—termed
lagrangean
—for which the corresponding submanifolds
\beta(M)\subset\mathscr{G}
are lagrangean in
(\mathscr{G},\omega)
, see
[
CDW87
, Chap. II]
. These form a Fréchet–Lie subgroup
{\rm Bisec}_{\rm lagr}(\mathscr{G},\omega)
within
{\rm Bisec}(\mathscr{G})
, which is regular in the sense of Kriegl and Michor, see
[
Ry01
]
. The corresponding left- and right-multiplications of
(\mathscr{G},\omega)
by
{\rm Bisec}_{\rm lagr}(\mathscr{G},\omega)\subset{\rm Bisec}(\mathscr{G})
are symplectic, see
[
CDW87
, Prop. 1.2, Chap. II]
, and project to Poisson actions on
(M,\Pi)
along both: the source and target maps,
[
CDW87
, p. 22]
. We shall refer to principaloid bundles with the structure group reduced to
{\rm Bisec}_{\rm lagr}(\mathscr{G},\omega)
as
symplectic principaloid
(\mathscr{G},\omega)
-bundles
in future studies. In some cases, it may be natural to further restrict the structure group, so as to obtain, e.g., hamiltonian
{\mathbb{B}}
-actions on
(\mathscr{G},\omega)
or on
(M,\Pi)
.

We conclude the present section by studying an example of two manifestly inequivalent
\mathscr{G}
-structures induced on a preprincipaloid bundle with a bisection-complete structure groupoid by representatives of two different equivariance classes of reductions. By Prop.
2.20
, these give rise to two different
{\mathbb{B}}
-structures on that bundle.

Example 2.27
.

Consider a trivial preprincipaloid bundle
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}=\Sigma\times\mathscr{G}
over base
\Sigma
with typical fibre
\mathscr{G}={\rm SO}(3)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
given by the arrow manifold of the action groupoid
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 26.9792pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.9792pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm SO}(3)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.20557pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.20557pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.20557pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
associated with the defining representation of
{\rm SO}(3)
. This trivial bundle admits a family of trivialisations

\displaystyle\tau_{\vec{d}}\hskip-2.0pt:\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}\longrightarrow\Sigma\times\mathscr{G},\ \bigl(\sigma,(R,\vec{r})\bigr)\longmapsto\bigl(\sigma,(R,\vec{r}+\vec{d})\bigr)

indexed by vectors
\vec{d}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
. Each of these trivialisations comes with a
\mathscr{G}
-action—and so also a
{\mathbb{B}}
-action—on
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
of the following form: For every
(\sigma,(R,\vec{r}))\in\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
and
M\in{\rm SO}(3)
,

(52)

\displaystyle\varrho_{\vec{d}}\bigl(\bigl(\sigma,(R,\vec{r})\bigr),\bigl(M,M^{-1}\bigl(\vec{r}+\vec{d}\bigr)\bigr)\bigr):=\tau_{\vec{d}}^{-1}\circ({\rm id}_{\Sigma}\times r_{(M,M^{-1}(\vec{r}+\vec{d}))})\circ\tau_{\vec{d}}\bigl(\sigma,(R,\vec{r})\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{\vec{d}}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,\bigl(R,\vec{r}+\vec{d}\bigr).\bigl(M,M^{-1}\bigl(\vec{r}+\vec{d}\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)=\tau_{\vec{d}}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,\bigl(R\cdot M,M^{-1}\bigl(\vec{r}+\vec{d}\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)=\bigl(\sigma,\bigl(R\cdot M,M^{-1}\bigl(\vec{r}+\vec{d}\bigr)-\vec{d}\bigr)\bigr)\,.

The
\mathscr{G}
-orbits of
(\sigma,({\boldsymbol{1}},\vec{0}))\in\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
under
\varrho_{\vec{d}}\,
take the form:

\displaystyle\varrho_{\vec{d}}\bigl(\bigl(\sigma,({\boldsymbol{1}},\vec{0})\bigr),\mathscr{G}\bigr)=\bigl\{\ \bigl(\sigma,\bigl(M,M^{-1}\vec{d}-\vec{d}\bigr)\bigr)\ |\ M\in{\rm SO}(3)\ \bigr\}\,.

Thus, for any two distinct choices of
\vec{d}
, we obtain different
\mathscr{G}
-orbits. Consider, in particular, the case
\vec{d}=0
.

Each such choice gives rise to a different structure of a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}_{\vec{d}}\,
on
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}
. Indeed, for
\mathscr{P}_{\vec{d}{}_{1}}
to be equivalent to
\mathscr{P}_{\vec{d}{}_{2}}\,
for arbitrary
\vec{d}{}_{2}\neq\vec{d}{}_{1}
, we would need to be able to have a common refinement of the respective trivialisations such that the transition maps between them would lie in
L({\mathbb{B}})
. In the case at hand, bisections are of the form

\displaystyle\beta\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\longrightarrow{\rm SO}(3)\times{\mathbb{R}}^{3},\ \vec{r}\longmapsto\bigl(\widetilde{M}\bigl(\vec{r}\bigr),\vec{r}\bigr)

for some
\widetilde{M}\in C^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3},{\rm SO}(3))
. A simple calculation yields the following expression for the left-multiplication of
(R,\vec{r})\in\mathscr{G}\cong\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}{}_{\sigma}
with
\sigma\in\Sigma
by the above
\beta\in{\mathbb{B}}
:

(53)

\displaystyle L_{\beta}\bigl(R,\vec{r}\bigr)=\bigl(\widetilde{M}\bigl(R\vec{r}\bigr)\cdot R,\vec{r}\bigr)\,.

On the other hand, the transition map between
\tau_{\vec{d}{}_{1}}
and
\tau_{\vec{d}{}_{2}}\,
is given by the formula

\displaystyle\bigl(\tau_{\vec{d}{}_{1}}\circ\tau_{\vec{d}{}_{2}}^{-1}\bigr)\bigl(\sigma,(R,\vec{r})\bigr)=\bigl(\sigma,(R,\vec{r}+\vec{d}{}_{1}-\vec{d}{}_{2})\bigr)\neq\bigl(\sigma,(R,\vec{r}\bigr)\bigr)\,,

which is manifestly not of the form (
53
).

3.
Connections

3.1.
Connections on principaloid bundles

Definition 3.1
.

A (compatible)
connection
on a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
is an Ehresmann connection
{\rm T}\mathscr{P}={\rm V}\mathscr{P}\oplus{\rm H}\mathscr{P}
with a
\mathscr{G}
-invariant horizontal distribution
{\rm H}\mathscr{P}\subset{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}\mu
. In other words, for every
g\in\mathscr{G}
and
p\in\mu^{-1}(\{t(g)\})
, the horizontal distribution satisfies

(54)

\displaystyle{\rm T}\varrho_{g}({\rm H}_{p}\mathscr{P})={\rm H}_{\varrho_{g}(p)}\mathscr{P}\,,

where
\varrho_{g}(p)\equiv\varrho(p,g)\equiv p\blacktriangleleft g
.

Remark 3.2
.

The restriction
{\rm H}\mathscr{P}\subset{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}\mu
is a prerequisite for the imposition of the condition of
\mathscr{G}
-invariance as the
\mathscr{G}
-action is
\mu
-fibred: An element
g\in\mathscr{G}
acts only on
\mu^{-1}(\{t(g))\}
. Furthermore, as the momentum
\mu
is modelled on
s
in a local trivialisation
\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}
, the kernel
{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}\mu
becomes
{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}O_{i}\oplus{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s
in that trivialisation, which makes the restriction meaningful from the point of view of the definition of a horizontal distribution.

As usual, we may replace the notion of a compatible (Ehresmann) connection with that of a smooth distribution of projectors over
\mathscr{P}
.

Definition 3.3
.

A (compatible)
connection 1-form
on
\mathscr{P}
is a
{\rm V}\mathscr{P}
-valued 1-form
\Theta\in\Omega^{1}(\mathscr{P},{\rm V}\mathscr{P})\equiv\Gamma({\rm T}^{*}\mathscr{P}\otimes{\rm V}\mathscr{P})

such that, when viewed as an element in
{\rm End}{({\rm T}\mathscr{P})}
:

(GC1)

\Theta\big|_{{\rm V}\mathscr{P}}={\rm id}_{{\rm V}\mathscr{P}}
(a projection onto
{\rm V}\mathscr{P}
);

(GC2)

{\rm T}\mu\circ\Theta={\rm T}\mu
(kernel inclusion);

(GC3)

\forall\ g\in\mathscr{G}\,,\ p\in\mu^{-1}(\{t(g)\})\hskip-2.0pt:\ {\rm T}_{p}\varrho_{g}({\rm ker}\,\Theta_{p})={\rm ker}\,\Theta_{\varrho_{g}(p)}
(
\mathscr{G}
-invariance).

The equivalence of the two definitions is established in the following proposition.

Proposition 3.4
.

Connections on a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
are in one-to-one correspondence with connection 1-forms on
\mathscr{P}
.

Proof.

The choice of a decomposition
{\rm T}\mathscr{P}={\rm V}\mathscr{P}\oplus{\rm H}\mathscr{P}
determines a 1-form
\Theta
by declaring the latter form to vanish on the horizontal
subbundle and to be the identity on the vertical one, which in particular implies (GC1) by construction. Conversely, given
\Theta
, property (GC1) determines a decomposition with
{\rm H}\mathscr{P}:={\rm ker}\,\Theta
. With this identification, condition (GC2) becomes equivalent to the relation
{\rm H}\mathscr{P}\subset{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}\mu
, and (GC3) is a restatement of the
\mathscr{G}
-invariance of the horizontal distribution.
∎

Example 3.5
.

If
\mathscr{G}={\rm G}
, then a connection on the principal
{\rm G}
-bundle
\mathscr{P}\equiv{\rm P}
is a standard principal
{\rm G}
-connection. In addition, since canonically
{\rm V}{\rm P}\cong{\rm P}\times\mathfrak{g}
, the corresponding connection 1-form
\Theta\in\Gamma({\rm T}^{*}{\rm P}\otimes{\rm V}{\rm P})=\Omega^{1}({\rm P})\otimes\mathfrak{g}
can be represented as a Lie algebra-valued 1-form in this case.

Example 3.6
.

If
\mathscr{G}={\rm Pair}(M)
, then the principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
can be identified with
\mathscr{F}\times_{\Sigma}\mathscr{M}
, where
\mathscr{M}\equiv\Sigma\times M
. A connection on
\mathscr{P}\cong\mathscr{F}\times_{\Sigma}\mathscr{M}
corresponds uniquely to a pair composed of a connection on
\mathscr{F}
and one on
\mathscr{M}
. As
\mu
is the canonical projection to the factor
M
in
\mathscr{M}
, the horizontal subbundle is constrained to lie within
{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}\mathscr{F}\oplus{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\widetilde{\rm pr}{}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}\Sigma
, where
{\rm pr}_{1}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{F}\times_{\Sigma}\mathscr{M}\to\mathscr{F}
,
{\rm pr}_{2}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{F}\times_{\Sigma}\mathscr{M}\to\mathscr{M}
, and
\widetilde{\rm pr}{}_{1}\hskip-2.0pt:\Sigma\times M\to\Sigma
denote the respective canonical projections. Note that the right action of
\mathscr{G}=M\times M
is trivial on
\mathscr{F}
, and thus the connection there is unrestricted by the condition of
\mathscr{G}
-invariance. On
\mathscr{M}=\Sigma\times M
,
\mathscr{G}
acts non-trivially only on the second factor
M
—by arbitrary replacements of points. Hence, the entire subbundle
{\rm H}_{\mathrm{can}}\mathscr{M}:=\widetilde{\rm pr}{}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}\Sigma\oplus 0
is preserved by the
\mathscr{G}
-action, and this corresponds to the canonical flat connection on
\mathscr{M}
.

In the equivalent identification
\mathscr{P}\cong\mathscr{F}\times M
, one has
{\rm V}\mathscr{P}\cong{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm V}\mathscr{F}\oplus{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm T}M
, and, according to what we established above, the connection 1-form splits as
\Theta={\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}+{\rm id}_{{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm T}M}
, with the trivial component on the factor
M
and an arbitrary connection 1-form
\mathcal{A}\in\Omega^{1}(\mathscr{F},{\rm V}\mathscr{F})
on the factor
\mathscr{F}
.

We thus find, when choosing
\mathscr{G}
to be the pair groupoid, that there is a one-to-one correspondence between principaloid bundles with connection
(\mathscr{P},{\rm H}\mathscr{P})
as in Def.
3.1
and ordinary fibre bundles with Ehresmann connection
(\mathscr{F},{\rm H}\mathscr{F})
.

Since the shadow bundle
\mathscr{F}
can be viewed as the quotient of
\mathscr{P}
by the
\mathscr{G}
-action, see Remark
2.18
, a connection on
\mathscr{P}
, being
\mathscr{G}
-invariant, descends to one on
\mathscr{F}
along the quotient map
\mathscr{D}
.

Proposition/Definition 3.7
.

A connection on a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
canonically induces an Ehresmann connection on
\mathscr{F}
along the sitting-duck map
\mathscr{D}
as

(55)

\displaystyle{\rm T}\mathscr{F}={\rm V}\mathscr{F}\oplus{\rm H}\mathscr{F}\,,\qquad\qquad{\rm V}\mathscr{F}={\rm T}\mathscr{D}({\rm V}\mathscr{P})\,,\qquad{\rm H}\mathscr{F}={\rm T}\mathscr{D}({\rm H}\mathscr{P})\,.

We shall call it the
shadow connection
on
\mathscr{F}
.

Proof.

Let
\,{\rm H}\mathscr{P}\,
be the horizontal distribution over
\mathscr{P}
. Using the sitting-duck map
\mathscr{D}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{F}
, we define, over an arbitrary point
\mathscr{D}(p)\in\mathscr{F}
, a subspace

\displaystyle{\rm H}_{\mathscr{D}(p)}\mathscr{F}:={\rm T}_{p}\mathscr{D}\bigl({\rm H}_{p}\mathscr{P}\bigr)\subset{\rm T}_{\mathscr{D}(p)}\mathscr{F}\,.

We readily check that the right-hand side does not depend on the choice of the representative
p
in the
\mathscr{D}
-fibre over
\mathscr{D}(p)
. Indeed, in virtue of Thm.
2.16
,
\mathscr{G}
acts transitively on the
\mathscr{D}
-fibre, which ensures that for any
q\in\mathscr{D}^{-1}(\{\mathscr{D}(p)\})
, we always find (a unique)
g\in t^{-1}(\{\mu(p)\})
such that
q=\varrho_{g}(p)
, and so—by the assumption of
\mathscr{G}
-invariance of the horizontal distribution—

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{q}\mathscr{D}\bigl({\rm H}_{q}\mathscr{P}\bigr)\equiv{\rm T}_{\varrho_{g}(p)}\mathscr{D}\bigl({\rm H}_{\varrho_{g}(p)}\mathscr{P}\bigr)=\bigl({\rm T}_{\varrho_{g}(p)}\mathscr{D}\circ{\rm T}_{p}\varrho_{g}\bigr)({\rm H}_{p}\mathscr{P})={\rm T}_{p}(\mathscr{D}\circ\varrho_{g})({\rm H}_{p}\mathscr{P})={\rm T}_{p}\mathscr{D}({\rm H}_{p}\mathscr{P})\,.

Here, the last equality is implied by the identity
\mathscr{D}\circ\varrho_{g}\big|_{\mu^{-1}(\{t(g)\})}=\mathscr{D}\big|_{\mu^{-1}(\{t(g)\})}
, true for every
g\in\mathscr{G}
, which, in turn, follows from the corresponding model identity
t\circ r_{g}\big|_{s^{-1}(\{t(g)\})}=t\big|_{s^{-1}(\{t(g)\})}
.

At this stage, it remains to verify that at every point
f=\mathscr{D}(p)\in\mathscr{F}
as above, we have

(56)

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{f}\mathscr{F}={\rm V}_{f}\mathscr{F}\oplus{\rm H}_{f}\mathscr{F}\,.

Being modelled on the surjective submersion
t
, both the sitting-duck map
\mathscr{D}\colon\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{F}
and its restriction
\mathscr{D}\big|\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}_{\sigma}\to\mathscr{F}_{\sigma}
to a fibre over every point
\sigma\in\Sigma
are also surjective submersions. Consequently,
{\rm T}_{p}\mathscr{D}({\rm T}_{p}\mathscr{P})={\rm T}_{f}\mathscr{F}
and
{\rm T}_{p}\mathscr{D}({\rm V}_{p}\mathscr{P})={\rm V}_{f}\mathscr{F}
. Thus, by the Rank Theorem, (
56
) follows. Altogether, then, by the Constant-Rank Theorem, we obtain an induced Ehresmann connection

{\rm T}\mathscr{F}={\rm V}\mathscr{F}\oplus{\rm H}\mathscr{F}

with the vector subbundle

(57)

\displaystyle{\rm H}\mathscr{F}={\rm T}\mathscr{D}({\rm H}\mathscr{P})\,.

∎

Definition 3.8
.

Let
\mathscr{P}
be a principaloid bundle. Given a connection
{\rm T}\mathscr{P}={\rm V}\mathscr{P}\oplus{\rm H}\mathscr{P}
on
\mathscr{P}
and the corresponding shadow connection
{\rm T}\mathscr{F}={\rm V}\mathscr{F}\oplus{\rm T}\mathscr{D}({\rm H}\mathscr{P})
on
\mathscr{F}
, the
shadow connection 1-form
on
\mathscr{F}
is a
{\rm V}\mathscr{F}
-valued 1-form
\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}\in\Omega^{1}(\mathscr{F},{\rm V}\mathscr{F})\equiv\Gamma({\rm T}^{*}\mathscr{F}\otimes{\rm V}\mathscr{F})
with the property
{\rm T}\mathscr{D}({\rm H}\mathscr{P})={\rm ker}\,\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}
.

Proposition 3.9
.

The shadow connection 1-form is uniquely determined by the identity

(58)

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}\circ{\rm T}\mathscr{D}={\rm T}\mathscr{D}\circ\Theta\,.

Proof.

The identity follows straightforwardly from (
55
). Uniqueness of the 1-form thus defined is ensured by the surjectivity of
{\rm T}\mathscr{D}
, itself a consequence of the surjective submersivity of
\mathscr{D}
.
∎

Example 3.10
.

A connection on
\mathscr{P}_{0}
of Example
2.25
is in a one-to-one correspondence with a connection on the principal
G
-bundle
{\mathsf{P}}
. The induced connection on
\mathscr{F}_{0}\cong{\mathsf{P}}\times_{\lambda}M
is the same as the one obtained from the standard procedure for associated bundles, see, e.g.,
[
Kob63
]
.

Remark 3.11
.

In standard principal
{\rm G}
-bundles
{\mathsf{P}}
, it is customary to describe connections by Lie algebra-valued connection 1-forms,
\omega\in\Omega^{1}({\mathsf{P}},\mathfrak{g})
. This is possible due to the freeness of the
{\rm G}
-action on
{\mathsf{P}}
, leading to the global identification
{\rm V}\mathscr{P}\cong{\mathsf{P}}\times\mathfrak{g}
. For a general principaloid bundle, an analogous identification with the Lie algebroid
E=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathscr{G})
is not available: For
{\rm dim}\,M>0
, the fundamental distribution of the
\mathscr{G}
-action on
\mathscr{P}
is a proper subbundle of
\,{\rm V}\mathscr{P}
, whose rank equals
{\rm dim}\,\mathscr{G}-{\rm dim}\,M
.

However, we recover
\Gamma(E)
-valuedness for certain local 1-forms on the base, see, in particular, formula (
62
) below.

3.2.
Local description

There is also a definition of compatible connection 1-forms on principaloid bundles using local data:

Definition 3.12
.

Local connection data
on a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
over
\Sigma
are an assignment of a
{\rm T}O_{i}
-foliated
{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}E
-valued 1-form
{\rm A}_{i}
on
O_{i}\times M
,
{\rm A}_{i}\in\Gamma({\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}^{*}O_{i}\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}E)
to the trivialising cover
\mathcal{O}\equiv\{O_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
\,\Sigma
. The 1-forms are subject to the following
gluing law
over double intersections
O_{ij}\equiv O_{i}\cap O_{j}\ni\sigma
:

(59)

\displaystyle{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m\bigr)={\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{m}}C_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)}\circ{\rm A}_{j}(\sigma,m)-\theta_{\rm R}\circ{\rm T}_{\sigma}({\rm ev}_{m}\circ\beta_{ij})\,.

Here,
\{\beta_{ij}\}_{(i,j)\in I^{\times 2}_{\mathcal{O}}}
is the transition 1-cocycle of
\mathscr{P}
associated with
\mathcal{O}
, and the pullback of the right-invariant Maurer–Cartan 1-form
\theta_{\rm R}
on
\mathscr{G}
is written in terms of the
evaluation map

{\rm ev}_{m}\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathbb{B}}\to\mathscr{G},\ \beta\mapsto\beta(m)
at
m\in M
.

Remark 3.13
.

For standard principal
{\rm G}
-bundles, with
{\rm G}
a matrix Lie group, the relation between two local connection 1-forms
A
and
A^{\prime}
restricted to a common domain
O
,
A,A^{\prime}\in\Omega^{1}(O,\mathfrak{g})
, takes the well-known form

(60)

\displaystyle A^{\prime}=g\,A\,g^{-1}-\,{\rm d}g\,g^{-1}\,\equiv\,{\rm Ad}_{g}A-g^{*}\theta_{\rm R}\,,

where
g\hskip-2.0pt:O\to{\rm G}\,
and
\theta_{\rm R}
is the right-invariant Maurer-Cartan form on
{\rm G}
. To recover a similar formula from Eq. (
59
), let us define
A_{\sigma}:={\rm A}_{j}(\sigma,\cdot)\in{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}_{\sigma}^{*}O\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\Gamma\left(({\rm pr}_{2}^{*}E)_{(\sigma,\cdot)}\right)\cong{\rm T}^{*}_{\sigma}O\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\Gamma(E)
, where
O:=O_{ij}
and
{\rm pr}_{1}
and
{\rm pr}_{2}
refer to the factors in
O\times M
; and, likewise,
A^{\prime}_{\sigma}:={\rm A}_{i}(\sigma,\cdot)\in{\rm T}^{*}_{\sigma}O\otimes_{\mathbf{R}}\Gamma(E)
. Note that for
M
compact,
\Gamma(E)
can be identified with the Lie algebra of
{\mathbb{B}}

[
SWo20
]
. Then, for
\beta(\sigma):=\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\in{\mathbb{B}}
, Eq. (
59
) takes the form

(61)

\displaystyle A_{\sigma}^{\prime}={\rm Ad}_{\beta(\sigma)}A_{\sigma}-{\rm T}_{\beta(\sigma)}r_{\beta(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{\sigma}\beta\,,

where the last term can be symbolically rewritten as “
{\rm d}\beta\,\beta^{-1}
”, the pullback by
\beta
of the right-invariant (
{\rm Lie}({\mathbb{B}})
-valued) Maurer-Cartan form on the infinite dimensional Lie group
{\mathbb{B}}
, evaluated at
\sigma
. In fact, if we contract the latter formula with a vector
v\in{\rm T}_{\sigma}O
and write

(62)

\displaystyle A(v):=\langle A_{\sigma},v\rangle\;\in\;\Gamma(E),

then, both formulas, (
60
) and
(
61
)
, take the
same
form

(63)

\displaystyle A^{\prime}(v)={\rm Ad}_{\beta(\sigma)}\>A(v)-{\rm T}_{\beta(\sigma)}r_{\beta(\sigma)^{-1}}\left({\rm T}_{\sigma}\beta(v)\right)\,,

since for
\mathscr{G}={\rm G}
, one has
\beta(\sigma)\equiv g(\sigma)
and
\Gamma(E)\cong\mathfrak{g}
. Note that, for a general Lie groupoid,
A(v)
is still to be evaluated at
m\in M
, whereupon
A(v)|_{m}\in E_{m}
.

While the above rewriting brings Eq. (
59
) into a form more familiar from the standard setting of local connection 1-forms on principal
G
-bundles, the original version avoids all infinite dimensionality of
{\mathbb{B}}
and the Lie algebra of that Lie group altogether, and is thus preferable in our context.

Remark 3.14
.

The appearance of the right-invariant Maurer-Cartan form on
\mathscr{G}
in the gluing law (
59
) calls for some care. Since the differential form
\theta_{\rm R}
is foliated, see Example
D.8
in the appendices, it only takes arguments from the distribution
{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s\subset{\rm T}\mathscr{G}
. Hence, it is necessary that the linear operator
{\rm T}_{\sigma}({\rm ev}_{m}\circ\beta_{ij})
map an arbitrary vector
v\in{\rm T}_{\sigma}\Sigma
into that distribution. That this is the case follows from the fact that the evaluation map
{\rm ev}_{m}
fixes the source of all the arrows in
\{({\rm ev}_{m}\circ\beta_{ij})(\sigma)\}_{\sigma\in O_{ij}}
to be
m
, so that, accordingly,
{\rm T}_{\sigma}({\rm ev}_{m}\circ\beta_{ij})(v)\in({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s)_{(\beta_{ij}(\sigma))(m)}
.

Equivalence of Definitions
3.3
and
3.12
is stated in

Theorem 3.15
.

For every principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
