, a connection 1-form determines local connection data, and vice versa. The relation between the two is given by the formula

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1\,*}\Theta\bigr)(\sigma,g)={\rm id}_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}\big|_{g}+{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(g)}}r_{g}\circ{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t(g)\bigr)\,,\qquad(\sigma,g)\in O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}\,.

Proof.

Consider local trivialisations
\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\mathscr{P}}^{-1}(O_{i})\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}
over an open cover
\{O_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of the base
\Sigma
of
\mathscr{P}
, and the associated transition 1-cocycle
\{\beta_{ij}\in C^{\infty}(O_{ij},{\mathbb{B}})\}
. Upon pullback
6
6

6

Recall that pullbacks along diffeomorphisms can be defined for arbitrary tensors, and not just differential forms, see,
e.g.
,
[
Th78
]
. For example, for a 1-1 tensor
\tau
and
\phi\in\mathrm{Diff}(M)
, one has:
(\phi^{*}\tau)(v,\alpha)=\tau({\rm T}_{m}\phi(v),\alpha\circ{\rm T}_{\phi(m)}\phi^{-1})
for every
v\in{\rm T}_{m}M
and
\alpha\in{\rm T}_{m}^{*}M\equiv({\rm T}_{m}M)^{*}
.
of
\Theta
along
\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}
, we obtain
\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1\,*}\Theta\in\Gamma({\rm T}^{*}(O_{i}\times\mathscr{G})\otimes{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm T}\mathscr{G})
. Taking into account axiom (GC1), we can now write the pullback of
\Theta
along
\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}
as

(64)

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1\,*}\Theta\bigr)(\sigma,g)={\rm id}_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}\big|_{{\rm T}_{g}\mathscr{G}}+\Gamma_{i}(\sigma,g)

in terms of a vector-valued 1-form
\Gamma_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}O_{i}\to{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm T}\mathscr{G}
, where we use the identifications
{\rm T}(O_{i}\times\mathscr{G})\cong{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\,{\rm T}O_{i}\oplus{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\,{\rm T}\mathscr{G}
and
{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\,{\rm T}\mathscr{G}|_{(\sigma,g)}\cong{\rm T}_{g}\mathscr{G}
. Likewise, the notation for the two terms in the sum (
64
) is adapted to the identification
{\rm T}^{*}(O_{i}\times\mathscr{G})\cong{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}\,{\rm T}^{*}O_{i}\oplus{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}\,{\rm T}^{*}\mathscr{G}
. Using local coordinates
\{\sigma^{\mu}\}^{\mu\in\overline{1,{\rm dim}\,\Sigma}}
on a neighbourhood of
\sigma\in O_{i}
, we write

\displaystyle\Gamma_{i}(\sigma,\cdot)={\rm d}\sigma^{\mu}(\sigma)\otimes\Gamma^{\sigma}_{i\,\mu}(\cdot)\,,

where
\Gamma^{\sigma}_{i\,\mu}\in\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})
.

Lemma 3.16
.

The vector fields
\Gamma^{\sigma}_{i\,\mu}
lie in the distribution
{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s
.

Proof of Lemma:

In virtue of Prop.
3.26
, the pullback (
64
) determines the local presentation of the horizontal subspace over
O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}
as the kernel of the 1-form
{\rm id}_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}+\Gamma_{i}
. This implies that the component of every horizontal vector field along the fibre is determined by the unconstrained component along the base and lies in the image of
\Gamma_{i}
. But the field has to belong to
{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}\mu
, which becomes
{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}O_{i}\oplus{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s
in the local model as the momentum
\mu
is modelled on the source map
s
.

\blacksquare

Lemma 3.17
.

The local presentation (
64
) can be written as

(65)

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1\,*}\Theta\bigr)(\sigma,g)={\rm id}_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}\big|_{g}+{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(g)}}r_{g}\circ{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t(g)\bigr)

in terms of a locally smooth
E
-valued 1-form

(66)

\displaystyle{\rm A}_{i}(\sigma,m)\in{\rm T}^{*}_{\sigma}O_{i}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}E_{m}\,.

Proof of Lemma:

In the light of the reasoning given in the proof of the previous lemma, the
\mathscr{G}
-invariance of the horizontal distribution translates into the same property of the
\Gamma^{\sigma}_{i\,\mu}\in\Gamma({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s)
, that is
\Gamma^{\sigma}_{i\,\mu}\in\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})_{\rm R}
, see Example
D.3
. Hence, for any
g\in\mathscr{G}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{i\,\mu}^{\sigma}(g)=\Gamma_{i\,\mu}^{\sigma}\bigl(r_{g}\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr)\bigr)=T_{{\rm Id}_{t(g)}}r_{g}\bigl(\Gamma_{i\,\mu}^{\sigma}\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr)\bigr)\,,

and so

\displaystyle\Gamma_{i}(\sigma,g)={\rm d}\sigma^{\mu}(\sigma)\otimes\Gamma^{\sigma}_{i\,\mu}(g)=T_{{\rm Id}_{t(g)}}r_{g}\circ\bigl({\rm d}\sigma^{\mu}(\sigma)\otimes\Gamma_{i\,\mu}^{\sigma}\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr)\bigr)\,.

Altogether, then, we obtain

\displaystyle{\rm A}_{i}(\sigma,m)={\rm d}\sigma^{\mu}(\sigma)\otimes\Gamma_{i\,\mu}^{\sigma}\bigl({\rm Id}_{m}\bigr)\,.

\blacksquare

We shall next investigate the gluing laws for the local 1-forms
{\rm A}_{i}
over the intersections
O_{ij}
. Clearly, these are determined by the behaviour of
{\rm id}_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}
— viewed, as before, as an endomorphism of
{\rm T}(O_{ij}\times\mathscr{G})
— under the fibre identifications

\displaystyle\gamma{}_{ij}\hskip-2.0pt:O_{ij}\times\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow O_{ij}\times\mathscr{G},\ (\sigma,g,j)\longmapsto\bigl(\sigma,\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g),i\bigr)\,,

written in terms of the smooth maps

(67)

\displaystyle\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}\hskip-2.0pt:O_{ij}\times\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G},\ (\sigma,g)\longmapsto\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g\equiv r_{g}\bigl(\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\bigl(t(g)\bigr)\bigr)\,.

These are the identifications that underlie the model (
10
) of the principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}\circ\gamma_{ij}\big|_{O_{ij}\times\mathscr{G}}=\mathscr{P}\tau_{j}^{-1}\big|_{O_{ij}\times\mathscr{G}}\,.

Let us fix coordinates
\{\zeta^{\alpha}\}^{\alpha\in\overline{1,{\rm dim}\,\mathscr{G}}}
on a neighbourhood
\mathscr{U}
of
g
(with
\partial_{\alpha}\equiv\frac{\partial\ }{\partial\zeta^{\alpha}}
) and coordinates
\{\widetilde{\zeta}{}^{\alpha}\}^{\alpha\in\overline{1,{\rm dim}\,\mathscr{G}}}
on a neighbourhood
\widetilde{\mathscr{U}}
of
\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)
in
\mathscr{G}
(with
\widetilde{\partial}{}_{\alpha}\equiv\frac{\partial\ }{\partial\widetilde{\zeta}{}^{\alpha}}
), and compute, for
(v,V)\in{\rm T}_{\sigma}O_{ij}\oplus{\rm T}_{g}\mathscr{G}\cong{\rm T}_{(\sigma,g)}(O_{ij}\times\mathscr{G})
arbitrary, how the local presentation (
65
) pulls back along the identifications. We obtain

(69)

\displaystyle\bigl(\bigl(\gamma_{ij}^{*}\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1\,*}\Theta\bigr)(\sigma,g)\bigr)(v,V)=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ\bigl(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}^{*}{\rm d}\widetilde{\zeta}{}^{\alpha}(\sigma,g)\otimes\widetilde{\partial}{}_{\alpha}\big|_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}+{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))}}r_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}\circ{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t\bigl(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)(v,V)

(71)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)^{-1}}\bigl({\rm T}_{g}L{}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)}(V)+{\rm T}_{\sigma}\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\cdot,g)(v)+{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))}}r_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}\circ{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t\bigl(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)\bigr)\bigr)(v)\bigr)

(73)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle V+{\rm T}_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ\bigl({\rm T}_{\sigma}\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\cdot,g)+{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))}}r_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}\circ{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t\bigl(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)(v)\,.

For the right-hand side of (
65
) to come from a globally smooth object on
\mathscr{P}
, the above must be equal to the result of evaluating
(\mathscr{P}\tau_{j}^{-1\,*}\Theta)(\sigma,g)
on the same pair
(v,V)
,

\displaystyle\bigl(\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{j}^{-1\,*}\Theta\bigr)(\sigma,g)\bigr)(v,V)=V+{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(g)}}r_{g}\circ{\rm A}_{j}\bigl(\sigma,t(g)\bigr)(v)\,,

which yields the gluing law

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(g)}}r_{g}\circ{\rm A}_{j}\bigl(\sigma,t(g)\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}{\rm T}_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ\bigl({\rm T}_{\sigma}\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\cdot,g)+{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))}}r_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}\circ{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t\bigl(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\,.

Now, differentiating formula (
67
) with respect to
\sigma
(for
g
fixed), one finds

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{\sigma}\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\cdot,g)\equiv{\rm T}_{\sigma}\bigl(r_{g}\bigl(\beta_{ij}(\cdot)\bigl(t(g)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)={\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}r_{g}\circ{\rm T}_{\sigma}\bigl({\rm ev}_{t(g)}\circ\beta_{ij}\bigr)\,,

with the evaluation map
{\rm ev}_{t(g)}
smooth in the relevant sense, see (the proof of)
[
M80
, Lemma 10.15]
. Its presence in the operator
{\rm T}_{\sigma}({\rm ev}_{t(g)}\circ\beta_{ij})
ensures that the latter maps an arbitrary vector from
{\rm T}_{\sigma}O_{ij}
to the tangent
({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s)_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}\subset{\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}\mathscr{G}
of the
s
-fibre through
\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))
with
s(\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g)))=t(g)
. Hence,
{\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}r_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{\sigma}\bigl({\rm ev}_{t(g)}\circ\beta_{ij}\bigr)
maps
{\rm T}_{\sigma}O_{ij}
to
{\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}r_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))^{-1}}(({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s)_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))})=({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s)_{{\rm Id}_{t(g)}}
,
i.e.
, to the fibre of the Lie algebroid
E\equiv{\rm Id}^{*}{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s=M{}_{{\rm Id}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\pi_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}}\hskip-1.0pt{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s
over
t(g)
, in keeping with
[
SWo20
, Prop. 1.8.]
. Taking into account the chain rule

(74)

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}r_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))^{-1}}={\rm T}_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}r_{(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}r_{g}\,,

we thus arrive at the reworked form of the gluing law

(76)

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(g)}}r_{g}\circ{\rm A}_{j}\bigl(\sigma,t(g)\bigr)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))}}r_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}\circ\bigl({\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}r_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{\sigma}\bigl({\rm ev}_{t(g)}\circ\beta_{ij}\bigr)+{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t\bigl(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\,,

A straightforward calculation, using the inverse of (
74
) in the second equality,

(78)

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{g}r_{g^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))}}r_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}={\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}r_{g^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))}}r_{\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))}}r_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}\,,

subsequently enables us to rewrite the gluing law as

(80)

\displaystyle{\rm A}_{j}\bigl(\sigma,t(g)\bigr)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))}}r_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}\circ\bigl({\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}r_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{\sigma}\bigl({\rm ev}_{t(g)}\circ\beta_{ij}\bigr)+{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t\bigl(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\,.

Finally, using identity (
214
) in the form

(82)

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))}}r_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}={\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}L_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t_{*}\gamma{}_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}}R_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)}\big|_{({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s)_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))}}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t_{*}\gamma{}_{ij}(\sigma)(t(g))}}C_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)^{-1}}\big|_{{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\gamma}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g))}}}\,,

taking into account the definition of the Maurer-Cartan form, see Example
D.8
, and replacing
t(g)
by
m
, we ultimately arrive at the anticipated gluing law

(84)

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{m}}C_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)}\circ{\rm A}_{j}(\sigma,m)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t_{*}\bigl(\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\bigr)(m)\bigr)+{\rm T}_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(m)}r_{\beta_{ij}(\sigma)(m)^{-1}}\circ{\rm T}_{\sigma}\bigl({\rm ev}_{m}\circ\beta_{ij}\bigr)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t_{*}\bigl(\beta_{ij}(\sigma)\bigr)(m)\bigr)+\bigl({\rm ev}_{m}\circ\beta_{ij}\bigr)^{*}\theta_{\rm R}(\sigma)\,.

For the converse, note that the gluing law (
59
) ensures descent of local 1-forms given by the right-hand side of (
65
) to the model of
\mathscr{P}
given in (
10
).
∎

Definition 3.18
.

Let
\{{\rm A}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be local data of a connection 1-form
\Theta
on a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
. The
{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}O_{i}
-foliated
{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s
-valued 1-forms given, for any
(\sigma,g)\in O_{i}\times\mathscr{G},\ i\in I
, by the formulæ

\displaystyle\Gamma_{i}(\sigma,g)={\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(g)}}r_{g}\circ{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t(g)\bigr)

are called the
Christoffel forms
of
\Theta
.

Proposition 3.19
.

Let
\mathscr{P}
be a principaloid bundle over
\Sigma
with local trivialisations
\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\mathscr{P}}^{-1}(O_{i})\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}
over an open cover
\{O_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
\Sigma
. Let
\Theta\in\Omega^{1}(\mathscr{P},{\rm V}\mathscr{P})
be a connection 1-form on
\mathscr{P}
with local connection data
\{{\rm A}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
. The shadow connection 1-form induced by
\Theta
on the shadow
\mathscr{F}
of
\mathscr{P}
is the
{\rm V}\mathscr{F}
-valued 1-form
\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}\in\Omega^{1}(\mathscr{F},{\rm V}\mathscr{F})
with local presentations

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1\,*}\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}\bigr)(\sigma,m)={\rm id}_{{\rm T}M}+\rho({\rm Id}_{m})\circ{\rm A}_{i}(\sigma,m)\,,\qquad\qquad(\sigma,m)\in O_{i}\times M\,,\qquad i\in I\,,

written in terms of the local trivialisations
\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\mathscr{F}}^{-1}(O_{i})\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }O_{i}\times M
corresponding to the
\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}
, and of the anchor map
\rho
of the tangent Lie algebroid
E
of
\mathscr{G}
.

Proof.

The shadow connection 1-form
\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}
is a smooth family of projectors onto
{\rm V}\mathscr{F}
and the latter subbundle within
{\rm T}\mathscr{F}
has
{\rm T}M
as a model. This explains the first (canonical) term
{\rm id}_{{\rm T}M}
in the local presentation of
\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}
. The structure of the non-canonical second term follows from the identity between the anchor
\rho\equiv{\rm T}t
and the local model of the tangent
{\rm T}\mathscr{D}
of the sitting-duck map inducing
{\rm H}\mathscr{F}
as in (
57
).
∎

3.3.
Existence and completeness

Theorem 3.20

(Existence of connections on a principaloid bundle)
.

On every principaloid bundle, there exists a connection.

Proof.

We invoke Prop.
3.4
and argue for the existence of a connection 1-form. To this end, consider a trivialising cover
\mathcal{O}\equiv\{O_{i}\}_{i\in I}
(assumed locally finite, see
[
N65
]
) of the base
\Sigma
of the principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
, together with the associated trivialisations
\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}
. Define the
I
-indexed family of smooth vector-valued 1-forms

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{i}:={\rm T}\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}\circ\jmath_{2}\circ\pi_{2}\circ{\rm T}\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}\,,\qquad i\in I

in terms of the canonical maps: the projection
\pi_{2}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm T}(O_{i}\times\mathscr{G})\cong{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}O_{i}\oplus{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm T}\mathscr{G}\to{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm T}\mathscr{G}
and the injection
\jmath_{2}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm T}\mathscr{G}\hookrightarrow{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}O_{i}\oplus{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm T}\mathscr{G}\cong{\rm T}(O_{i}\times\mathscr{G})
, where
{\rm pr}_{1}
and
{\rm pr}_{2}
are the canonical projections onto the first and the second factor in
O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}
, respectively. Use a smooth partition of unity
\{h_{i}\}_{i\in I}
associated with
\mathcal{O}
to define the globally smooth vector-valued 1-forms

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}:=\sum_{i\in I}\,\pi_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}h_{i}\,\mathcal{A}_{i}\,.

These satisfy the axioms (GC1)–(GC3) by construction.
∎

Since the set of principaloid bundles include general fibre bundles, see Example
2.24
, we do not expect connections to be complete always. However, completeness of the shadow connection on
\mathscr{F}
is sufficient for the completeness of the connection on
\mathscr{P}
under some additional assumptions:

Theorem 3.21
.

Let
\mathscr{P}
be a principaloid bundle with structure groupoid
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 6.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 21.14998pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
and connection 1-form
\Theta
. If
\mathscr{G}
admits a complete riemannian metric
{\rm g}
with respect to which right-invariant vector fields are bounded, and the connection
\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}
on the shadow
\mathscr{F}
of
\mathscr{P}
induced from
\Theta
is complete, then
\Theta
is complete. In particular, if
\mathscr{G}
admits a complete
\mathscr{G}
-invariant metric (in the sense of
[
dHF15
]
), and
M
is compact, then every connection
\Theta
on
\mathscr{P}
is complete.

Proof.

The problem of proving completeness of a connection on a fibre bundle
\pi_{\mathscr{E}}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{E}\to B
with base
\Sigma
and typical fibre
F
can, in general, be reduced to the following tasks (see
[
GHV72
, Problem VII.12]
):

1^{\circ}

finding a horizontal lift
\widetilde{\gamma}{}_{i}
to
\mathscr{E}
for any curve
\gamma_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:]a_{i},b_{i}[\to O_{i}
, defined for some
-\infty<a_{i}<b_{i}<\infty
, and contained entirely in an open set
O_{i}\subset B
belonging to a given open cover
\{O_{i}\}_{i\in I}
over which
E
trivialises;

2^{\circ}

checking that whenever there is a curve
\gamma_{ij}\hskip-2.0pt:]a_{i},b_{j}[\to O_{i}\cup O_{j},\ (i,j)\in I^{\times 2}_{\mathcal{O}}
, defined for some
-\infty<a_{i}<a_{j}<b_{i}<b_{j}<\infty
and glued smoothly from two smooth pieces
\gamma_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:]a_{i},b_{i}[\to O_{i}
and
\gamma_{j}\hskip-2.0pt:]a_{j},b_{j}[\to O_{j}
over
]a_{j},b_{i}[\subset\gamma_{ij}^{-1}(O_{i}\cap O_{j})
, the corresponding lifts
\widetilde{\gamma}{}_{i}
and
\widetilde{\gamma}{}_{j}
glue smoothly in
\mathscr{E}
.

In the former task, the key point is to ensure extendibility of every local lift from its domain given by a semi-closed interval
[c_{i},d_{i}[\subset]a_{i},b_{i}[
resp.
]d_{i},c_{i}]\subset]a_{i},b_{i}[
, bound on one side by an initial condition in
\mathscr{E}_{\gamma_{i}(c_{i})}
at
c_{i}
, to its closure
[c_{i},d_{i}]
resp.
[d_{i},c_{i}]
, see
[
M08
, Sec. 17.9]
. This goal is attained through a limiting procedure which uses a complete metric structure on
F
.

Specialising the above to the case of a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{P}
with connection 1-form
\Theta
for a proof of the first statement of the proposition, we start by considering the very formulation of the initial problem in
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}^{-1}(O_{i})\cong O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}
. The assumed completeness of
\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}
enables us to lift
\gamma_{i}
horizontally to
\mathscr{F}
. In this manner, we obtain a smooth curve
\underline{\gamma}{}_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:]a_{i},b_{i}[\to O_{i}\times M
through an arbitrary point
m_{i}\equiv({\rm pr}_{2}\circ\underline{\gamma}{}_{i})(c_{i})\in M
for some
c_{i}\in]a_{i},b_{i}[
, with a base projection
{\rm pr}_{1}\circ\underline{\gamma}{}_{i}=\gamma_{i}
and a velocity field
\dot{\underline{\gamma}}_{i}\in{\rm ker}\,(\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1\,*}\Theta_{\mathscr{F}})
. In the next step, we restrict the local datum
{\rm A}_{i}
of
\Theta
to
\underline{\gamma}{}_{i}
, whereby we obtain a time-dependent vector field

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\,]a_{i},b_{i}[{}_{{\rm pr}_{2}\circ\underline{\gamma}{}_{i}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s\subset{\rm T}\mathscr{G},\ (t,g)\longmapsto{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{{\rm pr}_{2}\circ\underline{\gamma}{}_{i}}}r_{g}\bigl({\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\dot{\underline{\gamma}}_{i}\bigr)\bigr)\equiv\Gamma_{i}\bigl(\gamma_{i}(t),g\bigr)\bigl(\dot{\underline{\gamma}}_{i}\bigr)\,.

By assumption, this field is bounded with respect to the complete metric
{\rm g}
on
\mathscr{G}
over every compact interval within
]a_{i},b_{i}[
. Let us, next, consider a lift of
\gamma_{i}
to
O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}
through the initial point
g_{i}\in t^{-1}(\{m_{i}\})
at
c_{i}
. Such a lift takes the form
\widetilde{\gamma}{}_{i}=(\gamma_{i},\widehat{\gamma}{}_{i})
, where
\widehat{\gamma}{}_{i}
is an integral curve of
\mathscr{X}_{i}
through
\widehat{\gamma}{}_{i}(c_{i})=g_{i}
.
A priori
, the latter exists and is unique locally, that is over some interval, say,
[c_{i},d_{i}[\subset]a_{i},b_{i}[
. In order to extend the local solution all the way to
b_{i}
—and likewise to
a_{i}
on the other side of
c_{i}
—we need to verify that the local solution reaches a point
\widetilde{g}{}_{i}\in\mathscr{G}
in the fibre over
d_{i}
as we take
c_{i}\to d_{i}
. However, using the aforementioned boundedness of
\mathscr{X}_{i}
over
[c_{i},d_{i}]
,

\displaystyle\forall\,(t,g)\in[c_{i},d_{i}]{}_{{\rm pr}_{2}\circ\underline{\gamma}{}_{k}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\hskip-2.0pt:\|\mathscr{X}_{i}(t,g)\|_{\rm g}\leq C_{i}<\infty\,,

we may estimate, for any
n_{i}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\times}
, the metric distance between the consecutive end-points
\widehat{\gamma}{}_{i}(d_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{i}})
and
\widehat{\gamma}{}_{i}(d_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{i}+1})
as

\displaystyle 0\leq d_{\rm g}\bigl(\widehat{\gamma}{}_{i}(d_{i}-\tfrac{1}{n_{i}}),\widehat{\gamma}{}_{i}(d_{i}-\tfrac{1}{n_{i}+1})\bigr)\leq\int_{d_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{i}}}^{d_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{i}+1}}\,{\mathsf{d}}t\,\|\dot{\widehat{\gamma}}{}_{i}(t)\|_{\rm g}=\int_{d_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{i}}}^{d_{i}-\frac{1}{n_{i}+1}}\,{\mathsf{d}}t\,\|\mathscr{X}_{i}\bigl(t,\widehat{\gamma}{}_{i}(t)\bigr)\|_{\rm g}\leq\tfrac{C_{i}}{n_{i}(n_{i}+1)}\xrightarrow[\ n_{i}\to\infty\ ]{}0\,.

The assumed completeness of the metric space
(\mathscr{G},d_{\rm g})
enables us to infer from this estimate that
\widehat{\gamma}{}_{i}
, and so also
\widetilde{\gamma}{}_{i}
do, indeed, extend to
d_{i}
, with

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}{}_{i}(d_{i})=(\gamma_{i}(d_{i}),\lim_{n_{i}\to\infty}\,\widehat{\gamma}{}_{i}(d_{i}-\tfrac{1}{n_{i}}))\,.

Taking the endpoint
\widetilde{\gamma}{}_{i}(d_{i})
of this extension as a new initial condition, we may, next, proceed with the extension to the right until we reach
b_{i}
, see the proof of
[
M08
, Prop. 23.9]
. Thus, we obtain a
\Theta
-horizontal lift
\widetilde{\gamma}{}_{i}
of
\gamma_{i}
for any interval
]a_{i},b_{i}[
through an arbitrary point
g_{i}\equiv({\rm pr}_{2}\circ\widetilde{\gamma}{}_{i})(c_{i})\in t^{-1}(\{m_{i}\})
at
c_{i}\in]a_{i},b_{i}[
. A smooth gluing of these lifts over intersections
O_{ij}
is ensured by the gluing law (
59
) for the corresponding local data of
\Theta
.

For a proof of the second statement of the proposition, note, first, that every connection on a fibre bundle with a compact typical fibre is complete—see,
e.g.
,
[
M08
, Sec. 17.9]
—and so the shadow connection
\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}
is complete for every
\Theta
. Moreover, the norm squared of a right-invariant vector field with respect to a right-invariant metric on
\mathscr{G}
, viewed as a function on
\mathscr{G}
with values in
{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
, is a pullback along
t
of a smooth function on the identity bisection, which is diffeomorphic with
M
, and hence compact by assumption. As a continuous function on a compact set, the norm is therefore bounded, and so both assumption from the first statement of the proposition are reproduced.
∎

Remark 3.22
.

Conditions under which there exists a riemannian metric on a Lie groupoid, and consequences of its existence, were discussed at length in
[
dHF15
]
.

3.4.
{\mathbb{B}}
-invariance

In the case of a bisection-complete strucure groupoid, an alternative definition of the principaloid bundle in terms of the
{\mathbb{B}}
-action is available. The underlying characterisation of the geometric object through its symmetry extends to connections in a natural way. By construction, a connection on
\mathscr{P}
,
i.e.
, the choice of the horizontal distribution
{\rm H}\mathscr{P}
, is
\mathscr{G}
-invariant, and hence also
{\mathbb{B}}
-invariant in the light of Prop.
2.20
. That the latter property determines the connection uniquely, for the distinguished class of strucure groupoids introduced in Def.
2.11
, follows from the analysis presented below.

Definition 3.23
.

A (compatible)
connection
on a
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
is a
{\mathbb{B}}
-invariant Ehresmann connection
{\rm T}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}={\rm V}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\oplus{\rm H}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
, i.e., a connection such that, for every
p\in\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
and
\beta\in{\mathbb{B}}
, the horizontal distribution
{\rm H}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
satisfies

(85)

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{p}\widehat{\mathscr{R}}{}_{\beta}({\rm H}_{p}\widehat{\mathscr{P}})={\rm H}_{\widehat{\mathscr{R}}{}_{\beta}(p)}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\,.

Remark 3.24
.

For a general groupoid
\mathscr{G}
with an object manifold
M
of non-zero dimension, the action of the group of bisections
{\mathbb{B}}
on
\mathscr{P}
is far from free. Choosing a bisection
\beta
in the stabiliser of a point
p\in\mathscr{P}
,
\mathscr{R}_{\beta}(p)=p
, the map
{\rm T}\mathscr{R}_{\beta}
may not be the identity. Correspondingly, condition (
85
) then poses severe restrictions on the horizontal subspace
{\rm H}_{p}\mathscr{P}
, namely that it lie inside
{\rm ker}{\rm T}\mu
.

As before, we may equivalently work with a smooth distribution of projectors over
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
.

Definition 3.25
.

Let
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
be a
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle. A (compatible)
connection 1-form
on
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
is a
{\rm V}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
-valued 1-form
\widehat{\Theta}\in\Omega^{1}(\widehat{\mathscr{P}},{\rm V}\widehat{\mathscr{P}})\equiv\Gamma({\rm T}^{*}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\otimes{\rm V}\widehat{\mathscr{P}})

such that, when viewed as an element in
{\rm End}{({\rm T}\widehat{\mathscr{P}})}
:

(BC1)

\widehat{\Theta}\big|_{{\rm V}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}}={\rm id}_{{\rm V}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}}
(a projection onto
{\rm V}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
);

(BC2)

\forall\ \beta\in{\mathbb{B}}\hskip-2.0pt:\quad\widehat{\Theta}\circ{\rm T}\mathscr{R}_{\beta}={\rm T}\mathscr{R}_{\beta}\circ\widehat{\Theta}
(
{\mathbb{B}}
-equivariance).

And, again, we readily establish the equivalence of the two definitions.

Proposition 3.26
.

Connections on a
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
are in a one-to-one correspondence with connection 1-forms on
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
.

Proof.

The choice of a decomposition
{\rm T}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}={\rm V}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\oplus{\rm H}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
determines a 1-form
\widehat{\Theta}
by declaring the latter to vanish on the horizontal subbundle and to be the identity on the vertical one, which in particular implies (BC1) by construction. Conversely, given
\widehat{\Theta}
, property (BC1) determines a decomposition with
{\rm H}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}:={\rm ker}\,\widehat{\Theta}
.

Note that (BC2) is trivially satisfied on vertical vectors due to (BC1). When applied to a horizontal vector,
i.e.
, one in the kernel of
\widehat{\Theta}
, (BC2) reduces to

\displaystyle\widehat{\Theta}\bigl(\mathscr{R}_{\beta}(p)\bigr)\circ{\rm T}_{p}\mathscr{R}_{\beta}|_{{\rm H}_{p}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}}=0

for every
p\in\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
and
\beta\in{\mathbb{B}}
. This identity follows immediately from identity (
85
) and,
vice versa
, identity (
85
) is implied by
the one above since
{\rm T}\mathscr{R}_{\beta}
, when applied to any tangent space, is an isomorphism and thus preserves the dimension of subspaces.
∎

Theorem 3.27
.

For an
{\rm Id}
-reducibile Lie groupoid
\mathscr{G}
, there is a one-to-one correspondence between
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundles with compatible connection and principaloid bundles with compatible connection.

Proof.

In virtue of Thm.
2.13
, the assumption about
\mathscr{G}
ensures that every
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle is a principaloid bundle and
vice versa
. Moreover, the
{\mathbb{B}}
-action on
\mathscr{P}
is induced from the
\mathscr{G}
-action by Prop.
2.20
, and so a principaloid bundle with compatible connection is automatically a
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle with compatible connection.

Let us, next, consider a model of a compatible connection on a
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
in one of
{\mathbb{B}}
-equivariant trivialisations
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\widehat{\mathscr{P}}}^{-1}(O_{i})\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}
associated with a cover
\{O_{i}\}_{i\in I}
. In the light of Prop.
3.26
, the local model of the horizontal distribution
{\rm H}_{p}\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
in
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}
is given by the kernel of a 1-form

(86)

\displaystyle\bigl(\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}^{-1\,*}\widehat{\Theta}\bigr)(\sigma,g)={\rm id}_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}\big|_{{\rm T}_{g}\mathscr{G}}+\widehat{\Gamma}{}_{i}(\sigma,g)\,,

obtained by pullback of the connection 1-form
\widehat{\Theta}
along
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}\tau_{i}
, with a vector-valued 1-form
\widehat{\Gamma}{}_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}O_{i}\to{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm T}\mathscr{G}
. Here, we use the same conventions as in the proof of Thm.
3.15
. Using local coordinates
\{\sigma^{\mu}\}^{\mu\in\overline{1,{\rm dim}\,\Sigma}}
on a neighbourhood of
\sigma\in O_{i}
, we write

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gamma}{}_{i}(\sigma,\cdot)={\rm d}\sigma^{\mu}(\sigma)\otimes\widehat{\Gamma}{}^{\sigma}_{i\,\mu}(\cdot)\,,

where
\widehat{\Gamma}{}^{\sigma}_{i\,\mu}\in\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})
. Invoking the assumption of
{\mathbb{B}}
-equivariance of
\widehat{\Theta}
, we note that while the identity operator
{\rm id}_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}
trivially commutes with
{\rm T}R_{\beta}
, the second term in (
86
) does so only if the
\widehat{\Gamma}{}_{i\,\mu}^{\sigma}
are
R({\mathbb{B}})
-invariant,

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gamma}{}_{i\,\mu}^{\sigma}\in\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})^{R({\mathbb{B}})}\,.

Therefore, by Prop.
2.15
, we establish a restriction on the codomain of the
\widehat{\theta}{}_{i}
in the following form:

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gamma}{}_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm T}O_{i}\longrightarrow{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s\,.

Accordingly, horizontal vector fields are restricted to lie in the distribution
{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}O_{i}\oplus{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s
within the model tangent bundle
{\rm pr}_{1}^{*}{\rm T}O_{i}\oplus{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm T}\mathscr{G}
, and so in the kernel of the local presentation of
{\rm T}\mu
.

Invoking Prop.
E.4
, we conclude that
{\rm T}\varrho_{g}
is, for every
g\in t^{-1}(\{\mu(p)\})
, represented by
{\rm T}R_{\beta_{g^{-1}}^{-1}}
acting on
{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s
. But the trivialisations are
{\mathbb{B}}
-equivariant, and therefore
{\rm T}\varrho_{g}
becomes
{\rm T}\mathscr{R}_{\beta_{g^{-1}}^{-1}}
globally over
\mathscr{P}
. The claim of the proposition now follows directly from the defining identity (
85
) and another application of Prop.
E.4
.
∎

4.
The Atiyah sequence, gauge transformations, and the trident of
\mathscr{P}

4.1.
The Atiyah–Ehresmann groupoid and its short exact sequence

The Atiyah–Ehresmann groupoid of an ordinary principal
{\rm G}
-bundle is
({\mathsf{P}}\times{\mathsf{P}})/{\rm G}
, where the quotient is relative to the diagonal action of
{\rm G}
. This is a bundle associated to
{\mathsf{P}}
by the defining right action
r
on
{\mathsf{P}}
. Thus, it can be described as

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,\bigl(O_{i}\times{\mathsf{P}}\bigr)/\sim_{r_{g_{\cdot\cdot}^{-1}}}

for the transition maps
g_{ij}\hskip-2.0pt:O_{ij}\to{\rm G}
of
{\mathsf{P}}
. We generalise this to principaloid bundles as follows.

Definition 4.1
.

We shall call the fibre bundle
\pi_{{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\to\Sigma
with typical fibre
\mathscr{P}
and model

\displaystyle\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,\bigl(\mathscr{P}\times O_{i}\bigr)/\sim_{\mathscr{R}_{\beta_{\cdot\cdot}^{-1}}}\longrightarrow\Sigma,\ [(p,\sigma)]\longmapsto\sigma\,,

the
Atiyah bundle of

\mathscr{P}
. We shall also denote, in the above model,

\displaystyle\pi_{1}\hskip-2.0pt:\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,\bigl(\mathscr{P}\times O_{i}\bigr)/\sim_{\mathscr{R}_{\beta_{\cdot\cdot}^{-1}}}\longrightarrow\Sigma,\ [(p,\sigma)]\longmapsto\pi_{\mathscr{P}}(p)\,.

Remark 4.2
.

{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
is canonically induced by the principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
through its transition 1-cocycle
\{\beta_{ij}\}_{i,j\in I^{\times 2}_{\mathcal{O}}}
realised by the defining action (
11
).

.

The pair
({\rm At}(\mathscr{P}),\mathscr{F})
carries a canonical structure of a Lie groupoid, fitting into the following short exact sequence of Lie groupoids:

Remark 4.4
.

The
\pi
-fibres of
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
are isomorphic to the Lie groupoid
\mathscr{G}
. We can, therefore, read the exact sequence (
87
) also as saying that
({\rm At}(\mathscr{P}),\mathrm{Pair}(\Sigma),\mathscr{G},\pi)
is a fibre-bundle object in the category of Lie groupoids:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 8.0347pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.0347pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}\>\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.0347pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.02496pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 26.02496pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.87221pt\raise-18.1986pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.87221pt\raise-28.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-36.3972pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 22.26106pt\raise-36.3972pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

The proof of Thm.
4.3
bases upon a pair of propositions and a lemma stated below. The Remark above then follows from noting that the fibre of
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
, which therefore also is the typical fibre of
{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})
, is the Lie groupoid

.

Proposition 4.5
.

The pair
({\rm At}(\mathscr{P}),\mathscr{F})
composes, in a canonical way, a Lie groupoid, with the following structure maps, modelled on those of the Lie groupoid
\mathscr{G}
in local trivialisations,

(88)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 36.52435pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-36.52435pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\times_{\mathscr{F}}{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.4788pt\raise 5.43056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt{\mathsf{M}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 93.42986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 93.42986pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm 148.92432pt\raise 5.43056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{J}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 182.02985pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 182.02985pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm 232.44096pt\raise 6.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{S}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 270.62984pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 232.00346pt\raise-6.50693pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{T}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 270.62984pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 270.62984pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 233.4132pt\raise 29.43056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{I}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 205.07274pt\raise 7.94609pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

Proof.

We begin by noting that
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
admits further (model) resolution given by

(89)

\displaystyle{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\cong\bigsqcup_{i,j\in I}\,\bigl(O_{i}^{(1)}\times\mathscr{G}\times O_{j}^{(2)}\bigr)/\sim_{L^{(1)}_{\beta_{\cdot\cdot}}\circ R^{(2)}_{\beta_{\cdot\cdot}^{-1}}}\,,

with local charts glued by the identifications

\displaystyle(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},k,l)\sim\bigl(\sigma_{1},\beta_{ik}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{lj}(\sigma_{2}),\sigma_{2},i,j\bigr)\,.

Accordingly, we define the structure maps as follows

(90)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\longrightarrow\mathscr{F},\ [(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)]\longmapsto[(\sigma_{2},s(g),j)]\,,

(91)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{T}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\longrightarrow\mathscr{F},\ [(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)]\longmapsto[(\sigma_{1},t(g),i)]\,,

(92)

(93)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{I}}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{F}\longrightarrow{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}),\ [(\sigma,m,i)]\longmapsto[(\sigma,{\rm Id}_{m},\sigma,i,i)]\,,

(94)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{J}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\longrightarrow{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}),\ [(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)]\longmapsto[(\sigma_{2},g^{-1},\sigma_{1},j,i)]

and

\displaystyle{\mathsf{M}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}){}_{{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{{\mathsf{T}}}\hskip-1.0pt{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\longrightarrow{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}),\ \bigl([(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)],[(\sigma_{2},h,\sigma_{3},k,l)]\bigr)\longmapsto[(\sigma_{1},g.\bigl(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h\bigr),\sigma_{3},i,l)]\,.

The well-definedness of the above maps is ensured by the identifications

•

for the source map, at
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in O_{ik}\times O_{jl}
,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},\beta_{ki}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{jl}(\sigma_{2}),\sigma_{2},k,l)]\bigr)=[(\sigma_{2},\beta_{lj}(\sigma_{2})\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,s(g),l)]\equiv[(\sigma_{2},s(g),j)]\,,

see Eq. (
205
) in Appendix
E
;

•

for the target map, at
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in O_{ik}\times O_{jl}
,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{T}}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},\beta_{ki}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{jl}(\sigma_{2}),\sigma_{2},k,l)]\bigr)=[(\sigma_{1},\beta_{ki}(\sigma_{1})\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,t(g),k)]\equiv[(\sigma_{1},t(g),i)]\,,

see Eq. (
206
);

•

for the identity map, at
\sigma\in O_{ij}
,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{I}}\bigl([(\sigma,\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m,j)]\bigr)=[(\sigma,C_{\beta_{ji}(\sigma)}({\rm Id}_{m}),\sigma,j,j)]\equiv[(\sigma,{\rm Id}_{m},\sigma,i,i)]\,,

see Eq. (
207
);

•

for the inverse map, at
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\in O_{ik}\times O_{jl}
,

\displaystyle{\mathsf{J}}[(\sigma_{1},\beta_{ki}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{jl}(\sigma_{2}),\sigma_{2},k,l)]=[(\sigma_{2},\beta_{lj}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright g{}^{-1}\vartriangleleft\beta_{ik}(\sigma_{2}),\sigma_{1},l,k)]\equiv[(\sigma_{2},g{}^{-1},\sigma_{1},j,i)]\,,

see Eq. (
208
);

•

for the multiplication map, at
(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})\in O_{im}\times O_{jkno}\times O_{lp}
,

(96)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{M}}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},\beta_{mi}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{jn}(\sigma_{2}),\sigma_{2},m,n)],[(\sigma_{2},\beta_{ok}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h\vartriangleleft\beta_{lp}(\sigma_{3}),\sigma_{3},o,p)]\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma_{1},(\beta_{mi}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{nj}(\sigma_{2})^{-1}).(\beta_{no}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright(\beta_{ok}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h)\vartriangleleft\beta_{lp}(\sigma_{3})),\sigma_{3},m,p)]

(98)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma_{1},(\beta_{mi}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g).((\beta_{jn}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright(\beta_{no}(\sigma_{2})\cdot\beta_{ok}(\sigma_{2}))\vartriangleright h)\vartriangleleft\beta_{lp}(\sigma_{3})),\sigma_{3},m,p)]

(100)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma_{1},(\beta_{mi}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g).((\beta_{jn}(\sigma_{2})\cdot\beta_{nk}(\sigma_{2}))\vartriangleright h\vartriangleleft\beta_{lp}(\sigma_{3})),\sigma_{3},m,p)]

(102)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma_{1},\beta_{mi}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright(g.(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h))\vartriangleleft\beta_{lp}(\sigma_{3}),\sigma_{3},m,p)]\equiv[(\sigma_{1},g.(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h),\sigma_{3},i,l)]\,,

see Eq. (
210
).

The constitutive relations between the structure maps are implied by the same relations for their local models. The only seemingly non-obvious ones are those involving the multiplication map
{\mathsf{M}}
, but in the light of the above consistency check, we may rewrite the definition of
{\mathsf{M}}
as

(103)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{M}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}){}_{{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-1.0pt\times_{{\mathsf{T}}}\hskip-1.0pt{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\longrightarrow{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}),\ \bigl([(\sigma_{1},g_{1},\sigma_{2},i,j)],[(\sigma_{2},g_{2},\sigma_{3},j,l)]\bigr)\longmapsto[(\sigma_{1},g_{1}.g_{2},\sigma_{3},i,l)]\,.

∎

Definition 4.6
.

We shall call
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 15.84724pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.84724pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
the
Atiyah–Ehresmann groupoid
of
\mathscr{P}
. The short exact sequence (
87
) of Lie groupoids shall be referred to as the
Atiyah sequence
for
\mathscr{P}
.

Lemma 4.7
.

The pair
(\pi,\pi_{\mathscr{F}})
is a Lie-groupoid epimorphism from
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
to
{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)
.

Proof.

The statement of the Lemma follows straightforwardly from the fact that the structure maps (
92
) and (
103
) cover the corresponding structure maps of
{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)
.
∎

Proposition 4.8
.

and
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 9.16667pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.16667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.39304pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.39304pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.39304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M_{2}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
be Lie groupoids, and let

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 80.33604pt\raise-42.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.0305pt\raise-24.97844pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\;\;s_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.0305pt\raise-42.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-10.63889pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.64195pt\raise-55.14438pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\chi_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 67.54439pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 67.54439pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M_{2}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}

be a morphism of Lie groupoids, whose component
\chi
is transverse
7
7

7

Recall that a smooth manifold map
f\hskip-2.0pt:M\to N
is said to be
transverse
to a submanifold
S\subset N
if at an arbitrary point
m\in f^{-1}(S)
, the following condition is satisfied:
{\rm T}_{m}f({\rm T}_{m}M)+{\rm T}_{f(m)}S={\rm T}_{f(m)}N
.
to the identity bisection
{\rm Id}(M_{2})\subset\mathscr{G}_{2}
. The preimage
\chi^{-1}({\rm Id}(M_{2}))
of
{\rm Id}(M_{2})
is a Lie subgroupoid of
\,\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 9.16667pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.16667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.39304pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.39304pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.39304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M_{1}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
, i.e., it is, in particular, a Lie groupoid.

Proof.

For
\chi^{-1}({\rm Id}(M_{2}))
to be a Lie subgroupoid in
\mathscr{G}_{1}
, the former has to be a submanifold in
\mathscr{G}_{1}
. In virtue of a classic variant of the Level-Set Theorem for submanifolds, this is ensured by the transversality of
\chi
.

At this stage, it remains to check that the subset
\chi^{-1}({\rm Id}(M_{2}))\subset\mathscr{G}_{1}
is a subgroupoid. To this end, consider an arbitrary pair
(g_{1},g^{\prime}_{1})\in\chi^{-1}({\rm Id}(M_{2}))\,{}_{s_{1}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t_{1}}\hskip-1.0pt\chi^{-1}({\rm Id}(M_{2}))
. There, then, exist points
m_{2},m^{\prime}_{2}\in M_{2}
such that
\chi(g_{1})={\rm Id}_{m_{2}}
and
\chi(g^{\prime}_{1})={\rm Id}_{m^{\prime}_{2}}
. As
\chi_{0}\circ s_{1}=s_{2}\circ\chi
and
\chi_{0}\circ t_{1}=t_{2}\circ\chi
, we establish the identity

\displaystyle m_{2}^{\prime}=t_{2}({\rm Id}_{m_{2}^{\prime}})=t_{2}\bigl(\chi(g_{1}^{\prime})\bigr)=\chi_{0}\bigl(t_{1}(g_{1}^{\prime})\bigr)=\chi_{0}\bigl(s_{1}(g_{1})\bigr)=s_{2}\bigl(\chi(g_{1})\bigr)=s_{2}({\rm Id}_{m_{2}})=m_{2}\,.

Since
\chi
is a morphism, this implies

\displaystyle\chi(g_{1}.g_{1}^{\prime})=\chi(g_{1}).\chi(g_{1}^{\prime})={\rm Id}_{m_{2}}.{\rm Id}_{m_{2}^{\prime}}={\rm Id}_{m_{2}}\,,

which leads to the desired conclusion
g_{1}.g_{1}^{\prime}\in\chi^{-1}({\rm Id}(M_{2}))
. Similarly, for every
g_{1}\in\chi^{-1}({\rm Id}(M_{2}))
, with
\chi(g_{1})={\rm Id}_{m_{2}}
, we have
\chi(g_{1}^{-1})=\chi(g_{1})^{-1}={\rm Id}_{m_{2}}^{-1}={\rm Id}_{m_{2}}
, and so also
g_{1}^{-1}\in\chi^{-1}({\rm Id}(M_{2}))
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.3
.
The existence of the structure of a Lie groupoid on
({\rm At}(\mathscr{P}),\mathscr{F})
is stated in Prop.
4.5
. The exactness of sequence (
87
) at its node
{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)
then follows from Lemma
4.7
. As a set,
{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})
fits into the short exact sequence by definition, and the only thing that remains to be proven is the embedding of the pair
({\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P}),\mathscr{F})
in the Atiyah–Ehresmann groupoid
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.84724pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.84724pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
as a Lie subgroupoid. Since
(\pi,\pi_{\mathscr{F}})
is an epimorphism of Lie groupoids, its arrow component
\pi
is automatically transverse to
{\rm Id}(\Sigma)\subset\Sigma\times\Sigma
(as a submersion), and so we conclude the present proof by invoking Prop.
4.8
. ∎

Definition 4.9
.

We shall call
{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})
the
adjoint bundle
, and
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 16.81947pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.81947pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.04584pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.04584pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.04584pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
the
adjoint groupoid
of
\mathscr{P}
.

Proposition 4.10
.

The adjoint bundle
{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})
has a model

(104)

\displaystyle{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})\cong\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,\bigl(O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}\bigr)/\sim_{C_{\beta_{\cdot\cdot}}}\,,

written in terms of the transition 1-cocycle
\{\beta_{ij}\}_{(i,j)\in I^{\times 2}_{\mathcal{O}}}
of
\mathscr{P}
, which we realise by conjugation of
\mathscr{G}
by
{\mathbb{B}}
.

Proof.

By definition, we have—in the notation of (
89
)—

\displaystyle{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})\equiv\pi^{-1}({\rm Id}(\Sigma))=\bigsqcup_{i,j\in I}\,\bigl(O_{i}^{(1)}\times_{\Sigma}\bigl(\mathscr{G}\times O_{j}^{(2)}\bigr)\bigr)/\sim_{L^{(1)}_{\beta_{\cdot\cdot}}\circ R^{(2)}_{\beta_{\cdot\cdot}^{-1}}}\,.

Thus, a point in
{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})
is an equivalence class
[(\sigma,g,\sigma,i,j)]\equiv[(\sigma,g\vartriangleleft\beta_{ji}(\sigma),\sigma,i,i)]\equiv[(\sigma,\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g,\sigma,j,j)]
. We define (smooth) maps

\displaystyle\jmath_{{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})}\hskip-2.0pt:\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,\bigl(O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}\bigr)/\sim_{C_{\beta_{\cdot\cdot}}}\longrightarrow{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P}),\ [(\sigma,g,i)]\longmapsto[(\sigma,g,\sigma,i,i)]

and

\displaystyle\iota_{{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})\longrightarrow\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,\bigl(O_{i}\times\mathscr{G}\bigr)/\sim_{C_{\beta_{\cdot\cdot}}},\ [(\sigma,g,\sigma,i,j)]\longmapsto[(\sigma,g\vartriangleleft\beta_{ji}(\sigma),i)]\,,

which are readily checked to be each other’s inverses. Their well-definedness is a consequence of the identities, written for
\sigma\in O_{ij}
and
\sigma^{\prime}\in O_{ijkl}
,

\displaystyle\jmath_{{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})}\bigl([(\sigma,C_{\beta_{ji}(\sigma)}(g),j)]\bigr)\equiv[(\sigma,C_{\beta_{ji}(\sigma)}(g),\sigma,j,j)]=[(\sigma,\beta_{ji}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{ji}(\sigma)^{-1},\sigma,j,j)]=[(\sigma,g,\sigma,i,i)]\,,

and

(106)

\displaystyle\iota_{{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})}\bigl([(\sigma^{\prime},\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{lj}(\sigma^{\prime})^{-1},\sigma^{\prime},k,l)]\bigr)\equiv[(\sigma^{\prime},\bigl(\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{lj}(\sigma^{\prime})^{-1}\bigr)\vartriangleleft\beta_{lk}(\sigma^{\prime}),k)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma^{\prime},\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{jl}(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot\beta_{lk}(\sigma^{\prime}),k)]=[(\sigma^{\prime},\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{jk}(\sigma^{\prime}),k)]

(108)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma^{\prime},\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\vartriangleright\bigl(g\vartriangleleft\beta_{jk}(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\bigr)\vartriangleleft\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})^{-1},k)]=[(\sigma^{\prime},g\vartriangleleft\beta_{jk}(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime}),i)]

(110)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma^{\prime},g\vartriangleleft\beta_{ji}(\sigma^{\prime}),i)]\,,

where in the second, third and fourth lines, we have used commutativity of left–multiplications of
\mathscr{G}
by
{\mathbb{B}}
with right-multiplications, alongside the 1-cocycle condition satisfied by the
\beta_{ij}(\sigma^{\prime})
.
∎

.

Applying the Lie functor to the short exact sequence
87
, we obtain an exact sequence of Lie algebroids:

79.77773pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern We shall call
\mathscr{E}
the
Atiyah algebroid
of
\mathscr{P}
. The fibre of
\mathscr{E}_{0}={\rm ker}\,\pi_{\mathscr{E}}
is the Lie algebroid
E=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathscr{G})
. This sequence appeared in the form of a
Q
-bundle in
[
KS14
]
, specifically for the case where the
Q
-manifolds correspond to Lie algebroids.

Example 4.12
.

If
\mathscr{G}={\rm G}
, then conjugation coincides with the adjoint action
{\rm Ad}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm G}\times{\rm G}\to{\rm G},\ (g,h)\mapsto ghg^{-1}
of the Lie group on itself, and we retrieve the adjoint bundle
{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})\cong{\rm Ad}\,{\rm P}
, associated with the pricipal
{\rm G}
-bundle
\mathscr{P}\equiv{\rm P}
through that adjoint action,
{\rm Ad}\,{\rm P}\equiv{\rm P}\times_{\rm Ad}{\rm G}
. Moreover,
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
is the standard Atiyah–Ehresmann groupoid of the principal
{\rm G}
-bundle and
\mathscr{E}
in Diag. (
111
) reduces to the standard Atiyah algebroid
{\rm T}{\rm P}/{\rm G}
.

Example 4.13
.

If
\mathscr{G}={\rm Pair}(M)
, then conjugation by
\beta\equiv(f,{\rm id}_{M})\in{\mathbb{B}}
, with
f\in{\rm Diff}(M)
, is just the cartesian square of
f
, i.e.,
C_{\beta}\equiv f\times f
, and the adjoint bundle of the principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}\cong\mathscr{F}\times M
is the
\Sigma
-fibred square
{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})\cong\mathscr{F}\times_{\Sigma}\mathscr{F}
of
\mathscr{F}
. Here,
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\equiv{\rm At}(\mathscr{F}\times M)
is just the pair groupoid
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 13.4167pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.4167pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}\times\mathscr{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.64307pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.64307pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.64307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
with the groupoid morphism
\pi
in Diag. (
87
) given by
\pi_{\mathscr{F}}\times\pi_{\mathscr{F}}
. Correspondingly,
\mathscr{E}={\rm T}\mathscr{F}
and
\pi_{\mathscr{E}}={\rm T}\pi_{\mathscr{F}}
.

The Atiyah sequence (
87
) gives rise to a specific subgroup of bisections in
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.84724pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.84724pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{At}(\mathscr{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.07361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
, which will become of relevance in what follows.

-projectable
. Such bisections compose a subgroup in
{\rm Bisec}({\rm At}(\mathscr{P}))
, which we shall denote henceforth as

\displaystyle{\rm Bisec}_{\pi}\bigl({\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\bigr)\,.

Proposition/Definition 4.15
.

Every
\pi
-projectable bisection
\beta\in{\rm Bisec}_{\pi}({\rm At}(\mathscr{P}))
such that
\underline{\beta}={\rm Id}\in{\rm Bisec}({\rm Pair}(\Sigma))
induces uniquely a bisection
\beta_{\rm v}\in{\rm Bisec}({\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P}))
. The subgroup of all such bisections shall be denoted as

\displaystyle{\rm Bisec}_{\pi}({\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P}))\,.

Proof.

Obvious.
∎

4.2.
Automorphisms

.
