55.90135pt\raise-24.97844pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathscr{P}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.90135pt\raise-42.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 22.60863pt\raise-56.068pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.29024pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 49.29024pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

Whenever
f={\rm id}_{\Sigma}
, we call
\Phi
a
vertical automorphism
, or a
gauge transformation
.

The
group of

principaloid-bundle automorphisms
of
\mathscr{P}
shall be denoted as

\displaystyle{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})\equiv{\rm Aut}_{{\rm{\bf Bun}}(\Sigma)}(\mathscr{P})\cap{\rm Diff}_{\varrho(\mathscr{G})}(\mathscr{P})\,,

and its subgroup composed of vertical automorphisms, also to be referred to as the
gauge group
of
\mathscr{P}
, as

\displaystyle{\rm Gauge}(\mathscr{P})\equiv{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})_{\rm vert}\,.

Proposition 4.17
.

Let
\mathscr{P}
be a principaloid (
\mathscr{G}
-)bundle, and let
\mathcal{O}\equiv\{O_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a trivialising cover of its base
\Sigma
. Given
f\in{\rm Diff}(\Sigma)
, define a refined cover
\mathcal{O}^{f}:=\{O^{f}_{(j,i)}\equiv f^{-1}(O_{j})\cap O_{i}\}_{(j,i)\in I^{\times 2}_{f,\mathcal{O}}}
of
\Sigma
with
I^{\times 2}_{f,\mathcal{O}}:=\{\ (j,i)\in I^{\times 2}\>|\>O^{f}_{(j,i)}\neq\emptyset\ \}
. An automorphism
\Phi\in{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})
of
\mathscr{P}
covering
f
is locally presented by a family of smooth maps

\displaystyle\gamma{}_{(j,i)}\hskip-2.0pt:O^{f}_{(j,i)}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{B}}\,,\qquad(j,i)\in I^{\times 2}_{f,\mathcal{O}}

as

(113)

\displaystyle\Phi\big|\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\mathscr{P}}^{-1}\bigl(\mathcal{O}^{f}_{(j,i)}\bigr)\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\pi_{\mathscr{P}}^{-1}\bigl(f\bigl(\mathcal{O}^{f}_{(j,i)}\bigr)\bigr),\ \mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g)\longmapsto\mathscr{P}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma),L_{\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)}(g)\bigr)\,.

Over
\mathcal{O}^{f}_{(j,i)(l,k)}\equiv O^{f}_{(j,i)}\cap O^{f}_{(l,k)}
, these maps are subject to the gluing relations

(114)

\displaystyle\gamma{}_{(l,k)}\big|_{\mathcal{O}^{f}_{(j,i)(l,k)}}=\bigl(f^{*}\beta{}_{lj}\cdot\gamma{}_{(j,i)}\cdot\beta{}_{ik}\bigr)\big|_{\mathcal{O}^{f}_{(j,i)(l,k)}}\,.

Conversely, every such family of maps determines an automorphism of
\mathscr{P}
.

Proof.

The local presentation (
113
) of the
\mathscr{G}
-equivariant map
\Phi
for the given choice of
\mathscr{G}
-equivariant trivialisations
\{\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}\}_{i\in I}
follows directly from Prop.
2.8
.

For the local maps (
113
) to come from a
globally
smooth one
\Phi\in{\rm Aut}_{{\rm{\bf Bun}}(\Sigma)}(\mathscr{P})
, the local presentations must glue over intersections
O^{f}_{(j,i)(l,k)}\ni\sigma
as

(116)

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma),\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g\bigr)\equiv\Phi_{(j,i)}\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g)\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}\Phi_{(l,k)}\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g)\bigr)=\Phi_{(l,k)}\bigl(\mathscr{P}\tau_{k}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,\beta_{ki}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}\tau_{l}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma),\gamma_{(l,k)}(\sigma)\cdot\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\vartriangleright g\bigr)=\mathscr{P}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma),\beta_{jl}\bigl(f(\sigma)\bigr)\cdot\gamma_{(l,k)}(\sigma)\cdot\beta_{ki}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g\bigr)\,,

whence the statement of the proposition follows upon setting
g={\rm Id}_{m}
for an arbitrary
m\in M
.

The converse claim is proven by reversing the above reasoning in the usual manner.
∎

Proposition 4.18
.

There are two canonical group homomorphisms (made explicit in the proof below),

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{*}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})\longrightarrow{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{F})\;,\qquad\mathscr{A}_{*}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Gauge}(\mathscr{P})\longrightarrow{\rm Aut}_{\rm vert}({\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P}))\,,

satisfying, for every
\Phi\in{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})
and every
\Phi_{\rm v}\in{\rm Gauge}(\mathscr{P})
, the equivariance identities

(117)

\displaystyle\mathscr{D}\circ\Phi=\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\circ\mathscr{D}\,,

(118)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}\circ\mathscr{A}_{*}(\Phi_{\rm v})=\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi_{\rm v})\circ{\mathsf{S}}\,,\qquad\qquad\mathscr{A}_{*}(\Phi_{\rm v})\circ{\mathsf{I}}={\mathsf{I}}\circ\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi_{\rm v})\,,

where
{\mathsf{S}}
and
{\mathsf{I}}
are the structure maps of Diag. (
88
), implicitly restricted to
{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})\subset{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
.

Proof.

In the light of Prop.
4.17
(and in the notation introduced in it), we find, for
\,(\sigma,g)\in O^{f}_{(j,i)}\times\mathscr{G}
,

\displaystyle(\mathscr{D}\circ\Phi)(\sigma,g)=\bigl(f(\sigma),t\bigl(L_{\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)}(g)\bigr)\bigr)=\bigl(f(\sigma),t_{*}(\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma))(t(g))\bigr)\,,

where the last equality uses Prop.
E.1
(ii). This provides us with the smooth candidate

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)_{(j,i)}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{\mathscr{F}}^{-1}\bigl(O^{f}_{(j,i)}\bigr)\longrightarrow\pi_{\mathscr{F}}^{-1}\bigl(f\bigl(O^{f}_{(j,i)}\bigr)\bigr),\ \mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,m)\longmapsto\mathscr{F}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma),t_{*}\bigl(\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)\bigr)(m)\bigr)

for a local presentation of
\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)
. We readily check the globality of the thus presented bundle automorphism through a direct calculation, carried out for
\sigma^{\prime}\in O^{f}_{(j,i)(l,k)}
,

(120)

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)_{(l,k)}\bigl(\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma^{\prime},m)\bigr)=\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)_{(l,k)}\bigl(\mathscr{F}\tau_{k}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m\bigr)\bigr)=\mathscr{F}\tau_{l}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma^{\prime}),\gamma_{(l,k)}(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},f^{*}\beta_{jl}(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot\gamma_{(l,k)}(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m\bigr)=\mathscr{F}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma^{\prime})\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)_{(j,i)}\bigl(\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma^{\prime},m)\bigr)\,,

in which the homomorphic nature of
t_{*}
has been taken into account, see Def.
B.10
. The latter also ensures homomorphicity of
\mathscr{F}_{*}
.

In the next step, we define the obvious local presentation of the induced automorphism

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{*}(\Phi)\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})\longrightarrow{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P}),\ \mathscr{A}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,g\bigr)\longmapsto\mathscr{A}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma),C{}_{\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)}\bigl(g\bigr)\bigr)\,,

and verify that it comes from a global map by checking, at an arbitrary
\sigma^{\prime}\in\mathcal{O}^{f}_{(j,i)(l,k)}
, the desired equality

(122)

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{*}(\Phi)\bigl(\mathscr{A}\tau_{k}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},C_{\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})}(g)\bigr)\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{A}\tau_{l}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma^{\prime}),C_{\gamma_{(l,k)}(\sigma^{\prime})}\circ C{}_{\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})}(g)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma^{\prime}),C_{\beta_{jl}(f(\sigma^{\prime}))}\circ C_{\gamma_{(l,k)}(\sigma^{\prime})}\circ C_{\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})}(g)\bigr)=\mathscr{A}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma^{\prime}),C_{f^{*}\beta_{jl}(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot\gamma_{(l,k)}(\sigma^{\prime})\cdot\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})}(g)\bigr)

(124)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma^{\prime}),C_{\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma^{\prime})}(g)\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{A}_{*}(\Phi)\bigl(\mathscr{A}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma^{\prime},g)\bigr)\,.

Finally, we verify the anticipated identities

(126)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}\circ\mathscr{A}_{*}(\Phi)\bigl(\mathscr{A}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,g)\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{F}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma),s\circ C_{\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)}(g)\bigr)=\mathscr{F}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma),t_{*}\bigl(\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)\bigr)\bigl(s(g)\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigl(\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,s(g)\bigr)\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\circ{\mathsf{S}}\bigl(\mathscr{A}\tau_{i}^{-1}\bigl(\sigma,g\bigr)\bigr)\,,

see Cor.
E.3
(i), and

(128)

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{*}(\Phi)\circ{\mathsf{I}}\bigl(\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,m)\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{A}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma),C_{\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)}({\rm Id}_{m})\bigr)=\mathscr{A}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma),{\rm Id}_{t_{*}(\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma))(m)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle{\mathsf{I}}\circ\mathscr{F}\tau_{j}^{-1}\bigl(f(\sigma),t_{*}\bigl(\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)\bigr)(m)\bigr)={\mathsf{I}}\circ\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigl(\mathscr{F}\tau_{i}^{-1}(\sigma,m)\bigr)\,,

see Prop.
E.1
(iii).
∎

Theorem 4.19
.

There is a canonical group isomorphism (made explicit in the proof below)

\displaystyle{\rm Bisec}_{\pi}\bigl({\rm At}(\mathscr{P})\bigr)\cong{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})\,,

which restricts to a group isomorphism

\displaystyle{\rm Bisec}_{\pi}({\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P}))\cong{\rm Gauge}(\mathscr{P})

for the respective subgroups.

Proof.

Consider an automorphism
\Phi\in{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})
which covers
f\in{\rm Diff}(\Sigma)
and is presented by local data
\{\gamma_{(j,i)}\hskip-2.0pt:O^{f}_{(j,i)}\to{\mathbb{B}}\}_{(j,i)\in I^{\times 2}_{f,\mathcal{O}}}
. These can be used to define a map

\displaystyle\beta_{\Phi}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{F}\longrightarrow{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}),\ [(\sigma,m,i)]\longmapsto[(f(\sigma),\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)(m),\sigma,j,i)]\,,

written, in the ‘resolved’ model (
89
) for
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
, and for an
arbitrary
index
j\in I
with the property
O^{f}_{(j,i)}\ni\sigma
(such an index exists, because the
O^{f}_{(j,i)}
compose a cover
\Sigma
, which refines
\mathcal{O}
). The map
\beta_{\Phi}
is readily checked to be globally smooth: Indeed, for every
\sigma^{\prime}\in O^{f}_{(j,i)}\cap O^{f}_{(l,k)}
, we obtain the identity

(130)

\displaystyle\beta_{\Phi}\bigl([(\sigma^{\prime},\beta_{ki}(\sigma)\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m,k)]\bigr)\equiv[(f(\sigma),\gamma_{(l,k)}(\sigma)(\beta_{ki}(\sigma)\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m),\sigma,l,k)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(f(\sigma),(f^{*}\beta_{lj}(\sigma)\cdot\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma))((t\cdot\beta_{ik}(\sigma))(\beta_{ki}(\sigma)\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m)).(\beta_{ki}(\sigma)((t\circ\beta_{ki}(\sigma))^{-1}(\beta_{ki}(\sigma)\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m))^{-1},\sigma,l,k)]

(132)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(f(\sigma),f^{*}\beta_{lj}(\sigma)(\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m).\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)(m).(\beta_{ki}(\sigma)(m))^{-1},\sigma,l,k)]

(134)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle[(f(\sigma),f^{*}\beta_{lj}(\sigma)\vartriangleright\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)(m)\vartriangleleft\beta_{ik}(\sigma),\sigma,l,k)]=[(f(\sigma),\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)(m),\sigma,j,i)]\,,

in which the second equality is a simple rewrite of Eq. (
114
).

Next, we verify that
\beta_{\Phi}
is a bisection of the Atiyah–Ehresmann groupoid of
\mathscr{P}
. To this end, we compute

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}\circ\beta_{\Phi}\bigl([(\sigma,m,i)]\bigr)\equiv{\mathsf{S}}\bigl([(f(\sigma),\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)(m),\sigma,j,i)]\bigr)\equiv[(\sigma,(s\circ(\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)))(m),i)]=[(\sigma,m,i)]\,,

using
\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)\in{\mathbb{B}}
along the way, and

(136)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{T}}\circ\beta_{\Phi}\bigl([(\sigma,m,i)]\bigr)\equiv{\mathsf{T}}\bigl([(f(\sigma),\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)(m),\sigma,j,i)]\bigr)\equiv[(f(\sigma),(t\circ(\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)))(m),j)]

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigl([(\sigma,m,i)]\bigr)\,,

see the local presentation
\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)_{(j,i)}
of
\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)
in the proof of Prop.
4.18
. Thus, altogether

(137)

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}\circ\beta_{\Phi}={\rm id}_{\mathscr{F}}\,,\qquad\qquad{\mathsf{T}}\circ\beta_{\Phi}=\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\in{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{F})\,,

which confirms our claim. The
\pi
-projectability of
\beta_{\Phi}
is manifest.

Conversely, given a
\pi
-projectable bisection
\beta\in{\rm Bisec}_{\pi}({\rm At}(\mathscr{P}))
, we associate with it an automorphism
\Phi_{\beta}\in{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})
as follows. Consider a local presentation of the bundle map
\beta\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{F}\to{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
relative to the ‘resolved’ model (
89
) for
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
,

(138)

\displaystyle\beta\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{F}\longrightarrow{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}),\ [(\sigma,m,i)]\longmapsto[(f(\sigma),b_{(j,i)}(\sigma,m),\sigma,j,i)]\,.

Here, we have already taken into account the assumption that
\beta
covers a bisection
(f,{\rm id}_{\Sigma})
of
{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)
, for some
f\in{\rm Diff}(\Sigma)
, and
j\in I
is an (arbitrary) index such that
O^{f}_{(j,i)}\ni\sigma
. For the above presentation to be independent of the arbitrary choices (of indices) made, we need to demand that the identity

(139)

\displaystyle b_{(l,k)}\bigl(\sigma^{\prime},\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m\bigr)=f^{*}\beta_{lj}(\sigma^{\prime})\vartriangleright b_{(j,i)}(\sigma^{\prime},m)\vartriangleleft\beta_{ik}(\sigma^{\prime})

hold true for every
m\in M
and
\sigma^{\prime}\in O^{f}_{(j,i)}\cap O^{f}_{(l,k)}
. Define

\displaystyle\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma):=b_{(j,i)}(\sigma,\cdot)\hskip-2.0pt:M\longrightarrow\mathscr{G}\,,

and invoke the local presentation of the structure maps
{\mathsf{S}}
and
{\mathsf{T}}
to read off from (
138
) the conditions

\displaystyle s\circ\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)={\rm id}_{M}\,,\qquad\qquad t\circ\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)\in{\rm Diff}(M)\,.

These jointly imply

\displaystyle\gamma_{(j,i)}\hskip-2.0pt:O^{f}_{(j,i)}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{B}}\,.

Upon rewriting Eq. (
139
) in terms of the
\gamma_{(j,i)}
and moving the transition maps of
\mathscr{P}
to the other side of the equality sign, we obtain the gluing law

(140)

\displaystyle\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma^{\prime})(m)=f^{*}\beta_{jl}(\sigma^{\prime})\vartriangleright\gamma_{(l,k)}(\sigma^{\prime})\bigl(\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m\bigr)\vartriangleleft\beta_{ki}(\sigma^{\prime})\,,

in which we recognise the familiar formula (
114
), evaluated at
m
. Hence, by Prop.
4.17
,
\beta
does, indeed, determine an automorphism
\Phi_{\beta}\in{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})
with local data
\gamma_{(j,i)}
which covers
f\in{\rm Diff}(\Sigma)
. We have the obvious identity
\beta\equiv\beta_{\Phi_{\beta}}
.

The last statement of the theorem follows straightforwardly from Prop.
4.15
.
∎

We conclude the present section with a natural definition of automorphisms of the
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundles of Def.
2.6
, which provides us with an alternative characterisation of
{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})
in the bisection-complete case.

Definition 4.20
.

An
automorphism
of a
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
is an arbitrary
{\mathbb{B}}
-equivariant bundle automorphism of
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
.

Proposition 4.21
.

Let
\mathscr{G}
be a bisection-complete Lie groupoid, and let
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
be a
\mathscr{G}
-fibred
{\mathbb{B}}
-bundle. Every automorphism of
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
is locally realised by elements of
L({\mathbb{B}})
as in Prop.
4.17
. Conversely, every automorphism of
\widehat{\mathscr{P}}
, viewed as a pricipaloid bundle, is
{\mathbb{B}}
-equivariant with respect to the induced
{\mathbb{B}}
-action (
11
).

Proof.

The first part of the proposition follows directly from Prop.
2.14
, and the second one is a consequence of the fact that left-multiplication commutes with right-multiplication.
∎

.

0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 464.19739pt\raise-259.68524pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\underline{\beta}{}_{\Phi}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 509.49463pt\raise-208.00795pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 508.69667pt\raise-252.81958pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 579.7247pt\raise-252.81958pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

which we may call the
extended Atiyah sequence
. It shows the interplay between
{\mathbb{B}}
-equivariant automorphisms of the bundle
\mathscr{P}
on the top floor, and the corresponding bisections of the groupoids on the bottom floor, where
{\mathbb{B}}
-equivariance is encoded by their construction. Note also that while the middle floor is redundant for ordinary principal
{\rm G}
-bundles, here, it carries information on the induction of automorphisms of the shadow bundle from those of the principaloid bundle.

Whenever the underlying bisection
\underline{\beta}{}_{\Phi}
of
{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)
is the identity bisection
{\rm Id}(\Sigma)
, with
f={\rm id}_{\Sigma}
, then
\beta_{\Phi}
induces a bisection of the adjoint groupoid
{\rm Ad}(\mathscr{P})
, which then corresponds to a gauge transformation of
(\mathscr{P},\mathscr{F})
.

4.3.
The trident of the principaloid bundle

Theorem 4.23
.

The Atiyah–Ehresmann groupoid acts on the principaloid bundle and its shadow in the following way:

\bullet

On every principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
, there exists a canonical structure of a left
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
-module, with momentum
\mu_{\mathscr{P}}=\mathscr{D}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}\to\mathscr{F}
and action

(141)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}){}_{{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\mathscr{D}}\hskip-2.0pt\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow\mathscr{P},\ \bigl([(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)],[(\sigma_{2},h,k)]\bigr)\longmapsto[(\sigma_{1},g.(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h),i)]\,,

written in the local models: (
89
) for
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
and (
10
) for
\mathscr{P}
, for
\sigma_{1}\in O_{i}
and
\sigma_{2}\in O_{jk}
.

\bullet

This structure covers the canonical structure of a left
{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)
-module on
\Sigma
, with momentum
\mu_{\Sigma}\equiv{\rm id}_{\Sigma}
and action

\displaystyle\lambda_{\Sigma}\hskip-2.0pt:(\Sigma\times\Sigma)\,{}_{{\rm pr}_{2}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{{\rm id}_{\Sigma}}\hskip-2.0pt\Sigma\longrightarrow\Sigma,\ \bigl((\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}),\sigma_{2}\bigr)\longmapsto\sigma_{1}\,.

\bullet

On the corresponding shadow bundle
\mathscr{F}
, there exists a canonical structure of a left
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
-module, with momentum
\mu_{\mathscr{F}}={\rm id}_{\mathscr{F}}
and action

(142)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}){}_{{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{{\rm id}_{\mathscr{F}}}\hskip-2.0pt\mathscr{F}\longrightarrow\mathscr{F},\ \bigl([(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)],[(\sigma_{2},m,k)]\bigr)\longmapsto[(\sigma_{1},t(g),i)]\,.

\bullet

The action
\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}
is intertwined with
\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}
by the sitting-duck map
\mathscr{D}
, as reflected in the identities

(143)

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathscr{P}}=\mu_{\mathscr{F}}\circ\mathscr{D}\,,\qquad\qquad\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})}\times\mathscr{D}\bigr)=\mathscr{D}\circ\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\,.

Proof.

Let us first analyse the triple
(\mathscr{P},\mu_{\mathscr{P}},\lambda_{\mathscr{P}})
. Composability of the two arrows:
g
and
\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h
in (
141
) is ensured by the identity

\displaystyle[(\sigma_{2},s(g),j)]\equiv{\mathsf{S}}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)]\bigr)=\mathscr{D}\bigl([(\sigma_{2},h,k)]\bigr)\equiv[(\sigma_{2},t(h),k)]=[(\sigma_{2},t_{*}(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2}))(t(h)),j)]\,.

Using the 1-cocycle condition for the
\beta_{ij}
alongside identities (
210
), we readily establish, for any
\sigma_{1}\in O_{il}
and
\sigma_{2}\in O_{jkmn}
, the identity

(145)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},\beta_{li}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{jm}(\sigma_{2}),\sigma_{2},l,m)],[(\sigma_{2},\beta_{nk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h,n)]\bigr)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle[(\sigma_{1},(\beta_{li}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{jm}(\sigma_{2})).(\beta_{mn}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright(\beta_{nk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h)),l)]

(147)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle[(\sigma_{1},(\beta_{li}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta_{jm}(\sigma_{2})).(\beta_{mk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h),l)]

(149)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma_{1},(\beta_{li}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g).(\beta_{jm}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright(\beta_{mk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h)),l)]=[(\sigma_{1},(\beta_{li}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright g).(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h),l)]

(151)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma_{1},\beta_{li}(\sigma_{1})\vartriangleright(g.(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h)),l)]=[(\sigma_{1},g.(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h),i)]\,,

which demonstrates that
\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}
is a well-defined globally smooth action map sought after. Its constitutive properties are readily verified in the following calculations: Axiom (GlM1) of Def.
C.1
is checked in

(153)

\displaystyle(\mu_{\mathscr{P}}\circ\lambda_{\mathscr{P}})\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)],[(\sigma_{2},h,k)]\bigr)\equiv\mathscr{D}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g.(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h),i)]\bigr)=[(\sigma_{1},t(g.(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h)),i)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma_{1},t(g),i)]\equiv{\mathsf{T}}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)]\bigr)\,.

Axiom (GlM2) is seen to hold true in virtue of

(155)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\bigl({\mathsf{I}}\circ\mu_{\mathscr{P}}\bigl([(\sigma,g,i)]\bigr),[(\sigma,g,i)]\bigr)\equiv\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\bigl([(\sigma,{\rm Id}_{t(g)},\sigma,i,i)],[(\sigma,g,i)]\bigr)\equiv[(\sigma,{\rm Id}_{t(g)}.(\beta_{ii}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g),i)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma,g,i)]\,.

Axiom (GlM3) is established, with the help of the first of identities (
209
), in

(157)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g_{1},\sigma_{2},i,j)],\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\bigl([(\sigma_{2},g_{2},\sigma_{3},k,l)],[(\sigma_{3},h,m)]\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g_{1},\sigma_{2},i,j)],[(\sigma_{2},g_{2}.(\beta_{lm}(\sigma_{3})\vartriangleright h),k)]\bigr)=[(\sigma_{1},g_{1}.(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright(g_{2}.(\beta_{lm}(\sigma_{3})\vartriangleright h))),i)]

(159)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma_{1},(g_{1}.(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright g_{2})).(\beta_{lm}(\sigma_{3})\vartriangleright h))),i)]\equiv\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g_{1}.(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright g_{2}),\sigma_{3},i,l)],[(\sigma_{3},h,m)]\bigr)

(161)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\bigl({\mathsf{M}}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g_{1},\sigma_{2},i,j)],[(\sigma_{2},g_{2},\sigma_{3},k,l)]\bigr),[(\sigma_{3},h,m)]\bigr)\,.

Projectability of the above structure of a left
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
-module on
\mathscr{P}
onto the canonical structure of a left
{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)
-module on
\Sigma
(along
(\pi,\pi_{\mathscr{F}})
) is self-evident.

Passing to the triple
(\mathscr{F},\mu_{\mathscr{F}},\lambda_{\mathscr{F}})
, we note well-definedness of
\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}
and manifest projectability of the entire structure onto
(\Sigma,{\rm id}_{\Sigma},\lambda_{\Sigma})
. The first of identities (
143
) is trivially satisfied, and so we are left with the second one to prove. First of all, note the obvious identity

\displaystyle\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})}\times\mathscr{D}\bigr)\bigl({\rm At}(\mathscr{P}){}_{{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\mathscr{D}}\hskip-2.0pt\mathscr{P}\bigr)\equiv{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}){}_{{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{{\rm id}_{\mathscr{F}}}\hskip-2.0pt\mathscr{F}\,,

which ensures meaningfulness of that
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
-equivariance condition. The latter is checked in a direct computation:

(163)

\displaystyle\bigl(\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})}\times\mathscr{D}\bigr)\bigr)\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)],[(\sigma_{2},h,k)]\bigr)\equiv\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)],[(\sigma_{2},t(h),k)]\bigr)\equiv[(\sigma_{1},t(g),i)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(\sigma_{1},t(g.(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h)),i)]\equiv\mathscr{D}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g.(\beta_{jk}(\sigma_{2})\vartriangleright h),i)]\bigr)\equiv\bigl(\mathscr{D}\circ\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\bigr)\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)],[(\sigma_{2},h,k)]\bigr)\,.

∎

Remark 4.24
.

Note that the independence of
\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}
of the choice of local charts permits us to simplify (
141
) as

(164)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}){}_{{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\mathscr{D}}\hskip-2.0pt\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow\mathscr{P},\ \bigl([(\sigma_{1},g,\sigma_{2},i,j)],[(\sigma_{2},h,j)]\bigr)\longmapsto[(\sigma_{1},g.h,i)]\,.

In analogy with the construction of Remark
2.21
, and consistently with Thm.
4.19
, we establish

Proposition 4.25
.

The structure of a left
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
-module on
\mathscr{P}
gives rise to a geometric implementation of automorphisms
{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})\ni\Phi
on
\mathscr{P}
(resp. on
\mathscr{F}
) through restriction of
\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}
(resp. of
\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}
) to the corresponding submanifolds
\beta_{\Phi}(\mathscr{F})\subset{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
, embedded by the
\pi
-projectable bisections
\beta_{\Phi}\in{\rm Bisec}_{\pi}({\rm At}(\mathscr{P}))
,

and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 6.2639pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 91.62216pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}){}_{{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{{\rm id}_{\mathscr{F}}}\hskip-2.0pt\mathscr{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 121.93127pt\raise-25.14511pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 121.93127pt\raise-43.01247pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-6.2639pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.46832pt\raise-19.03403pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hskip 8.19447pt\beta_{\Phi}\times{\rm id}_{\mathscr{F}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 102.39816pt\raise-8.05554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.31628pt\raise-56.79024pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 115.66737pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 115.66737pt\raise-50.29024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

Proof.

Given an automorphism
\Phi
covering
f\in{\rm Diff}(\Sigma)
, with local data
\{\gamma_{(j,i)}\hskip-2.0pt:O^{f}_{(j,i)}\to{\mathbb{B}}\}_{(j,i)\in I^{\times 2}_{f,\mathcal{O}}}
, we obtain

(166)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\bigl(\beta_{\Phi}\bigl([(\sigma,t(\beta_{ik}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g),i)]\bigr),[(\sigma,g,k)]\bigr)=\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\bigl([(f(\sigma),\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)(\beta_{ik}(\sigma)\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,t(g)),\sigma,j,i)],[(\sigma,g,k)]\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(f(\sigma),\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)(\beta_{ik}(\sigma)\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,t(g)).(\beta_{ik}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g),j)]=[(f(\sigma),(\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)(\beta_{ik}(\sigma)\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,t(g))\vartriangleleft\beta_{ik}(\sigma)).g,j)]

(168)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(f(\sigma),\gamma_{(j,k)}(\sigma)(t(g)).g,j)]\equiv[(f(\sigma),\gamma_{(j,k)}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g,j)]\equiv\Phi\bigl([(\sigma,g,k)]\bigr)\,.

The well-definedness of the above calculation is ensured by the identity
{\mathsf{S}}\circ\beta_{\Phi}\circ\mu_{\mathscr{P}}=\mu_{\mathscr{P}}
(see (
137
)), which implies composability of the arrows in the third expression. The third equality is implied by the second identity in Eq. (
210
), and the next one follows upon invoking the explicit form (
140
) of the gluing law (
114
). Thus, altogether, we arrive at the anticipated identity

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\circ\bigl(\beta_{\Phi}\times{\rm id}_{\mathscr{P}}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\mu_{\mathscr{P}},{\rm id}_{\mathscr{P}}\bigr)=\Phi\,.

The corresponding identity for
\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}
is now readily derived with the help of identities (
143
) and the first one of the equivariance identities of Prop.
4.18
:

(170)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}\circ\bigl(\beta_{\Phi}\times{\rm id}_{\mathscr{F}}\bigr)\circ\mathscr{D}\equiv\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}\circ\bigl(\beta_{\Phi}\times{\rm id}_{\mathscr{F}}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\mu_{\mathscr{F}},{\rm id}_{\mathscr{F}}\bigr)\circ\mathscr{D}=\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}\circ\bigl(\beta_{\Phi}\times{\rm id}_{\mathscr{F}}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\mu_{\mathscr{P}},\mathscr{D}\bigr)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathscr{F}}\circ\bigl({\rm id}_{{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})}\times\mathscr{D}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\beta_{\Phi}\times{\rm id}_{\mathscr{P}}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\mu_{\mathscr{P}},{\rm id}_{\mathscr{P}}\bigr)=\mathscr{D}\circ\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\circ\bigl(\beta_{\Phi}\times{\rm id}_{\mathscr{P}}\bigr)\circ\bigl(\mu_{\mathscr{P}},{\rm id}_{\mathscr{P}}\bigr)=\mathscr{D}\circ\Phi

(172)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\circ\mathscr{D}\,,

and taking into account the surjectivity of
\mathscr{D}
.
∎

Definition 4.26
.

Let
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 10.18057pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.18057pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.40694pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.40694pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.40694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M_{A}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt,\ A\in\{1,2,3\}
be Lie groupoids, let
\widehat{P}
be a principal
(\mathscr{G}_{1},\mathscr{G}_{2})
-bibundle, as in Def.
C.13
, and let
\Sigma
be a manifold. We shall call the quintuple
(\mathscr{G}_{1},\widehat{P},\mathscr{G}_{2};\Sigma,\mathscr{G}_{3})
a (
left
)
trident
with
base

\Sigma
and fibre
\mathscr{G}_{3}
if the following conditions are satisfied:

•

\widehat{P}
is the total space of a fibre bundle
\pi_{\widehat{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:\widehat{P}\to\Sigma
with base
\Sigma
and typical fibre
\mathscr{G}_{3}
;

•

the right
\mathscr{G}_{2}
-action
\rho_{2}\equiv\blacktriangleleft
preserves
\pi_{\widehat{P}}
-fibres, i.e.,
\pi_{\widehat{P}}(p\blacktriangleleft g_{2})=\pi_{\widehat{P}}(p)
for all
(p,g_{2})\in\widehat{P}{}_{\mu_{2}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t_{2}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}_{2}
;

•

the Lie groupoid
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 9.16667pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.16667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.39304pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.39304pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.39304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M_{1}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt
is the total space of a fibre-bundle object in the category of Lie groupoids, with base

and typical fibre
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 9.16667pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.16667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.39304pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 23.39304pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 23.39304pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M_{3}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt

The trident shall be represented by the following diagram:

(173)

Theorem 4.27
.

For every principaloid bundle

, the quintuple

is a trident, captured by the following diagram

37.1868pt\raise-72.12772pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{F}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 74.97777pt\raise-72.12772pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 148.29576pt\raise-72.12772pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-107.85823pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 40.4507pt\raise-107.85823pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 71.36665pt\raise-107.85823pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 111.63676pt\raise-107.85823pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 148.29576pt\raise-107.85823pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

Proof.

The structure of a right principal
\mathscr{G}
-module on
(\mathscr{P},\mathscr{F},\mathscr{D})
was established in Thm.
2.16
. The underlying right
\mathscr{G}
-action on
\mathscr{P}
preserves
\pi_{\mathscr{P}}
-fibres by definition. Furthermore, the identification of
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
as a fibre-bundle object in the category of Lie groupoids, with base
{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)
and typical fibre
\mathscr{G}
, was made in Remark
4.4
. Therefore, it remains to investigate the left action of
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
on
\mathscr{P}
, as identified in Thm.
4.23
.

We begin by noting that
\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}){}_{{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\mathscr{D}}\hskip-2.0pt\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow\mathscr{P}
preserves
\mu
-fibres. Indeed, the action restricts to fibres of
\mathscr{P}
as the
left
-multiplication, and so leaves the moment map
\mu
, locally modelled on
s
, invariant.

The action gives rise to a smooth map

\displaystyle(\lambda_{\mathscr{P}},{\rm pr}_{2})\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}){}_{{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\mathscr{D}}\hskip-2.0pt\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow\mathscr{P}{}_{\mu}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\mu}\hskip-2.0pt\mathscr{P}\,,

whose well-definedness is ensured by the following equality (see Eq. (
164
)):

\displaystyle\mu\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g.h,i)]\bigr)\equiv s(g.h)=s(h)\equiv\mu\bigl([(\sigma_{2},h,j)]\bigr)\,.

Consider the division map

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathscr{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{P}{}_{\mu}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\mu}\hskip-2.0pt\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}),\ \bigl([(\sigma_{1},g,i)],[(\sigma_{2},h,j)]\bigr)\longmapsto[(\sigma_{1},g.h^{-1},\sigma_{2},i,j)]\,.

It is well-defined since
s(g)\equiv\mu([(\sigma_{1},g,i)])=\mu([(\sigma_{2},h,j)])\equiv s(h)=t(h^{-1})
. It now remains to verify the identity

(174)

\displaystyle(\psi_{\mathscr{P}},{\rm pr}_{2})=(\lambda_{\mathscr{P}},{\rm pr}_{2})^{-1}\,.

We have

\displaystyle{\mathsf{S}}\bigl([(\sigma_{1},g.h^{-1},\sigma_{2},i,j)]\bigr)\equiv[\sigma_{2},s(g.h^{-1}),j]=[\sigma_{2},t(h),j]\equiv\mathscr{D}\bigl([(\sigma_{2},h,j)]\bigr)\,,

so that
(\psi_{\mathscr{P}},{\rm pr}_{2})
maps
\mathscr{P}{}_{\mu}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\mu}\hskip-2.0pt\mathscr{P}
to
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P}){}_{{\mathsf{S}}}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\mathscr{D}}\hskip-2.0pt\mathscr{P}
. Identity (
174
) can now be checked in a direct calculation. This establishes
\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}
as a principal action.

That
\lambda_{\mathscr{P}}
covers the canonical left action of
{\rm Pair}(\Sigma)
on
\Sigma
is part of Thm.
4.23
.
∎

Remark 4.28
.

In the approach to the theory of principal
{\rm G}
-bundles due to Ehresmann
[
Eh50
]
, we encounter triples
({\mathsf{P}},{\rm G},{\rm At}({\mathsf{P}}))
consisting of a principal
{\rm G}
-bundle
{\mathsf{P}}
, its structure
group

{\rm G}
, and its structure
groupoid

{\rm At}({\mathsf{P}})
. This is captured by Diag. (
202
) for
M_{2}
a point and
M_{1}=\Sigma
the base of
{\mathsf{P}}
. In the above, instead, the right wing of the
W
-diagram is a general groupoid. This aligns with an idea of Pradines
8
8

8

The idea later resurfaced in various incarnations in the study of foliations and generalised (Morita) morphisms of groupoids, in particular in the works of Hilsum and Skandalis
[
HiS83
,
HiS87
]
(leading to the related notion of Hilsum–Skandalis maps), and Haefliger
[
H84
]
.

[
P77
]
(see also
[
P07
]
), whose goal was to symmetrise the structure as in Def.
C.13
, except that here
M_{1}
cannot remain to be
\Sigma
, but it is to be replaced, in an essential way, by the bundle
\mathscr{F}
, whose fibre is
M_{2}=M
. Thus, in distinction to Pradines, there emerges the trident diagram (
173
).

Example 4.29
.

In the setting of Example
2.25
, with
\mathscr{P}={\mathsf{P}}\times M
, the action of
\mathscr{G}={\rm G}\times M
on
\mathscr{P}
coincides with the diagonal action of
\,{\rm G}
on
{\mathsf{P}}\times M
, and the action of
{\rm At}(\mathscr{P})
is by automorphisms of the factor
{\mathsf{P}}
in
{\mathsf{P}}\times M
. That the two actions commute can be used to see that the automorphisms of
{\mathsf{P}}
descend to automorphisms of the associated bundle
\mathscr{F}={\mathsf{P}}\times_{\lambda}M\equiv({\mathsf{P}}\times M)/{\rm G}
. This includes the important vertical automorphisms, which correspond to gauge transformations.

4.4.
Gauge transformations of connections

Definition 4.30
.

Let
\mathscr{P}
be a principaloid bundle with connection 1-form
\Theta\in\Omega^{1}(\mathscr{P},{\rm V}\mathscr{P})
. Given an automorphism
\Phi\in{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})\cong{\rm Bisec}_{\pi}({\rm At}(\mathscr{P}))
, we denote

(175)

\displaystyle\Theta^{\Phi}:=\Phi^{-1\,*}\Theta\equiv{\rm T}\Phi\circ\Theta\circ{\rm T}\Phi^{-1}\in\Omega^{1}(\mathscr{P},{\rm V}\mathscr{P})\,.

Whenever
\Phi\in{\rm Gauge}(\mathscr{P})\equiv{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})_{\rm vert}
, the 1-form
\Theta^{\Phi}
shall be called the
gauge transform
of
\Theta
.

Proposition 4.31
.

Let
\mathscr{P}
be a principaloid bundle with connection 1-form
\Theta
with local connection data
\{{\rm A}_{i}\in\Omega^{1}(O_{i}\times M,{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}E)\}_{i\in I}
associated with a trivialising cover
\mathcal{O}\equiv\{\mathcal{O}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of the base
\Sigma
. For every automorphism
\Phi\in{\rm Aut}(\mathscr{P})
covering a diffeomorphism
f\in{\rm Diff}(\Sigma)
and presented by local data
\gamma_{(j,i)}\hskip-2.0pt:O^{f}_{(j,i)}\to{\mathbb{B}},\ (j,i)\in I^{\times 2}_{f,\mathcal{O}}
, local connection data
\{{\rm A}_{i}^{\Phi}\}_{i\in I}
of
\Theta^{\Phi}
obey, for any
\sigma\in O^{f}_{(j,i)}
and
m\in M
, the following transformation law:

(176)

\displaystyle{\rm A}_{j}^{\Phi}\bigl(f(\sigma),t_{*}\bigl(\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)\bigr)(m)\bigr)\circ{\rm T}_{\sigma}f={\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{m}}C_{\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)}\circ{\rm A}_{i}(\sigma,m)-\bigl({\rm ev}_{m}\circ\gamma_{(j,i)}\bigr)^{*}\theta_{\rm R}(\sigma)\,,

For
\Phi\in{\rm Gauge}(\mathscr{P})
, the above formula specialises to

(177)

\displaystyle{\rm A}_{i}^{\Phi}\bigl(\sigma,t_{*}\bigl(\gamma_{i}(\sigma)\bigr)(m)\bigr)={\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{m}}C_{\gamma_{i}(\sigma)}\circ{\rm A}_{i}(\sigma,m)-\bigl({\rm ev}_{m}\circ\gamma_{i}\bigr)^{*}\theta_{\rm R}(\sigma)\,,

where
\gamma_{i}\equiv\gamma_{(i,i)}
.

Proof.

The statement of the proposition is verified through a calculation fully analogous to the derivation of the gluing law for local connection data, carried out in the proof of Thm.
3.15
. More specifically, upon fixing local coordinates:
\{\zeta^{\alpha}\}^{\alpha\in\overline{1,{\rm dim}\,\mathscr{G}}}
on a neighbourhood
\mathscr{U}
of
g\in\mathscr{G}
and
\{\widehat{\zeta}{}^{\alpha}\}^{\alpha\in\overline{1,{\rm dim}\,\mathscr{G}}}
on a neighbourhood
\widehat{\mathscr{U}}
of
\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g
, formula (
175
) yields an equality

(179)

\displaystyle{\rm id}_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}\big|_{{\rm T}_{g}\mathscr{G}}+{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(g)}}r_{g}\circ{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t(g)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{\check{\eta}{}_{(j,i)}(\sigma,g)}L_{\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ\bigl(\check{\eta}{}_{(j,i)}^{*}{\rm d}\widehat{\zeta}{}^{\alpha}(\sigma,g)\otimes\widehat{\partial}{}_{\alpha}\big|_{\check{\eta}{}_{(j,i)}(\sigma,g)}+{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\eta}{}_{(j,i)}(\sigma,g))}}r_{\check{\eta}{}_{(j,i)}(\sigma,g)}\circ{\rm A}_{j}^{\Phi}\bigl(f(\sigma),t\bigl(\check{\eta}{}_{(j,i)}(\sigma,g)\bigr)\bigr)\circ{\rm T}_{\sigma}f\bigr)\,,

expressed in terms of the smooth map

\displaystyle\check{\eta}{}_{(j,i)}\hskip-2.0pt:O^{f}_{(j,i)}\times\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G},\ (\sigma,g)\longmapsto\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)\vartriangleright g\,.

Computing the right-hand side of the above condition as in the proof quoted, we obtain

(181)

\displaystyle{\rm id}_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}\big|_{{\rm T}_{g}\mathscr{G}}+{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(g)}}r_{g}\circ{\rm A}_{i}\bigl(\sigma,t(g)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm id}_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}\big|_{{\rm T}_{g}\mathscr{G}}+{\rm T}_{\check{\eta}{}_{(j,i)}(\sigma,g)}L_{\gamma_{(j,i)}(\sigma)^{-1}}\circ\bigl({\rm T}_{\sigma}\check{\eta}{}_{(j,i)}(\cdot,g)+{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\check{\eta}{}_{(j,i)}(\sigma,g))}}r_{\check{\eta}{}_{ij}(\sigma,g)}\circ{\rm A}_{j}^{\Phi}\bigl(f(\sigma),t\bigl(\check{\eta}{}_{(j,i)}(\sigma,g)\bigr)\bigr)\circ{\rm T}_{\sigma}f\bigr)\,.

The anticipated transformation law now follows readily, again, as in the said proof.
∎

Proposition 4.32
.

For every
vertical
automorphism
\Phi\in{\rm Gauge}(\mathscr{P})
of a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
with connection 1-form
\Theta
, the shadow connection 1-form
\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}\in\Omega^{1}(\mathscr{F},{\rm V}\mathscr{F})
satisfies the identity

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)^{*}\>\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}^{\Phi}=\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}\,,

in which
\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}^{\Phi}
is the shadow connection induced by the gauge transform
\Theta^{\Phi}
, and in which the pullback along
\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)
is explicitly given by

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)^{*}\>\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}^{\Phi}\equiv{\rm T}\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigr)^{-1}\circ\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}^{\Phi}\circ{\rm T}\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigr)\,.

Proof.

In the light of Prop.
3.9
, the gauge-transformed connection 1-form
\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}^{\Phi}
satisfies

\displaystyle\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}^{\Phi}\circ{\rm T}\mathscr{D}={\rm T}\mathscr{D}\circ\Theta^{\Phi}\,.

Upon sandwiching it between
{\rm T}(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi))^{-1}
and
{\rm T}\Phi
and using the tangent variant of the intertwiner property (
117
) of
\mathscr{D}
:

\displaystyle{\rm T}\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigr)\circ{\rm T}\mathscr{D}={\rm T}\mathscr{D}\circ{\rm T}\Phi\,,

alongside definition (
175
), we obtain

(183)

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)^{*}\>\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}^{\Phi}\circ{\rm T}\mathscr{D}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm T}\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigr)^{-1}\circ\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}^{\Phi}\circ{\rm T}\mathscr{D}\circ{\rm T}\Phi={\rm T}\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigr)^{-1}\circ{\rm T}\mathscr{D}\circ\Theta^{\Phi}\circ{\rm T}\Phi={\rm T}\mathscr{D}\circ{\rm T}\Phi^{-1}\circ\Theta^{\Phi}\circ{\rm T}\Phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm T}\mathscr{D}\circ\Theta=\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}\circ{\rm T}\mathscr{D}\,,

where the last equality uses Eq. (
58
) again. The statement of the proposition now follows by the surjectivity of
{\rm T}\mathscr{D}
.
∎

4.5.
Covariant derivatives

Definition 4.33
.

The
covariant derivative
of a section
\varphi\in\Gamma(\mathscr{F})
of the shadow bundle
\mathscr{F}
of a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}

relative to
a connection 1-form
\Theta
on
\mathscr{P}
is the
{\mathbb{R}}
-linear mapping

\displaystyle\nabla^{\Theta}_{\cdot}\varphi\hskip-2.0pt:\Gamma({\rm T}\Sigma)\longrightarrow\Gamma({\rm V}\mathscr{F}),\mathcal{V}\longmapsto\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}\circ{\rm T}\varphi(\mathcal{V})\equiv\nabla^{\Theta}_{\mathcal{V}}\varphi\,.

Proposition 4.34
.

For a section
\varphi\in\Gamma(\mathscr{F})
of the shadow bundle
\mathscr{F}
of a principaloid bundle
\mathscr{P}
, and a gauge transformation
\Phi\in{\rm Gauge}(\mathscr{P})
of
\mathscr{P}
, let
\varphi^{\Phi}
denote the corresponding gauge transform of
\varphi
:

\displaystyle\varphi^{\Phi}\equiv\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\circ\varphi\,.

The covariant derivative of
\varphi
relative to a connection 1-form
\Theta
on
\mathscr{P}
transforms
covariantly
as

\displaystyle\nabla^{\Theta^{\Phi}}_{\cdot}\bigl(\varphi^{\Phi}\bigr)={\rm T}\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigr)\circ\nabla^{\Theta}_{\cdot}\varphi\,.

Proof.

Upon invoking Prop.
4.32
, we calculate directly:

(185)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\Theta^{\Phi}}_{\cdot}\bigl(\varphi^{\Phi}\bigr)\equiv\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}^{\Phi}\circ{\rm T}(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\circ\varphi)={\rm T}\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigr)\circ\bigl({\rm T}\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigr)^{-1}\circ{}^{\Phi}\hskip-2.0pt\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}\circ{\rm T}\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigr)\bigr)\circ{\rm T}\varphi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm T}\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigr)\circ\Theta_{\mathscr{F}}\circ{\rm T}\varphi\equiv{\rm T}\bigl(\mathscr{F}_{*}(\Phi)\bigr)\circ\nabla^{\Theta}_{\cdot}\varphi\,.

∎

Appendix A
Conventions on maps and differential forms

Convention A.1
.

Let
M_{A}
and
N_{A}
, with
A\in\{1,2\}
, be smooth manifolds and
f_{A}\hskip-2.0pt:M_{A}\to N_{A}
smooth maps. We write

\displaystyle f_{1}\times f_{2}\hskip-2.0pt:M_{1}\times M_{2}\longrightarrow N_{1}\times N_{2},\ (m_{1},m_{2})\longmapsto\bigl(f_{1}(m_{1}),f_{2}(m_{2})\bigr)\,.

Convention A.2
.

Let
M
and
N_{A}
, with
A\in\{1,2\}
, be manifolds and
f_{A}\hskip-2.0pt:M\to N_{A}
smooth maps. We write

\displaystyle(f_{1},f_{2})\hskip-2.0pt:M\longrightarrow N_{1}\times N_{2},\ m\longmapsto\bigl(f_{1}(m),f_{2}(m)\bigr)\,.

-valued

-form on

Definition A.4
.

Let
M
be a smooth manifold. A (
smooth
)
foliation
on
M
is an integrable subbundle
\mathscr{F}\hookrightarrow{\rm T}M
, i.e., a subbundle with the
Frobenius property

\displaystyle[\Gamma(\mathscr{F}),\Gamma(\mathscr{F})]\subset\Gamma(\mathscr{F})\,.

Appendix B
Conventions on Lie groupoids

Definition B.1
.

A
groupoid
is a small category with all morphisms invertible. Thus, it is a septuple
{\rm{\bf Gr}}=({\rm Ob}{\rm{\bf Gr}},{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}},s,t,{\rm Id},{\rm Inv},{\rm m}\equiv.)
composed of a pair of sets:

•

the
object set

{\rm Ob}{\rm{\bf Gr}}
;

•

the
arrow set

{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}
,

and a quintuple of
structure maps
:

•

the
source map

s\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\to{\rm Ob}{\rm{\bf Gr}}
;

•

the
target map

t\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\to{\rm Ob}{\rm{\bf Gr}}
;

•

the
unit map

{\rm Id}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Ob}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\to{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}},\ m\mapsto{\rm Id}_{m}
;

•

the
inverse map

{\rm Inv}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\to{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}},\ g\mapsto{\rm Inv}(g)\equiv g^{-1}
;

•

the
multiplication map

{\rm m}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\,{}_{s}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\to{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}},\ (g,h)\mapsto{\rm m}(g,h)\equiv g.h
,

defined in terms of the subset
{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\,{}_{s}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}
of composable morphisms,

\displaystyle{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\,{}_{s}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}=\{\ (g,h)\in{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\times{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\quad|\quad s(g)=t(h)\ \}\equiv{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\times_{{\rm Ob}{\rm{\bf Gr}}}{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\,,

and subject to the conditions (in force whenever the expressions are well-defined):

(i)

s(g.h)=s(h),\ t(g.h)=t(g)
;

(ii)

(g.h).k=g.(h.k)
;

(iii)

{\rm Id}_{t(g)}.g=g=g.{\rm Id}_{s(g)}

;

(iv)

s(g^{-1})=t(g),\ t(g^{-1})=s(g),\ g.g^{-1}={\rm Id}_{t(g)},\ g^{-1}.g={\rm Id}_{s(g)}
.

A
morphism
between two groupoids
{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{A},\ A\in\{1,2\}
is a functor
\Phi\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{1}\to{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{2}
.

A
Lie groupoid
is a groupoid whose object and arrow sets are smooth manifolds, whose structure maps are smooth, and whose source and target maps are surjective submersions. A morphism between two Lie groupoids is a functor between them with smooth object and morphism components.

We shall represent a Lie groupoid
{\rm{\bf Gr}}
by a (non-commutative) diagram

(186)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.368pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 199.3576pt\raise 7.30286pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

Remark B.2
.

From now onwards, we shall mostly use the shorthand notation
{\rm Ob}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\equiv M
and
{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}\equiv\mathscr{G}
, and, furthermore, write
\mathscr{G}\sim{\rm{\bf Gr}}
.

Example B.3
.

A Lie group
{\rm G}
can be viewed as a Lie groupoid
(\{\bullet\},{\rm G},\bullet,\bullet,\bullet\mapsto e,{\rm Inv}_{\rm G},\cdot)
with the singleton
\{\bullet\}
as the object manifold.

Example B.4
.

An important example of a Lie groupoid is provided by the
pair groupoid

{\rm Pair}(M)
of a smooth manifold
{\rm Ob}\,({\rm Pair}(M))=M
, with the object manifold
M
and the arrow manifold
{\rm Mor}\,({\rm Pair}(M))=M\times M
, the source map
s={\rm pr}_{2}
and the target map
t={\rm pr}_{1}
given by the canonical projections, the composition of morphisms

\displaystyle{\rm m}\hskip-2.0pt:(M\times M)\,{}_{{\rm pr}_{2}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{{\rm pr}_{1}}\hskip-1.0pt(M\times M)\longrightarrow M\times M,\ \bigl((m_{3},m_{2}),(m_{2},m_{1})\bigr)\longmapsto(m_{2},m_{1})\,,

the unit map

\displaystyle{\rm Id}\hskip-2.0pt:M\longrightarrow M\times M,\ m\longmapsto(m,m)\,,

and the inversion map

\displaystyle{\rm Inv}\hskip-2.0pt:M\times M\longrightarrow M\times M,\ (m_{2},m_{1})\longmapsto(m_{1},m_{2})\,.

The pair groupoid contains, as a proper Lie subgroupoid, the
Lie groupoid of

M
obtained through restriction of the arrow manifold to the diagonal
M\times_{M}M\equiv M
.

Whenever
M
is the total space of a surjective submersion
\pi_{M}\hskip-2.0pt:M\to\Sigma
, the pair groupoid
{\rm Pair}(M)
contains, as a proper Lie subgroupoid, the
\Sigma
-fibred pair groupoid

{\rm Pair}_{\Sigma}(M)
of
M
, with the arrow manifold

\displaystyle{\rm Mor}\,({\rm Pair}_{\Sigma}(M))=M\times_{\Sigma}M\equiv\{\ (m_{1},m_{2})\in M\times M\quad|\quad\pi_{M}(m_{1})=\pi_{M}(m_{2})\ \}\,.

Example B.5
.

Another Lie groupoid of relevance is the
action groupoid

{\rm G}\,{\hskip-1.13791pt\ltimes\hskip-1.42271pt}_{\lambda}M
associated with a smooth action

\displaystyle\lambda\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm G}\times M\longrightarrow M,\ m\longmapsto\lambda_{g}(m)\equiv g\vartriangleright m\equiv g.m

of a Lie group
{\rm G}
on a smooth manifold
M
, with the object manifold
{\rm Ob}\,({\rm G}\,{\hskip-1.13791pt\ltimes\hskip-1.42271pt}_{\lambda}M)=M
and the arrow manifold
{\rm Mor}\,({\rm G}\,{\hskip-1.13791pt\ltimes\hskip-1.42271pt}_{\lambda}M)={\rm G}\times M
, the source map
s={\rm pr}_{2}
given by the canonical projection, the target map
t=\lambda
given by the smooth (left) action
\lambda
, the composition of morphisms

\displaystyle{\rm m}\hskip-2.0pt:({\rm G}\times M)\,{}_{{\rm pr}_{2}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{\lambda}\hskip-1.0pt({\rm G}\times M)\longrightarrow{\rm G}\times M,\ \bigl((h,g.m),(g,m)\bigr)\longmapsto(h\cdot g,m)=:(h,g.m).(g,m)\,,

the unit map (
e\in{\rm G}
is the group unit)

\displaystyle{\rm Id}\hskip-2.0pt:M\longrightarrow{\rm G}\times M,\ m\longmapsto{\rm Id}_{m}=(e,m)\,,

and – finally – the inversion map

\displaystyle{\rm Inv}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm G}\times M\longrightarrow{\rm G}\times M,\ (g,m)\longmapsto\bigl(g^{-1},g.m\bigr)=:{\rm Inv}(g,m)\equiv(g,m)^{-1}\,.

The action groupoid can be endowed with the structure of a
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
-space in several ways, amidst which there is a distinguished one: The (left) action of
{\rm G}\times{\rm G}
intertwined with
\lambda\circ({\rm pr}_{2}\times{\rm id}_{M})
by
s
and with
\lambda\circ({\rm pr}_{1}\times{\rm id}_{M})
by
t
is given by the formula

(187)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mathscr{L}\hskip-2.0pt:({\rm G}\times{\rm G})\times({\rm G}\times M)\longrightarrow{\rm G}\times M,\ \bigl((h_{1},h_{2}),(g,m)\bigr)\longmapsto\bigl(h_{1}\cdot g\cdot h_{2}^{-1},h_{2}.m\bigr)\,.

This can equivalently be seen as an ‘adjoint’ action of
{\rm G}\,{\hskip-1.13791pt\ltimes\hskip-1.42271pt}_{\lambda}M
on itself.

Example B.6
.

Let
(M,\mathscr{G},s,t,{\rm Id},{\rm Inv},{\rm m})
be a Lie groupoid. We call it a
symplectic groupoid
, after Refs.
[
W87
,
CDW87
]
, whenever there exists a closed non-degenerate 2-form
\omega\in\Omega^{2}(\mathscr{G})
which makes
(\mathscr{G},\omega)
a symplectic manifold, and such that the graph
{\rm graph}({\rm m})
of
{\rm m}
is a lagrangean submanifold in
(\mathscr{G}^{\times 3},({\rm pr}_{1}^{*}+{\rm pr}_{2}^{*}-{\rm pr}_{3}^{*})\omega)
. As shown in Ref.
[
CDW87
, Thm. 1.1, Chap. II]
, the object manifold
M
of a symplectic groupoid carries a canonical Poisson bivector
\Pi\in\Gamma(\bigwedge^{2}{\rm T}^{*}M)
relative to which the source map is Poisson and the target map is anti-Poisson.

Example B.7
.

The geometry of the cotangent bundle
{\rm T}^{*}{\rm G}
of a Lie group
{\rm G}
combines structures from Examples
B.5
and
B.6
. Indeed, its global trivialisation,
{\rm T}^{*}{\rm G}\cong{\rm G}\times\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, turns it into the arrow manifold of the action groupoid
{\rm G}{\hskip-1.13791pt\ltimes\hskip-1.42271pt}_{{\rm T}_{e}{\rm Ad}^{*}}\mathfrak{g}^{*}
(relative to the coadjoint action), and the choice of the Poincaré 2-form on it (i.e., the exterior derivative of the tautological 1-form) as the symplectic form gives rise to a symplectic groupoid, see
[
CDW87
]
.

Example B.8
.

Let
({\mathbb{V}},B,{\mathbb{R}}^{N},\pi_{\mathbb{V}})
be a (real) vector bundle of rank
N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\times}
with base
B
. The
general linear groupoid of

{\mathbb{V}}
is the Lie groupoid
{\rm GL}({\mathbb{V}})
with the object manifold
{\rm Ob}\,{\rm GL}({\mathbb{V}})=B
, the arrow manifold

\displaystyle{\rm Mor}\,{\rm GL}({\mathbb{V}})=\bigsqcup_{x,y\in B}\,{\rm Iso}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{V}}_{x},{\mathbb{V}}_{y})\,,

the source and arrow maps

\displaystyle s\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ \bigsqcup_{x,y\in B}\,{\rm Iso}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{V}}_{x},{\mathbb{V}}_{y})\longrightarrow B,\ (\chi_{u,v},u,v)\longmapsto u\,,

(189)

\displaystyle t\

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle\ \bigsqcup_{x,y\in B}\,{\rm Iso}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{V}}_{x},{\mathbb{V}}_{y})\longrightarrow B,\ (\chi_{u,v},u,v)\longmapsto v\,,

the composition of morphisms

\displaystyle{\rm m}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Mor}\,{\rm GL}({\mathbb{V}})\,{}_{s}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt{\rm Mor}\,{\rm GL}({\mathbb{V}})\longrightarrow{\rm Mor}\,{\rm GL}({\mathbb{V}}),\ \bigl((\chi_{y,z},y,z),(\chi_{x,y},x,y)\bigr)\longmapsto(\chi_{y,z}\circ\chi_{x,y},x,z)\,,

the unit map

\displaystyle{\rm Id}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Ob}\,{\rm GL}({\mathbb{V}})\longrightarrow{\rm Mor}\,{\rm GL}({\mathbb{V}}),\ x\longmapsto({\rm id}_{{\mathbb{V}}_{x}},x,x)\,,

and the inversion map

\displaystyle{\rm Inv}\hskip-2.0pt:\bigsqcup_{x,y\in B}\,{\rm Iso}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{V}}_{x},{\mathbb{V}}_{y})\circlearrowleft,\ (\chi_{u,v},u,v)\longmapsto(\chi_{u,v}^{-1},v,u)\,.

Definition B.9
.

Let
{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{a},\ a\in\{1,2,3\}
be Lie groupoids and let
\jmath\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{1}\to{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{2}
and
\pi\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{2}\to{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{3}
be Lie-groupoid morphisms. We say that the quintuple
({\rm{\bf Gr}}_{1},{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{2},{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{3},\jmath,\pi)
composes a
short exact sequence of Lie groupoids

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 5.87498pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.87498pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\boldsymbol{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.32773pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 34.32773pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm{\bf 68.57832pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\jmath}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 87.0443pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 87.0443pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern Gr}}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 120.19633pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 139.76086pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 139.76086pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern Gr}}_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 192.47743pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern if
\jmath
is a monomorphism,
\pi
is an epimorphism, and the
\pi
-preimage of the identity bisection
{\rm Id}({\rm Ob}{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{3})\subset{\rm Mor}{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{3}
is canonically isomorphic to the (faithful)
\jmath
-image of
{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{1}
in
{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{2}
. If, moreover, there exists a Lie-groupoid morphism
\sigma\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{3}\to{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{2}
such that the identity
\pi\circ\sigma={\rm id}_{{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{3}}
obtains, then we say that the short exact sequence
splits
, and call it a
split short exact sequence
(
of Lie groupoids
).

Definition B.10

(
[
MoMr03
]
)
.

Let
{\rm{\bf Gr}}=(M,\mathscr{G},s,t,{\rm Id},{\rm Inv},.)
be a Lie groupoid. A (
global
)
bisection of

{\rm{\bf Gr}}
is a section
\beta\hskip-2.0pt:M\to\mathscr{G}
of the surjective submersion
s\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{G}\to M
such that the induced map

\displaystyle t_{*}\beta\equiv t\circ\beta\hskip-2.0pt:M\longrightarrow M

is a diffeomorphism. Equivalently, it is a submanifold
S\subset\mathscr{G}
with the property that both restrictions:
s\big|_{S}
and
t\big|_{S}
are diffeomorphisms. We shall denote the set of bisections as

\displaystyle{\rm Bisec({\bf Gr})}\,.

A
local bisection of

{\rm{\bf Gr}}
is a local section
\beta\hskip-2.0pt:O\to\mathscr{G}
of
s
over an open subset
O\subset M
, such that the induced map

\displaystyle t_{*}\beta\equiv t\circ\beta\hskip-2.0pt:O\longrightarrow t\circ\beta(O)

is a diffeomorphism. We shall denote the set of local bisections as
{\rm Bisec}_{\rm loc}({\rm{\bf Gr}})
.

Definition B.11
.

The
group of bisections of

{\rm{\bf Gr}}
is the canonical structure of a group on
{\rm Bisec({\bf Gr})}
. Its binary operation is defined as

\displaystyle\cdot\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Bisec({\bf Gr})}\times{\rm Bisec({\bf Gr})}\longrightarrow{\rm Bisec({\bf Gr})},\ (\beta_{2},\beta_{1})\longmapsto\beta_{2}\bigl(t\circ\beta_{1}(\cdot)\bigr).\beta_{1}(\cdot)\equiv\beta_{2}\cdot\beta_{1}\,.

The neutral element is
{\rm Id}
, termed the
unit bisection
in the present context, and the corresponding inverse is

\displaystyle{\rm Inv}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Bisec({\bf Gr})}\longrightarrow{\rm Bisec({\bf Gr})},\ \beta\longmapsto{\rm Inv}\circ\beta\circ\bigl(t_{*}\beta\bigr)^{-1}\equiv\beta^{-1}\,,

where the inverse on the right-hand side is the one in
{\rm Diff}(M)
.

Remark B.12
.

The binary operation of
{\rm Bisec({\bf Gr})}
admits a simple pictorial representation in Fig.
1

Figure 1
.
The binary operation on
{\rm Bisec({\bf Gr})}
.

Remark B.13
.

For a fixed Lie groupoid, we shall often use the shorthand notation
{\rm Bisec({\bf Gr})}\equiv{\mathbb{B}}
.

Definition B.14
.

The
left regular action of

{\mathbb{B}}
(
on itself
) is

\displaystyle\ell\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathbb{B}}\times{\mathbb{B}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{B}},\ (\gamma,\beta)\longmapsto\gamma\cdot\beta\equiv\ell_{\gamma}(\beta)\,,

and the
right regular action of

{\mathbb{B}}
(
on itself
) is

\displaystyle\wp\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathbb{B}}\times{\mathbb{B}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{B}},\ (\beta,\gamma)\longmapsto\beta\cdot\gamma\equiv\wp_{\gamma}(\beta)\,.

The
adjoint action of

{\mathbb{B}}

on itself
is

\displaystyle c\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathbb{B}}\times{\mathbb{B}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{B}},\ (\gamma,\beta)\longmapsto\gamma\cdot\beta\cdot\gamma^{-1}\equiv c_{\gamma}(\beta)\,.

Definition B.15
.

The
shadow action of

{\mathbb{B}}

on

M
is

\displaystyle t_{*}\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathbb{B}}\times M\longrightarrow M,\ (\beta,m)\longmapsto t\bigl(\beta(m)\bigr)\,.

By the usual abuse of the notation, we shall refer by the same name to and use the same symbol for the group homomorphism

(190)

\displaystyle t_{*}\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathbb{B}}\longrightarrow{\rm Diff}(M)\,.

Definition B.16
.

The
left-multiplication of

\mathscr{G}

by

{\mathbb{B}}
is the left action

\displaystyle L\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathbb{B}}\times\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G},\ \bigl(\beta,g\bigr)\longmapsto\beta\bigl(t\bigl(g\bigr)\bigr).g\equiv L_{\beta}\bigl(g\bigr)\equiv\beta\vartriangleright g\,.

The
right-multiplication of

\mathscr{G}

by

{\mathbb{B}}
is the right action

\displaystyle R\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{G}\times{\mathbb{B}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G},\ \bigl(g,\beta\bigr)\longmapsto g.\bigl(\beta^{-1}\bigl(s\bigl(g\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}\equiv R{}_{\beta}\bigl(g\bigr)\equiv g\vartriangleleft\beta\,.

The
conjugation of

\mathscr{G}

by

{\mathbb{B}}
is the
left
action

\displaystyle C\hskip-2.0pt:{\mathbb{B}}\times\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G},\ (\beta,g)\longmapsto\beta\bigl(t(g)\bigr).g.\beta\bigl(s(g)\bigr)^{-1}\equiv C_{\beta}(g)\equiv\beta\vartriangleright g\vartriangleleft\beta^{-1}\,.

Remark B.17
.

Taking into account the significance of the right-multiplication in the present paper, we simplify its definition for the sake of transparency. Thus, we find

\displaystyle g\vartriangleleft\beta\equiv g.\beta\bigl((t_{*}\beta)^{-1}\bigl(s(g)\bigr)\bigr)\,.

Appendix C
Groupoid modules and principal groupoid bundles

Definition C.1
.

Given a Lie groupoid
{\rm{\bf Gr}}=(M,\mathscr{G},s,t,{\rm Id},{\rm Inv},.)
, a
right-
\mathscr{G}
-module space
is a triple
(X,\mu,\varrho)
composed of

•

a smooth manifold
X
;

•

a smooth map
\mu\hskip-2.0pt:X\to M
, called the (
right
)
moment map
;

•

a smooth map

\displaystyle\varrho\hskip-2.0pt:X{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow X,\ (x,g)\longmapsto\varrho(x,g)\equiv\varrho_{g}(x)\equiv x\blacktriangleleft g\,,

termed the (
right
)
action
(
map
),

subject to the relations (in force whenever the expressions are well-defined):

(GrM1)

\mu(x\blacktriangleleft g)=s(g)
;

(GrM2)

x\blacktriangleleft{\rm Id}_{\mu(x)}=x
;

(GrM3)

(x\blacktriangleleft g)\blacktriangleleft h=x\blacktriangleleft(g.h)
.

Similarly, a
left-
\mathscr{G}
-module space
is a triple
(X,\mu,\lambda)
composed of

•

a smooth manifold
X
;

•

a smooth map
\mu\hskip-2.0pt:X\to M
, called the (
left
)
moment map
;

•

a smooth map

\displaystyle\lambda\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{G}{}_{s}\hskip-2.0pt\times_{\mu}\hskip-1.0ptX\longrightarrow X,\ (g,x)\longmapsto\lambda(g,x)\equiv\lambda_{g}(x)\equiv g\blacktriangleright x\,,

termed the (
left
)
action
(
map
),

subject to the relations (in force whenever the expressions are well-defined):

(GlM1)

\mu(g\blacktriangleright x)=t(g)
;

(GlM2)

{\rm Id}_{\mu(x)}\blacktriangleright x=x
;

(GlM3)

h\blacktriangleright(g\blacktriangleright x)=(h.g)\blacktriangleright x
.

A right action
\varrho
is termed
free
iff the following implication obtains:

\displaystyle x\blacktriangleleft g=x\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad g={\rm Id}_{\mu(x)}\,,

so that, in particular, the
isotropy group

\mathscr{G}_{m}=s^{-1}(\{m\})\cap t^{-1}(\{m\})
of
m\in M
acts freely (in the usual sense) on the fibre
\mu^{-1}(\{m\})
. A free left action is defined analogously.

A right action
\varrho
is termed
transitive
iff for any two points
x,x^{\prime}\in X
there exists an arrow
g\in\mathscr{G}
such that
x^{\prime}=x\blacktriangleleft g
. A transitive left action is defined analogously.

Let
{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{A},\ A\in\{1,2\}
be a pair of Lie groupoids and let
(X_{A},\mu_{A},\varrho^{A})
be the respective
right-
\mathscr{G}_{A}
-module spaces. A
morphism
between the latter is a pair
(\Theta,\Phi)
consisting of a smooth manifold map
\Theta\hskip-2.0pt:X_{1}\to X_{2}
together with a functor
\Phi\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{1}\to{\rm{\bf Gr}}_{2}
for which the following diagrams commute

(191)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.90898pt\raise 5.39168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Theta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.17912pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.76515pt\raise-21.42184pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 53.31801pt\raise-42.8437pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 53.31801pt\raise-42.8437pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M_{2}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

(192)

(193)

(194)

There are two canonical left-module structures associated with the groupoid itself, which we stumble upon in our considerations. The first of them is indicated in

Example C.2
.

The object manifold
M
of a Lie groupoid
{\rm{\bf Gr}}=(M,\mathscr{G},s,t,{\rm Id},{\rm Inv},.)
carries a natural structure of a left-
\mathscr{G}
-module space given by

\displaystyle\bigl(M,{\rm id}_{M},{\rm Aim}^{\mathscr{G}}\bigr)\,,

where the action reads

\displaystyle{\rm Aim}^{\mathscr{G}}:=t\circ{\rm pr}_{1}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{G}{}_{s}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{{\rm id}_{M}}\hskip-1.0ptM\longrightarrow M,\ \bigl(g,s(g)\bigr)\longmapsto t(g)\equiv{\rm Aim}^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}\bigl(s(g)\bigr)\,.

Another one is described in the following

Example C.3
.

We may regard
\mathscr{G}
as a left-
\mathscr{G}^{\times 2}
-module space for the
product groupoid

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 7.80692pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm{\bf Gr}}^{\times 2}\qquad:\hskip-56.9055pt}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 74.13188pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Mor}\bigl({\rm{\bf Gr}}^{\times 2}\bigr)=\mathscr{G}^{\times 2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 172.69595pt\raise 8.74503pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.75891pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{s^{\times 2}:=s\times s}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 229.36096pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 173.82617pt\raise-8.74503pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.75891pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t^{\times 2}:=t\times t}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 229.36096pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 229.36096pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M^{\times 2}={\rm Ob}\bigl({\rm{\bf Gr}}^{\times 2}\bigr)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

with the composition map defined as

\displaystyle\bigl(g{}_{1},g{}_{2}\bigr)\bullet\bigl(h{}_{1},h{}_{2}\bigr):=\bigl(g{}_{1}.h{}_{1},g{}_{2}.h{}_{2}\bigr)\,,

the identity map

\displaystyle{\rm Id}_{(m_{1},m_{2})}=({\rm Id}_{m_{1}},{\rm Id}_{m_{2}})\,,

and the inversion map

\displaystyle{\rm Inv}(g_{1},g_{2})=\bigl({\rm Inv}(g_{1}),{\rm Inv}(g_{2})\bigr)\,.

The left-
\mathscr{G}^{\times 2}
-module space

\displaystyle(\mathscr{G},\mu_{\rm c},{\rm c})

is the triple composed of the smooth manifold
\mathscr{G}
, the moment map

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm c}\equiv(t,s)\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow M^{\times 2}\,,

defining the set

\displaystyle\mathscr{G}^{\times 2}{}_{s^{\times 2}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{\mu_{\rm c}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}:=\bigl\{\ \bigl((h_{1},h_{2}),g\bigr)\in\mathscr{G}^{\times 3}\quad|\quad s^{\times 2}(h_{1},h_{2})=\mu_{\rm c}(g)\ \bigr\}\,,

and the action

\displaystyle{\rm c}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{G}^{\times 2}{}_{s^{\times 2}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{\mu_{\rm c}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G},\ \bigl((h_{1},h_{2}),g\bigr)\longmapsto h_{1}.g.h_{2}^{-1}\,.

Given these data for
{\rm{\bf Gr}}={\rm G}\,{\hskip-1.13791pt\ltimes\hskip-1.42271pt}_{\lambda}M
, we may rewrite Eq. (
187
) as

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}\bigl((h_{1},h_{2}),(g,m)\bigr)=(h_{1},g.m).(g,m).\bigl(h_{2}^{-1},h_{2}.m\bigr)={\rm c}\bigl(\bigl(\bigl(h_{1},t(g,m)\bigr),\bigl(h_{2},s(g,m)\bigr)\bigr),(g,m)\bigr)\,.

(195)

Note also that the above left-
\mathscr{G}^{\times 2}
-module structure on
\mathscr{G}
is, in fact, a combination of two ‘chiral’
\mathscr{G}
-module structures on the same manifold: the left-
\mathscr{G}
-module structure

\displaystyle(\mathscr{G},t,l\equiv.)\,,

with the
left-fibred action

(196)

\displaystyle l\bigl(h,g\bigr)\equiv l_{h}\bigl(g\bigr)=h.g\,,

and the right-
\mathscr{G}
-module structure

(197)

\displaystyle(\mathscr{G},s,r\equiv.)\,,

with the
right-fibred action

(198)

\displaystyle r\bigl(g,h\bigr)\equiv r_{h}\bigl(g\bigr)=g.h\,.

Example C.4
.

Let
{\mathbb{E}}=(\mathcal{E},M,{\mathbb{R}}^{N},\pi_{\mathcal{E}},\alpha_{\mathcal{E}},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathcal{E}})
be a Lie algebroid over the object manifold of a Lie groupoid
{\rm{\bf Gr}}=(M,\mathscr{G},s,t,{\rm Id},{\rm Inv},.)
. A
linear representation of

{\rm{\bf Gr}}
in
{\mathbb{E}}
is a Lie-groupoid morphism
\varrho\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm{\bf Gr}}\to{\rm GL}({\mathbb{V}})
, see Example
B.8
. It endows the vector bundle
{\mathbb{V}}
with the structure of a left-
\mathscr{G}
-module space, with moment map
\mu_{\mathbb{V}}=\pi_{\mathbb{V}}
and action

\displaystyle\varrho^{\mathbb{V}}\equiv{\rm ev}\hskip-2.0pt:\bigsqcup_{x,y\in B}\,{\rm Iso}_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{V}}_{x},{\mathbb{V}}_{y}){}_{s}\hskip-1.0pt\times_{\mu_{\mathbb{V}}}\hskip-3.0pt{\mathbb{V}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{V}},\ \bigl((\chi_{x,y},x,y),v\bigr)\longmapsto\chi_{x,y}(v)\,.

Definition C.5
.

[
Mo91
, Sec. 1.2]

A
right principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle
is a quintuple
({\rm\breve{P}},\Sigma,\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}},\mu,\varrho)
composed of a pair of smooth manifolds:

•

the
total space

{\rm\breve{P}}
;

•

the
base

\Sigma
,

and a triple of smooth maps:

•

a surjective submersion
\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm\breve{P}}\to\Sigma
, termed the
base projection
;

•

the
moment map

\mu\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm\breve{P}}\to M
;

•

the
action
(
map
)
\varrho\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm\breve{P}}{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\to{\rm\breve{P}}
,

with the following properties:

(PGr1)

({\rm\breve{P}},\mu,\varrho)
is a right
\mathscr{G}
-module space;

(PGr2)

\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}
is
\mathscr{G}
-invariant in the sense made precise by the following commutative diagram (in which
{\rm pr}_{1}
is the canonical projection)

(PGr3)

the map

\displaystyle({\rm pr}_{1},\varrho)\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm\breve{P}}{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow{\rm\breve{P}}\hskip 1.0pt{}_{\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}}\hskip-4.0pt\,\,{\rm\breve{P}},\ (p,g)\longmapsto(p,p\blacktriangleleft g)

is a diffeomorphism, so that
\mathscr{G}
acts freely and transitively on
\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}
-fibres. The smooth inverse of
({\rm pr}_{1},\varrho)
takes the form

\displaystyle({\rm pr}_{1},\varrho)^{-1}=:({\rm pr}_{1},\phi_{{\rm\breve{P}}})\,,\qquad\qquad\phi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm\breve{P}}\hskip 1.0pt{}_{\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}}\hskip-4.0pt\,\,{\rm\breve{P}}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G}

and
\phi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}
is called the
division map
.

The Lie groupoid
\mathscr{G}
is termed the
structure groupoid
of
{\rm\breve{P}}
.

\mathscr{G}
-bundle by the simplified diagram

in which the remaining structure is implicit.

Let
({\rm\breve{P}}{}_{A},\Sigma,\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}{}_{A}},\mu_{A},\varrho^{A}),\ A\in\{1,2\}
be a pair of right principal

-bundles over a common base

9

In Ref.
[

, Sec. 5.7]
, these morphisms were termed “equivariant maps”.
between the two bundles is a morphism
(\Theta,{\rm Id}_{\rm{\bf Gr}})
between the corresponding right

-modules

which maps

-fibres to
\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}{}_{2}}
-fibres.
