3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 101.33185pt\raise-28.56386pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower-3.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.2666pt\raise-12.7333pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.00403pt\raise-28.45273pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-35.84163pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 40.2701pt\raise-35.84163pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm Ob}{\rm{\bf Gr}}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 94.72073pt\raise-35.84163pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

Customarily, principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundles are taken to be right
\mathscr{G}
-modules, and so whenever the term is used without a qualifier, it is to be understood that we are dealing with a right principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle.

Example C.6
.

[
MoMr03
, Remark 5.34(1)]

There is a canonical structure of a principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle on
{\rm{\bf Gr}}
, given by

\displaystyle U_{\rm{\bf Gr}}:=(\mathscr{G},M,t,s,r)\,,

where
r
is the right-fibred action (
198
). We call this structure the
unit bundle of

{\rm{\bf Gr}}
.

Axioms (PGr1) and (PGr2) from Def
C.5
are self-evident, and so it remains to verify property (PGr3): The map

\displaystyle({\rm pr}_{1},r)\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{G}{}_{s}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G}{}_{t}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G},\ (g,h)\longmapsto(g,g.h)

admits the smooth inverse

\displaystyle({\rm pr}_{1},\phi_{U_{\rm{\bf Gr}}})\hskip-2.0pt:\mathscr{G}{}_{t}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G}{}_{s}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G},\ (g,h)\longmapsto\bigl(g,g^{-1}.h\bigr)\,.

Example C.7
.

[
MoMr03
, Remark 5.34(2),(3)]

A
trivial principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle
over
\Sigma
is the pullback
f^{*}U_{\rm{\bf Gr}}
of the unit bundle
U_{\rm{\bf Gr}}
along an arbitrary smooth map
f\hskip-2.0pt:\Sigma\to M
, that is the principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle

\displaystyle f^{*}U_{\rm{\bf Gr}}:=\bigl(f^{*}\mathscr{G},\Sigma,{\rm pr}_{1},s\circ{\rm pr}_{2},f^{*}\varrho\bigr)

with
f^{*}\mathscr{G}=\Sigma\,{}_{f}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}
and

\displaystyle f^{*}\varrho\hskip-2.0pt:f^{*}\mathscr{G}\hskip 1.0pt{}_{s\circ{\rm pr}_{2}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-4.0pt\hskip 2.0pt\mathscr{G}\longrightarrow f^{*}\mathscr{G},\ \bigl((\sigma,g),h\bigr)\longmapsto(\sigma,g.h)\,.

Proposition/Definition C.8
.

[
MoMr03
, Remark 5.34(4)]

Let
({\rm\breve{P}},\Sigma,\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}},\mu,\varrho)
be a principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle. Given any open subset
O\subset\Sigma
endowed with a local section
s\hskip-2.0pt:O\to{\rm\breve{P}}
, define the corresponding
local moment map
as
\mu_{O}:=\mu\circ s
. The map
\tau_{O}\hskip-2.0pt:\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}^{-1}(O)\to\mu_{O}^{*}U_{\rm{\bf Gr}},\ p\mapsto\phi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}(s(\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}(p)),p)
is a diffeomorphism with inverse
\tau_{O}^{-1}\hskip-2.0pt:\mu_{O}^{*}U_{\rm{\bf Gr}}\to\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}}^{-1}(O)),\ (\sigma,g)\mapsto s(\sigma)\blacktriangleleft g
. We call
\tau_{O}
a
local trivialisation
of
{\rm\breve{P}}
over
O
. Thus, every principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle is locally trivial.

Corollary/Definition C.9
.

Let
({\rm\breve{P}},\Sigma,\pi_{{\rm\breve{P}}},\mu,\varrho)
be a principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle, and let
\mathcal{O}\equiv\{O_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be an open cover of its base
\Sigma
endowed with local sections
s_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:O_{i}\to{\rm\breve{P}}
. The bundle admits a model

(200)

\displaystyle{\rm\breve{P}}\cong\bigsqcup_{i\in I}\,\mu_{i}^{*}U_{\rm{\bf Gr}}/\sim_{l_{\gamma_{\cdot\cdot}}}\,,

in which the
\mu_{i}\equiv\mu\circ s_{i}\hskip-2.0pt:O_{i}\to{\rm\breve{P}}
are the local moment maps induced by the
s_{i}
, and the quotient is taken with respect to the equivalence relation

\displaystyle(\sigma,g,i)\sim(\sigma^{\prime},g^{\prime},j)\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\bigl(\ \sigma^{\prime}=\sigma\in O_{ij}\quad\land\quad g^{\prime}=\gamma_{ji}(\sigma).g\ \bigr)\,,

written in terms of the
transition 1-cocycle

\displaystyle\gamma_{ij}:=\phi_{\rm\breve{P}}\circ(s_{i},s_{j})\hskip-2.0pt:O_{ij}\longrightarrow\mathscr{G}\,.

The collection
(\{\gamma_{ij}\}_{(i,j)\in I^{\times 2}_{\mathcal{O}}},\{\mu_{i}\}_{i\in I})
is termed the
trivialising data
of
{\rm\breve{P}}
.

Proof.

A constructive proof relies on the identities

(201)

\displaystyle s\circ\gamma_{ij}=\mu_{j}\,,\qquad t\circ\gamma_{ij}=\mu_{i}\,,\qquad\gamma_{ij}.\gamma_{jk}\big|_{O_{ijk}}=\gamma_{ik}\big|_{O_{ijk}}

(following directly from Def.
C.5
), and is conceptually the same as that of the classic Clutching Theorem for fibre bundles. Its technical details can be found,
e.g.
, in Ref.
[
R04b
, Sec. 3.2]
.
∎

Theorem C.10

(The Godement Criterion)
.

[
Se64
,
F24
]

Let
M
be a smooth manifold and let
\sim
be an equivalence relation on M, with graph

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 10.05556pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.05556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{R}_{\sim}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.05557pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.5083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 38.5083pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M\times M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.02164pt\raise-19.79094pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.39583pt\raise-42.77454pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.73047pt\raise-19.79094pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm pr}_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 106.90376pt\raise-42.67912pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-8.39583pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 55.41103pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 106.7665pt\raise-49.9569pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,.

There exists a smooth structure on the quotient

\displaystyle M//\sim=\{\ [m]_{\sim}\quad|\quad m\in M\ \}\,,

compatible with the quotient topology, and such that
\pi\hskip-2.0pt:M\longrightarrow M//\sim
is a submersion, iff the graph
\mathcal{R}_{\sim}
is a proper submanifold of
M\times M
and the restriction of the projection
{\rm pr}_{1}\hskip-2.0pt:M\times M\longrightarrow M
to
\mathcal{R}_{\sim}
is a submersion.

Remark C.11
.

In virtue of the above Theorem, there exists a canonical diffeomorphism

\displaystyle{\rm\breve{P}}/\mathscr{G}\cong\Sigma

for every principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle
\,{\rm\breve{P}}
, with the left-hand side given by the smooth quotient of the total space
\,{\rm\breve{P}}\ni p_{1},p_{2}\,
by the equivalence relation

\displaystyle p_{1}\sim p_{2}\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\exists\ g\in\mathscr{G}\hskip-2.0pt:p_{2}=p_{1}\blacktriangleleft g\,.

Remark C.12
.

The definition of a principal
\mathscr{G}
-bundle can be viewed as a structural relation between three Lie groupoids. Indeed, we may rephrase it as a statement of existence, for a given surjective submersion
\pi_{\rm\breve{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm\breve{P}}\to\Sigma
and a (smooth) map
\mu\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm\breve{P}}\to M
, of

•

an action Lie groupoid
{\rm\breve{P}}{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\rtimes_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}
with
{\rm\breve{P}}
as the object manifold and
{\rm\breve{P}}{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}
as the arrow manifold, with
{\rm pr}_{1}
as the source map, a smooth map
\varrho\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm\breve{P}}{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\to{\rm\breve{P}}
as the target map,
({\rm id}_{\rm\breve{P}},{\rm Id}\circ\mu)
as the unit map,
(\varrho,{\rm Inv}\circ{\rm pr}_{2})
as the inverse map, and
(\varrho(p,g),h).(p,g)=(p,g.h)
as the multiplication map,

•

a Lie-groupoid morphism
\Phi_{\rm\breve{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm Pair}_{\Sigma}({\rm\breve{P}})\to{\rm{\bf Gr}}
with
\mu
as the object component and a smooth map
\phi_{\rm\breve{P}}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm\breve{P}}\times_{\Sigma}{\rm\breve{P}}\to\mathscr{G}
as the morphism component,

such that the Lie-groupoid morphism
\widetilde{\varrho}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm\breve{P}}{}_{\mu}\hskip-3.0pt\rtimes_{t}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}\to{\rm Pair}_{\Sigma}({\rm\breve{P}})
, with the object component
{\rm id}_{\rm\breve{P}}
and the morphism component
({\rm pr}_{1},\varrho)
, is invertible, with the inverse
\widetilde{\phi}{}_{\rm\breve{P}}
given by (
{\rm id}_{\rm\breve{P}}
on objects, and)
({\rm pr}_{1},\phi_{\rm\breve{P}})
on morphisms. Thus, the entire information on
{\rm\breve{P}}
is neatly encoded in the following commutative diagram in the category of Lie groupoids:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern in which the Lie-groupoid morphism
\widehat{\mu}
has
\mu
as the object component and
{\rm pr}_{2}
as the morphism component.

Definition C.13
.

Let
\hskip-5.0pt\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 10.18057pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.18057pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.40694pt\raise 1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.40694pt\raise-1.07639pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 24.40694pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M_{A}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\hskip-3.0pt,\ A\in\{1,2\}
be Lie groupoids. A
(\mathscr{G}_{1},\mathscr{G}_{2})
-bibundle
is a manifold
\widehat{P}
which carries the structure of a left
\mathscr{G}_{1}
-module
(\widehat{P},\mu_{1},\lambda_{1}\equiv\blacktriangleright)
and that of a right
\mathscr{G}_{2}
-module
(\widehat{P},\mu_{2},\rho_{2}\equiv\blacktriangleleft)
, such that the two actions commute and each moment map is invariant with respect to the other action, i.e., we have, for all
(g_{1},p,g_{2})\in\mathscr{G}_{1}{}_{s_{1}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{\mu_{1}}\hskip-1.0pt\widehat{P}{}_{\mu_{2}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{t_{2}}\hskip-1.0pt\mathscr{G}_{2}
,

•

(g_{1}\blacktriangleright p)\blacktriangleleft g_{2}=g_{1}\blacktriangleright(p\blacktriangleleft g_{2})
;

•

\mu_{1}(p\blacktriangleleft g_{2})=\mu_{1}(p)
;

•

\mu_{2}(g_{1}\blacktriangleright p)=\mu_{2}(p)
.

Whenever
(\widehat{P},M_{2},\mu_{2},\mu_{1},\lambda_{1})
is a (left) principal
\mathscr{G}_{1}
-bundle (with base
M_{2}\cong\widehat{P}/\mathscr{G}_{1}
), and
(\widehat{P},M_{1},\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\rho_{2})
is a (right) principal
\mathscr{G}_{2}
-bundle (with base
M_{1}\cong\widehat{P}/\mathscr{G}_{2}
), we call
\widehat{P}
a (
bi
)
principal
(\mathscr{G}_{1},\mathscr{G}_{2})
-bibundle
, and depict it by the following
W
-shaped diagram:

(202)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 9.16667pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.16667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathscr{G}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.72598pt\raise 0.6877pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\hbox{\kern-0.72598pt\raise-0.6877pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.14505pt\raise-28.6472pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise Appendix D
Conventions on Lie algebroids

Definition D.1
.

Let
M
be a smooth manifold. A (
real
)
Lie algebroid over

M
(
of rank

N\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\times}
) is a quintuple
(()\mathcal{E},M,{\mathbb{R}}^{N},\pi_{\mathcal{E}}),\rho_{\mathcal{E}},[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathcal{E}})
composed of

•

a vector bundle
(\mathcal{E},M,{\mathbb{R}}^{N},\pi_{\mathcal{E}})
;

•

a vector-bundle morphism

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 15.67638pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.756pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rho_{\mathcal{E}}\hskip 8.19447pt}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.84857pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.67638pt\raise-17.86525pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{\mathcal{E}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-28.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 36.84857pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm T}M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.85551pt\raise-17.86525pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{{\rm T}M}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.85551pt\raise-28.45274pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-8.39583pt\raise-35.73051pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.3548pt\raise-41.66383pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.92778pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm id}_{M}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 40.45969pt\raise-35.73051pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{aligned}\,,

termed the
anchor
(
map
);

•

a binary operation
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathcal{E}}\hskip-2.0pt:\Gamma(\mathcal{E})\times\Gamma(\mathcal{E})\to\Gamma(\mathcal{E})
,

satisfying the following conditions:

•

[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathcal{E}}
is a Lie bracket;

•

\forall_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{E})}\ \forall_{f\in C^{\infty}(M;{\mathbb{R}})}\hskip-2.0pt:[\varepsilon_{1},f\vartriangleright\varepsilon_{2}]_{\mathcal{E}}=f\vartriangleright[\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}]+\rho_{\mathcal{E}}(\varepsilon_{1})(f)\vartriangleright\varepsilon_{2}
(the
Leibniz rule
).

Example D.2
.

The
tangent Lie algebroid of

M
is the canonical structure of a Lie algebroid on the tangent bundle
\pi_{{\rm T}M}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm T}M\to M
with anchor
\rho_{{\rm T}M}={\rm id}_{{\rm T}M}
, and the standard Lie bracket
[\cdot,\cdot]_{{\rm T}M}
of vector fields on
M
.

Example D.3
.

Fix a Lie groupoid
\mathscr{G}
. Let

\displaystyle\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})_{\rm R}=\bigl\{\ \mathscr{V}\in\Gamma({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s)\quad|\quad\forall_{g\in\mathscr{G}}\ \forall_{h\in s^{-1}(\{t(g)\})}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm T}_{h}r_{g}\bigl(\mathscr{V}(h)\bigr)=\mathscr{V}(h.g)\ \bigr\}

be the set of
right-invariant vector fields on

\mathscr{G}
, defined in terms of the right-fibred action of Example
C.3
, and let

\displaystyle\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})_{\rm L}=\bigl\{\ \mathscr{V}\in\Gamma({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}t)\quad|\quad\forall_{g\in\mathscr{G}}\ \forall_{h\in t^{-1}(\{s(g)\})}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm T}_{h}l_{g}\bigl(\mathcal{V}(h)\bigr)=\mathcal{V}(g.h)\ \bigr\}

be the set of
left-invariant vector fields on

\mathscr{G}
, defined in terms of the left-fibred action of Example
C.3
. The
right tangent algebroid of

\mathscr{G}
is the vector bundle

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{1}\hskip-2.0pt:\mathfrak{gr}_{\rm R}:={\rm Id}^{*}{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s=M\,{}_{{\rm Id}}\hskip-3.0pt\times_{\pi_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}}\hskip-1.0pt{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s\longrightarrow M

with anchor

\displaystyle\rho_{\mathfrak{gr}_{\rm R}}={\rm T}t\circ{\rm pr}_{2}\,,

and Lie bracket

\displaystyle[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{gr}_{\rm R}}=\iota_{\rm R}^{-1}\circ[\cdot,\cdot]_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}\circ(\iota_{\rm R}\times\iota_{\rm R})

induced by the canonical (
{\mathbb{R}}
-linear) isomorphism (note that – by definition –
\pi_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}\circ\mathscr{S}={\rm Id}
)

\displaystyle\iota_{\rm R}\hskip-2.0pt:\Gamma(\mathfrak{gr}_{\rm R})\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})_{\rm R},\ \bigl({\rm id}_{M},\mathscr{S}\bigr)\longmapsto{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{t(\cdot)}}r_{\cdot}\bigl(\mathscr{S}\bigl(t(\cdot)\bigr)\bigr)\,,

with the obvious inverse

\displaystyle\iota_{\rm R}^{-1}={\rm ev}_{{\rm Id}}\hskip-2.0pt:\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})_{\rm R}\longrightarrow\Gamma(\mathfrak{gr}_{\rm R}),\ \mathscr{V}\longmapsto\bigl(\cdot,\mathscr{V}\bigl({\rm Id}(\cdot)\bigr)\bigr)\,.

The
left tangent algebroid of

\mathscr{G}
is defined analogously as the Lie algebroid

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathfrak{gr}_{\rm L}:={\rm Id}^{*}{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}t,M,{\mathbb{R}}^{{\rm rk}({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}t)},{\rm pr}_{1},{\rm T}s\circ{\rm pr}_{2},\iota_{\rm L}^{-1}\circ[\cdot,\cdot]_{{\rm T}\mathscr{G}}\circ(\iota_{\rm L}\times\iota_{\rm L})\bigr)\,,

where

\displaystyle\iota_{\rm L}\hskip-2.0pt:\Gamma(\mathfrak{gr}_{\rm L})\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ }\Gamma({\rm T}\mathscr{G})_{\rm L},\ \bigl({\rm id}_{M},\mathscr{S}\bigr)\longmapsto{\rm T}_{{\rm Id}_{s(\cdot)}}l_{\cdot}\bigl(\mathscr{S}\bigl(s(\cdot)\bigr)\bigr)

is the left-handed counterpart of
\iota_{\rm R}
.

Example D.4
.

The tangent Lie algebroid of the Lie groupoid
{\rm G}
of Example
B.3
is the tangent Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}\equiv{\rm Lie}({\rm G})
of the Lie group
{\rm G}
, with the Lie-algebra bracket as the Lie bracket.

Example D.5
.

The tangent Lie algebroid of the pair groupoid
{\rm Pair}(M)
of Example
B.4
is the tangent bundle
{\rm T}M
over the object manifold
M
, with the identity map as the anchor and the commutator of vector fields as the Lie bracket.

Example D.6
.

An important example of a Lie algebroid is the (right) tangent algebroid of the action groupoid from Example
B.5
. It goes by the name of the
action algebroid
and is denoted as
\mathfrak{g}\,{\hskip-1.13791pt\ltimes\hskip-1.42271pt}_{\lambda}M
(here,
\mathfrak{g}\equiv{\rm Lie}{\rm G}
is the (tangent) Lie algebra of the Lie group
{\rm G}
). One readily finds the convenient description

\displaystyle\Gamma\bigl({\rm T}({\rm G}\times M)\bigr)_{\rm R}=\bigl\{\ f^{A}\circ\lambda(\cdot)\vartriangleright R_{A}\bigl({\rm pr}_{1}(\cdot)\bigr)\quad|\quad f^{A}\in C^{\infty}(M;{\mathbb{R}})\,,\quad A\in\overline{1,{\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}\,{\rm T}_{e}{\rm G}}\ \bigr\}\,,

written out in terms of the basis right-invariant vector fields
R_{A}
on
{\rm G}
associated with an arbitrary basis
\tau=\{t_{A}\}_{A\in\overline{1,{\rm dim}_{\mathbb{R}}\,{\rm T}_{e}{\rm G}}}
of
{\rm T}_{e}{\rm G}
. Upon applying
\iota_{\rm R}^{-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Gamma\bigl({\rm T}({\rm G}\times M)\bigr)_{\rm R}\ni f^{A}\circ\lambda(\cdot)\vartriangleright R_{A}\bigl({\rm pr}_{1}(\cdot)\bigr)\longmapsto f^{A}(\cdot)\vartriangleright t_{A}\in\Gamma\bigl((e,\cdot)^{*}{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}{\rm pr}_{2}\bigr)\equiv\Gamma\bigl({\rm Id}^{*}{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s\bigr)\,,

which illustrates the bundle isomorphism

\displaystyle{\rm Id}^{*}{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s\cong M\times\mathfrak{g}\,.

On global sections, the anchor evaluates as

\displaystyle\Gamma\alpha_{\mathfrak{g}\,{\hskip-1.13791pt\ltimes\hskip-1.42271pt}_{\lambda}M}\hskip-2.0pt:\Gamma\bigl({\rm Id}^{*}{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s\bigr)\longrightarrow\Gamma({\rm T}M)\hskip-2.0pt:f^{A}(\cdot)\vartriangleright t_{A}\longmapsto-f^{A}(\cdot)\vartriangleright\mathscr{K}_{A}(\cdot)\,,

with, on the right-hand side, the fundamental vector fields of
\lambda
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{A}\equiv\mathscr{K}_{t_{A}}={\rm T}_{(e,\cdot)}\lambda\bigl(-t_{A},{\boldsymbol{0}}_{{\rm T}M}(\cdot)\bigr)\,.

Their bracket can be expressed in terms of the structure constants
f_{BC}^{\ \ \ A}
of
\mathfrak{g}
, associated with
\tau
through the structure equations

\displaystyle[t_{A},t_{B}]_{\mathfrak{g}}=f_{AB}^{\ \ \ C}\,t_{C}\,,

as

\displaystyle[f_{1}^{A}\vartriangleright t_{A},f_{2}^{B}\vartriangleright t_{B}]_{\mathfrak{g}\,{\hskip-1.13791pt\ltimes\hskip-1.42271pt}_{\lambda}M}=\bigl(f_{2}^{B}\,\mathcal{K}_{B}(f_{1}^{A})-f_{1}^{B}\,\mathcal{K}_{B}(f_{2}^{A})-f_{1}^{B}\,f_{2}^{C}\,f_{BC}^{\ \ \ A}\bigr)\vartriangleright t_{A}\,.

Example D.7
.

The tangent Lie algebroid of the symplectic groupoid of Example
B.6
is canonically isomorphic to the Lie algebroid
(({\rm T}^{*}M,M,{\mathbb{R}}^{{\rm dim}\,M},\pi_{{\rm T}^{*}M}),\Pi^{\#},\{\cdot,\cdot\})
, with the anchor given by the vector-bundle morphism
\Pi^{\#}\hskip-2.0pt:{\rm T}^{*}M\to{\rm T}M
canonically induced by the Poisson bivector
\Pi
, and the Lie bracket

\displaystyle\{\cdot,\cdot\}\hskip-2.0pt:\Gamma({\rm T}^{*}M)\times\Gamma({\rm T}^{*}M)\longrightarrow\Gamma({\rm T}^{*}M),\ (\omega_{1},\omega_{2})\longmapsto\imath_{\Pi^{\#}(\omega_{1})}{\mathsf{d}}\omega_{2}-\imath_{\Pi^{\#}(\omega_{2})}{\mathsf{d}}\omega_{1}+{\mathsf{d}}\bigl(\omega_{1}\wedge\omega_{2}(\Pi)\bigr)\,,

see Ref.
[
CDW87
, Chap. II, Prop. 2.1]
.

Lie algebroids provide us with canonical examples of foliated vector-valued differential 1-forms.

Example D.8

(
[
FS14
]
)
.

Fix a Lie groupoid
\mathscr{G}
with the tangent Lie algebroids
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm H},\ H\in\{L,R\}
. The
left-invariant Maurer–Cartan form on

\mathscr{G}
is the
{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}t
-foliated 1-form with values in
\mathfrak{gr}_{\rm L}
given in

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern L}(g)(v)=\bigl(s(g),{\rm T}_{g}l_{g^{-1}}(v)\bigr)\,,\quad v\in({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}t)_{g}\,.

Similarly, the
right-invariant Maurer–Cartan form on

\mathscr{G}
is the
{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s
-foliated 1-form with values in
\mathfrak{gr}_{\rm R}
given in

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern Thus,
\theta_{\rm L}(g)\in({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}t)^{*}_{g}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}t)_{{\rm Id}_{s(g)}}
and
\theta_{\rm R}(g)\in({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s)^{*}_{g}\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}({\rm ker}\,{\rm T}s)_{{\rm Id}_{t(g)}}
.

Appendix E
Useful properties of Lie groupoids, bisections, and Lie algebroids

Proposition E.1
.

The left- and right-multiplications of
\mathscr{G}
by
{\mathbb{B}}
from Def.
B.16
have the following properties relative to the structure maps of
\mathscr{G}
(written for arbitrary
(\beta,g)\in{\mathbb{B}}\times\mathscr{G}
):

(i)

s\circ L_{\beta}=s\,,\quad s\circ R_{\beta}=t_{*}(\beta^{-1})\circ s
(
s
intertwines
L
with the trivial action and
R
with
t_{*}\circ{\rm Inv}
);

(ii)

t\circ L_{\beta}=t_{*}\beta\circ t\,,\quad t\circ R_{\beta}=t
(
t
intertwines
L
with
t_{*}
and
R
with the trivial action);

(iii)

L_{\beta}\circ{\rm Id}=\beta\,,\quad R_{\beta}\circ{\rm Id}=\beta\circ t_{*}\bigl(\beta^{-1}\bigr)
;

(iv)

{\rm Inv}\circ L_{\beta}=R_{\beta^{-1}}\circ{\rm Inv}\,,\quad{\rm Inv}\circ R_{\beta}=L_{\beta^{-1}}\circ{\rm Inv}
(
{\rm Inv}
intertwines
L
with
R\circ{\rm Inv}
);

(v)

L_{\beta}\circ r_{g}=r_{g}\circ L_{\beta}\,,\quad R_{\beta}\circ l_{g}=l_{g}\circ R_{\beta}
;

(vi)

r_{g}\circ R_{\beta}=r_{L_{\beta}(g)}\,,\quad l_{g}\circ L_{\beta}=l_{R_{\beta}(g)}
.

Proof.

Ad (i)

The first identity is trivial. For the second one, we compute explicitly, for any
h\in\mathscr{G}
,

(204)

\displaystyle s\circ R_{\beta}(h)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle s\bigl(h.\bigl(\beta^{-1}\bigl(s(h)\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}\bigr)=t\bigl(\beta^{-1}\bigl(s(h)\bigr)\bigr)\equiv t\bigl(\bigl(\beta\circ(t_{*}\beta)^{-1}\bigl(s(h)\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}\bigr)=s\circ\beta\circ(t_{*}\beta)^{-1}\bigl(s(h)\bigr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(t_{*}\beta)^{-1}\circ s(h)=t_{*}\bigl(\beta^{-1}\bigr)\circ s(h)\,.

where the last equality follows from the homomorphicity of
t_{*}
.

Ad (ii)

The second identity is trivial. For the first one, we compute explicitly, for any
h\in\mathscr{G}
,

\displaystyle t\circ L_{\beta}(h)\equiv t\bigl(\beta\bigl(t(h)\bigr).h\bigr)=t\circ\beta\bigl(t(h)\bigr)\equiv t_{*}\beta\circ t(h)\,.

Ad (iii)

We find, for an arbitrary
m\in M
,

\displaystyle L_{\beta}\circ{\rm Id}(m)\equiv\beta\bigl(t({\rm Id}_{m})\bigr).{\rm Id}_{m}=\beta(m)

and

\displaystyle R_{\beta}\circ{\rm Id}(m)\equiv{\rm Id}_{m}.\bigl(\beta^{-1}\bigl(s({\rm Id}_{m})\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}=\bigl(\beta^{-1}(m)\bigr)^{-1}\equiv\beta\circ(t_{*}\beta)^{-1}(m)=\beta\circ t_{*}\bigl(\beta^{-1}\bigr)(m)\,.

Ad (iv)

For the first identity, we compute explicitly, for any
h\in\mathscr{G}
,

\displaystyle{\rm Inv}\circ L_{\beta}(h)\equiv\bigl(\beta\bigl(t(h)\bigr).h\bigr)^{-1}=h^{-1}.\beta\bigl(t(h)\bigr)^{-1}\equiv h^{-1}.\bigl(\beta^{-1}\bigr)^{-1}\bigl(s\bigl(h^{-1}\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}\equiv R_{\beta^{-1}}\circ{\rm Inv}(h)\,.

The second identity now follows by replacing
\beta
with
\beta^{-1}
in the one just proved, and subsequently sandwiching both sides of it between two copies of
{\rm Inv}
.

Ad (v)

Take an arbitrary arrow
h\in s^{-1}(\{t(g)\})
and calculate directly:

\displaystyle L_{\beta}\circ r_{g}(h)\equiv\beta\bigl(t(h.g)\bigr).(h.g)=\beta\bigl(t(h)\bigr).h.g\equiv r_{g}\bigl(\beta\bigl(t(h)\bigr).h\bigr)\equiv r_{g}\circ L_{\beta}(h)\,.

The proof of the second identity is fully analogous.

Ad (vi)

For the first identity, take an arbitrary arrow
h\in s^{-1}(t_{*}\beta(t(g)))
and calculate directly

\displaystyle r_{g}\circ R_{\beta}(h)\equiv\bigl(h.\bigl(\beta^{-1}\bigl(s(h)\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}\bigr).g=h.\bigl(\beta^{-1}\bigl(t_{*}\beta\bigl(t(g)\bigr)\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}.g\equiv h.\beta\bigl(t(g)\bigr).g\equiv h.\bigl(L_{\beta}(g)\bigr)\equiv r_{L_{\beta}(g)}(h)\,.

The second identity follows by replacing
\beta
with
\beta^{-1}
and
g
with
g^{-1}
in the one just proved, and subsequently using (iv).

∎

Remark E.2
.

Upon evaluation on the respective arguments
h\in\mathscr{G},\ m\in M,\ u\in s^{-1}(\{t(g)\}),\ v\in t^{-1}(\{s(g)\}),\ w\in s^{-1}(t_{*}\beta(t(g))),\ y\in t^{-1}((t_{*}\beta)^{-1}(s(g)))
and in the shorthand notation
L_{\beta}(h)\equiv\beta\vartriangleright h,\ R_{\beta}(h)\equiv h\vartriangleleft\beta
and
t_{*}\beta(m)\equiv\beta\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m
, the functional identities of Prop.
E.1
take the following form:

(205)

\displaystyle s(\beta\vartriangleright h)=s(h)\,,\qquad\qquad s(h\vartriangleleft\beta)=\beta^{-1}\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,(s(h)\bigr)\,,

(206)

\displaystyle t(\beta\vartriangleright h)=\beta\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,t(h)\,,\qquad\qquad t(h\vartriangleleft\beta)=t(h)\,,

(207)

\displaystyle\beta\vartriangleright{\rm Id}_{m}=\beta(m)\,,\qquad\qquad{\rm Id}_{m}\vartriangleleft\beta=\beta\bigl(\beta^{-1}\,\underline{\vartriangleright}\,m\bigr)\,,

(208)

\displaystyle(\beta\vartriangleright h)^{-1}=h^{-1}\vartriangleleft\beta^{-1}\,,\qquad\qquad(h\vartriangleleft\beta)^{-1}=\beta^{-1}\vartriangleright h^{-1}\,,

(209)

\displaystyle\beta\vartriangleright(u.g)=(\beta\vartriangleright u).g\,,\qquad\qquad(g.v)\vartriangleleft\beta=g.(v\vartriangleleft\beta)\,,

(210)

\displaystyle(w\vartriangleleft\beta).g=w.(\beta\vartriangleright g)\,,\qquad\qquad g.(\beta\vartriangleright y)=(g\vartriangleleft\beta).y\,.

Corollary E.3
.

The conjugation of
\mathscr{G}
by
{\mathbb{B}}
from Def.
B.16
has the following properties relative to the structure maps of
\mathscr{G}
(written for arbitrary
(\beta,g)\in{\mathbb{B}}\times\mathscr{G}
):

(i)

s\circ C_{\beta}=t_{*}\beta\circ s
(
s
intertwines
C
with
t_{*}
);

(ii)

t\circ C_{\beta}=t_{*}\beta\circ t
(
t
intertwines
C
with
t_{*}
);

(iii)

C_{\beta}\circ{\rm Id}={\rm Id}\circ t_{*}\beta
(
{\rm Id}
intertwines
C
with
t_{*}
);

(iv)

{\rm Inv}\circ C_{\beta}=C_{\beta}\circ{\rm Inv}
(
{\rm Inv}
intertwines
C
with itself);

(v)

C_{\beta}\circ{\rm m}={\rm m}\circ(C_{\beta}\times C_{\beta})
(
C
distributes over
{\rm m}
).

Proof of Corollary:

Ad (i)

Point (i) of Prop.
E.1
implies
s\circ C_{\beta}=s\circ R_{\beta^{-1}}=t_{*}\beta\circ s
.

Ad (ii)

Point (ii) of Prop.
E.1
implies
t\circ C_{\beta}=t\circ L_{\beta}=t_{*}\beta\circ t
.

Ad (iii)

Point (iii) of Prop.
E.1
implies
C_{\beta}\circ{\rm Id}\equiv R_{\beta^{-1}}\circ(L_{\beta}\circ{\rm Id})=R_{\beta^{-1}}\circ\beta=R_{\beta^{-1}}\circ(R_{\beta}\circ{\rm Id}\circ t_{*}\beta)=R_{\beta\cdot\beta^{-1}}\circ{\rm Id}\circ t_{*}\beta=R_{\rm Id}\circ{\rm Id}\circ t_{*}\beta={\rm Id}\circ t_{*}\beta
.

Ad (iv)

Point (iv) of Prop.
E.1
implies
{\rm Inv}\circ C_{\beta}\equiv({\rm Inv}\circ L_{\beta})\circ R_{\beta^{-1}}=R_{\beta^{-1}}\circ({\rm Inv}\circ R_{\beta^{-1}})=(R_{\beta^{-1}}\circ L_{\beta})\circ{\rm Inv}\equiv C_{\beta}\circ{\rm Inv}
.

Ad (v)

We calculate directly, in the notation of Remark
E.2
and using identities (
209
) and (
210
) along the way,

(212)

\displaystyle C_{\beta}\circ{\rm m}(g_{2},g_{1})\equiv\bigl(\beta\vartriangleright(g_{2}.g_{1})\bigr)\vartriangleleft\beta^{-1}=\bigl((\beta\vartriangleright g_{2}).g_{1}\bigr)\vartriangleleft\beta^{-1}=\bigl(\bigl((\beta\vartriangleright g_{2})\vartriangleleft\beta^{-1}\bigr).(\beta\vartriangleright g_{1})\bigr)\vartriangleleft\beta^{-1}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\bigl(\bigl((\beta\vartriangleright g_{2})\vartriangleleft\beta^{-1}\bigr).\bigl(\bigl((\beta\vartriangleright g_{1})\vartriangleleft\beta^{-1}\bigr)\equiv C_{\beta}(g_{2}).C_{\beta}(g_{1})\equiv{\rm m}\circ(C_{\beta}\times C_{\beta})(g_{2},g_{1})\,.

\blacksquare

Proposition E.4
.

For every
g\in\mathscr{G}
and every global bisection
\beta_{g}
such that

(213)

\displaystyle g=\beta_{g}\bigl(s(g)\bigr)\,,

the following identities hold true:

(i)

\beta_{g}((t_{*}\beta_{g})^{-1}(t(g)))=g
;

(ii)

the
functional
identity:

\displaystyle r_{g}=R_{\beta_{g}}\big|_{s^{-1}(t(g))}\,,

and so also, for every
h\in s^{-1}(t(g))
,

(214)

\displaystyle{\rm T}_{h}r_{g}={\rm T}_{h}R_{\beta_{g}}\big|_{{\rm ker}\,{\rm T}_{h}s}\,.

Proof.

For (i), evaluate the target map on both sides of the defining equality (
213
),

\displaystyle t(g)=(t_{*}\beta_{g})\bigl(s(g)\bigr)\,,

and subsequently invoke the invertibility of
t_{*}\beta_{g}\in{\rm Diff}(\mathscr{G})
to obtain the identity

\displaystyle s(g)=(t_{*}\beta_{g})^{-1}\bigl(t(g)\bigr)\,.

Upon substituting the latter back in (
213
), we arrive at the desired identity,

(215)

\displaystyle g=\beta_{g}\bigl(s(g)\bigr)=\beta_{g}\bigl((t_{*}\beta_{g})^{-1}\bigl(t(g)\bigr)\bigr)\,.

For (ii), invoke Eq. (
206
) to find the equality, for every
h\in s^{-1}(t(g))
,

\displaystyle r_{g}(h)\equiv h.g=h.\bigl({\rm Id}_{t(g)}\vartriangleleft\beta_{g}\bigr)=\bigl(h.{\rm Id}_{t(g)}\bigr)\vartriangleleft\beta_{g}=R_{\beta_{g}}(h)\,.

Its differentiation yields formula (
214
).
∎

Theorem E.5
.

[
Ry02
]

The tangent Lie algebra of the (Fréchet–)Lie group
{\mathbb{B}}
of bisections of a Lie groupoid
\mathscr{G}
is isomorphic to the Lie algebra
\Gamma_{\rm c}(\mathfrak{g}_{\rm R})
(resp.
\Gamma_{\rm c}(\mathfrak{g}_{\rm L})
) of smooth compactly supported sections of the tangent Lie algebroid
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm R}
(resp.
\mathfrak{g}_{\rm L}
) of
\mathscr{G}
.
